### [8] [Impact of Helicity-Isospin Convention Matching on the Extraction of $α$ from $B^0\to(ρπ)^0$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31828)
*Radek Žlebčík*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Corrects a helicity convention mismatch in ρ→ππ decays used in time-dependent Dalitz-plot analyses of B⁰→(ρπ)⁰, reducing tension in the direct vs. indirect determination of the CKM angle α from 2.9σ to 2.1σ; combined with B→ππ and B→ρρ, yields α = (87.0^{+11.8}_{-4.7})°.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the significant tension (2.9σ) between the direct measurement of the unitarity-triangle angle α from B⁰→(ρπ)⁰ decays and its indirect determination from global CKM fits, arising from an inconsistent ρ→ππ helicity convention between Dalitz-plot analysis and isospin decomposition.

Method: Identifies and corrects a mismatch between the ρ→ππ helicity amplitude convention employed in time-dependent Dalitz-plot analyses of B⁰→(ρπ)⁰ and that assumed in the isospin analysis; re-performs the isospin decomposition and statistical combination with B→ππ and B→ρρ channels using the consistent convention.

Result: Tension in α decreases from 2.9σ to 2.1σ; updated direct determination yields α = (87.0^{+11.8}_{-4.7})° when combined with B→ππ and B→ρρ.

Conclusion: The helicity convention mismatch was a key systematic bias; its correction improves consistency between direct and indirect determinations of α, though residual tension remains, indicating need for further experimental and theoretical scrutiny.

Abstract: We show that the $ρ\toππ$ helicity convention used in the time-dependent Dalitz-plot measurements of $B^0\to(ρπ)^0$ decays does not match the convention assumed in the subsequent isospin analysis. Once corrected, the tension between the direct determination of the unitarity-triangle angle $α$ from these decays and its indirect determination from global fits decreases from $2.9σ$ to $2.1σ$. Combined with the $B\toππ$ and $B\toρρ$ modes, the direct determination becomes $α= (87.0^{+11.8}_{-4.7})^\circ$, compared with the original world average $(84.1^{+3.7}_{-3.0})^\circ$.

</details>


### [9] [Neutrino Direct Simulation Monte Carlo: Accurate modeling of out-of-equilibrium decaying cosmological relics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31833)
*Kensuke Akita,Miguel Escudero,Oleksii Ihnatenko,Maksym Ovchynnikov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the Neutrino Direct Simulation Monte Carlo (νDSMC) method to jointly simulate decaying relics, neutrino spectra, electromagnetic plasma, cosmic expansion, and Big Bang nucleosynthesis—enabling accurate, efficient modeling of nonthermal neutrino evolution and its cosmological consequences.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reliably model neutrino thermalization in nonstandard cosmologies with MeV-scale energy injection from decaying relics, where standard approximations (e.g., momentum-averaged treatments) fail, and to enable robust parameter scans for new physics.

Method: Extended νDSMC framework incorporating joint evolution of relics, neutrinos (with flavor conversion, quantum statistics), electromagnetic plasma, and Hubble expansion; coupled to BBN; validated against quantum kinetic and quasi-classical Boltzmann solvers.

Result: νDSMC achieves excellent agreement with independent Boltzmann/quantum kinetic solutions while including more physics (e.g., pion production, inverse decays) and running significantly faster for highly nonthermal neutrino spectra; momentum-averaged approximations yield qualitatively wrong predictions (e.g., sign error in ΔN_eff, incorrect light-element abundances).

Conclusion: The extended νDSMC framework provides a fast, physically comprehensive, and numerically reliable tool for testing new physics via precision cosmology—particularly where neutrino nonthermalization strongly impacts N_eff and BBN yields.

Abstract: We consider nonstandard cosmologies in which decaying relics inject energy at MeV temperatures, driving neutrinos out of equilibrium. The subsequent neutrino evolution shapes predictions for the cosmic radiation density and primordial light element abundances. Earlier work introduced Neutrino Direct Simulation Monte Carlo ($ν$DSMC), which describes neutrino transport through Monte Carlo sampling of particle interactions. Here, we extend this method by jointly evolving the relic population, neutrino spectra, electromagnetic plasma, and cosmic expansion, and coupling this evolution to Big Bang nucleosynthesis. The framework includes direct and inverse decays of the relic, neutrino flavor conversion, quantum statistics, and pion production by energetic neutrinos. To reveal the physical impact of these effects and independently test $ν$DSMC, we compare several approaches to neutrino evolution across a range of nonstandard cosmological scenarios. We find excellent agreement with independent solutions of quantum kinetic or quasi-classical Boltzmann equations. Compared with these calculations, $ν$DSMC includes a broader range of physical processes and is substantially faster for strongly nonthermal neutrino populations. This makes broad, robust parameter scans for nonstandard cosmologies practical. We show that when departures from thermal equilibrium are large, approximations that average over neutrino momenta can give qualitatively incorrect predictions, including the wrong sign of the change in the effective number of neutrino species and substantially incorrect primordial nuclear abundances. We conclude that the extended $ν$DSMC framework enables reliable cosmological tests of new physics by following neutrino thermalization and its observable consequences.

</details>


### [10] [Disentangling $V_{ud}$ from New Physics in Beta Decays with the FCC-ee](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31834)
*Lemonia Gialidi,Elie Hammou,Kamil Laurent,Vaisakh Plakkot,Juan Rojo,Jordy de Vries*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper combines superallowed β-decay data with collider (LEP, LHC, HL-LHC, FCC-ee) data within the SMEFiT framework to simultaneously extract V_ud and SMEFT Wilson coefficients, showing that only FCC-ee can fully decouple V_ud from New Physics and achieve SM-level precision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Superallowed β-decays provide the most precise determination of V_ud but are degenerate with left-handed New Physics contributions; low-energy data alone cannot resolve this degeneracy.

Method: Joint SMEFT global fit using SMEFiT, incorporating a dedicated likelihood for superallowed decays (with radiative and nuclear-structure uncertainties) plus LEP, LHC, HL-LHC projections, and FCC-ee projections.

Result: Current and HL-LHC data only partially break the V_ud–New Physics degeneracy; FCC-ee fully decouples them, recovering SM-level precision on V_ud and significantly tightening constraints on relevant SMEFT Wilson coefficients (e.g., C_φl^(3)). Robust under flavour assumptions and theory uncertainties.

Conclusion: FCC-ee is essential for a model-independent, SMEFT-consistent extraction of V_ud and for resolving the Cabibbo angle anomaly; it demonstrates critical complementarity between high-precision low-energy and future high-energy collider experiments.

Abstract: Superallowed nuclear $β$-decays enable the most precise determination of the CKM element $V_{ud}$, but they are only sensitive to an effective combination of $V_{ud}$ and New Physics contributions to left-handed charged currents, which low-energy data alone cannot disentangle. Here we determine $V_{ud}$ simultaneously with the relevant Wilson coefficients of the Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT) by combining, within the SMEFiT framework, a dedicated likelihood for superallowed transitions, including radiative and nuclear-structure uncertainties, with LEP and LHC world data and HL-LHC and FCC-ee projections. Current collider data and HL-LHC projections only partially lift the degeneracy between $V_{ud}$ and possible New Physics effects. Instead, the FCC-ee fully decouples $V_{ud}$ from New Physics, recovering SM-level precision while significantly tightening the constraints on the SMEFT Wilson coefficients. This conclusion is robust against variations in flavour assumptions and theory uncertainties. Our results highlight the complementarity between future colliders and low-energy experiments, and pave the way towards a SMEFT-consistent interpretation of first-row CKM unitarity tests and the Cabibbo angle anomaly.

</details>


### [11] [Milky Way White Dwarfs as Inelastic Dark Matter Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31850)
*Javier F. Acevedo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes white dwarfs in the Galactic Center as probes for inelastic dark matter (IDM) annihilation, where IDM particles on bound but extended orbits annihilate partially outside the stars, yielding a detectable gamma-ray signal; constraints on IDM scattering cross-sections are derived using H.E.S.S. data and applied to dark photon and higgsino models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit gravitational capture of inelastic dark matter by white dwarfs in the Galactic Center as a novel astrophysical probe—especially for IDM that does not fully thermalize inside the star due to kinematic thresholds, enabling external annihilation and observable gamma-ray emission.

Method: Analytical modeling of IDM capture, orbital dynamics, and partial external annihilation around white dwarfs; computation of collective gamma-ray flux from the Galactic Center region; comparison with H.E.S.S. gamma-ray observations within 1°; derivation of conservative upper limits on the IDM-nucleon scattering cross-section; mapping onto specific particle physics models (dark photon-mediated and higgsino with MeV-scale mass splittings).

Result: Derivation of tentative upper limits on the IDM scattering cross-section at TeV masses, constrained by non-detection of excess gamma rays in H.E.S.S. data; concrete exclusion bounds for dark photon and higgsino IDM models with ~MeV mass splittings.

Conclusion: White dwarfs in the Galactic Center provide a viable and complementary channel to constrain inelastic dark matter, particularly for parameter space where traditional direct detection or stellar capture/annihilation signatures are suppressed; the method yields competitive bounds under conservative assumptions.

Abstract: We show that white dwarfs in the Galactic Center are a sensitive probe of inelastic dark matter (IDM) annihilation. IDM particles traversing a white dwarf may become gravitationally captured without fully settling in its interior if their energy drops below the kinematic threshold for inelastic scattering. In this regime, a fraction of the incoming particles remains bound on long-lived orbits that extend beyond the white dwarf's volume, enabling annihilation to partially proceed outside of the object. Some of the annihilation products subsequently escape, rather than being absorbed by the stellar material, producing a potentially detectable signal. We estimate the collective gamma-ray flux from the external annihilation sourced by these systems, as a function of the IDM parameters, and compare it to H.E.S.S. observations within the central degree of the galaxy. From this comparison, we derive tentative limits on the IDM scattering cross-section at the TeV mass scale, under largely conservative assumptions for the IDM density in this region. As a concrete application, we map these results into specific parameter constraints for dark photon-mediated and higgsino models with mass splittings at the MeV scale.

</details>


### [12] [Landscape of pentaquark bound states with neural-network Variational Monte Carlo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32624)
*Jing-Zhe Song,Wei-Lin Wu,Shi-Lin Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses neural network variational Monte Carlo to systematically study ground states of manifestly exotic negative-parity pentaquarks in a constituent quark model; it finds 21 shallow molecular bound states (no deeply bound ones), all composed of heavy meson–baryon or kaon–heavy baryon pairs, validates thresholds via scattering-state convergence, reproduces experimentally observed η_c N and η_c Λ scattering states, and confirms no bound fully heavy pentaquarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically identify manifestly exotic pentaquark bound states—those with quantum numbers forbidding mixing with conventional baryons—and clarify whether deeply bound or stable pentaquarks exist, especially in light of experimental observations of hidden-charm resonances.

Method: Neural network variational Monte Carlo (NN-VMC) within the constituent quark potential model; exhaustive enumeration of 387 color–spin–isospin configurations for all negative-parity manifestly exotic pentaquark systems; binding energy computed relative to relevant baryon–meson thresholds; cross-validation via convergence of unbound system energies to thresholds from above (S-wave scattering states).

Result: 21 shallowly bound pentaquark candidates (binding energies 2–15 MeV), all molecular (no compact deeply bound states); specific configurations form heavy vector meson + baryon (e.g., QQqqQ̄, QsqqQ̄) or K + heavy baryon (e.g., QQqq s̄, Qqqq s̄); unbound systems converge to baryon–meson thresholds, validating methodology; lowest scattering states η_c N and η_c Λ reproduced; all fully heavy pentaquarks (e.g., ccccb̄) are unbound.

Conclusion: No compact, deeply bound pentaquarks exist in this model; all bound states are shallow hadronic molecules; results strongly constrain theoretical expectations and guide experimental searches—particularly at LHCb and Belle II—for exotic pentaquarks, especially near known meson–baryon thresholds.

Abstract: We present a systematic study of the ground states of pentaquark systems using the neural network variational Monte Carlo method within the constituent quark potential model. We investigate all manifestly exotic pentaquark systems with negative parity, which cannot mix with the conventional baryons through the creation and annihilation of light or strange quark pair. We exhaust 387 color, spin, and isospin configurations and identify 21 pentaquark bound state candidates with binding energies ranging from $2$ to $15$ MeV. A striking observation is that all of the bound states are shallowly bound molecular states, and a deeply bound pentaquark state does not exist. The $QQqq\bar{Q}$, $ssqq\bar{Q}$, $Qsqq\bar{Q}$ and $Qqqq\bar{Q}$ bound states are composed of a heavy vector meson and a baryon, while the $QQqq\bar{s}$ and $Qqqq\bar{s}$ bound states are composed of a $K$ meson and a heavy baryon. As a cross-validation, the ground-state energies of the unbound systems converge to the baryon--meson thresholds from above, which are consistent with the S-wave scattering states and provide a reliable baseline for the bound-state criterion. Especially, we reproduce the lowest scattering states $η_c N$ and $η_c Λ$ in the $qqqc\bar{c}$ and $qqsc\bar{c}$ channels respectively, where the hidden-charm pentaquark resonances were observed experimentally. We also find that all fully heavy pentaquark states are unbound. Our results provide valuable guidance for future pentaquark searches at LHCb, Belle II, and other experiments.

</details>


### [13] [Prompting Particle Physics: Tokenized Multi-modal Foundation Models for Combinatorially Many Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31862)
*Nilotpal Kakati,Daniel Murnane,Baran Hashemi,Samuel Klein,Jeffrey Krupa,Eilam Gross,Lukas Heinrich,Michael Kagan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a unified token-based model for collider experiment reconstruction and simulation, enabling multiple tasks (e.g., particle flow, detector simulation) via modality masking—using a shared vocabulary of jet objects (tracks, clusters, particles, jets) and training one model to map any subset of input modalities to any output modalities; both autoregressive and parallel decoders achieve stable training and high fidelity, with the autoregressive variant surpassing a state-of-the-art particle flow method on key jet metrics and matching Geant4 simulation realism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To replace fragmented, task-specific algorithm chains in collider experiments with a single, interpretable, multi-task model that preserves standard intermediate physics objects (e.g., tracks, clusters, jets) while enabling flexible cross-modal inference.

Method: Represents all jet-related objects (tracks, calorimeter cells, clusters, particles, jets) as tokens in a shared vocabulary; trains a single transformer-based model to map any subset of input modalities to any subset of output modalities; uses both autoregressive and parallel decoding; trains jointly over all modality combinations with tokenisation for numerical stability.

Result: Both models generate realistic, physically plausible reconstruction and simulation outputs; the autoregressive model achieves superior fidelity to Geant4-simulated energy deposits and outperforms a state-of-the-art particle flow algorithm on standard jet-level metrics (e.g., jet mass resolution, energy response).

Conclusion: A unified token-based architecture enables robust, interpretable, and multi-task learning across collider reconstruction and simulation pipelines without sacrificing physical object semantics or performance—demonstrating feasibility of replacing modular pipelines with a single, flexible foundation model.

Abstract: Reconstruction and simulation at a collider experiment are long chains of specialised algorithms, each tuned to a single step. We explore how one model can serve many of those steps at once, while still producing the intermediate objects (tracks, calorimeter cells, clusters, particles and jets) that make the chain interpretable. To do so, we represent every object in a jet in a single shared token vocabulary and train one model to map any subset of these modalities to any other. A task is then only a choice of which modalities to provide and which to request: particle flow, detector simulation and charged energy subtraction are all directions through the same set of weights. We train over all modality combinations, with a decoder emitting tokens either autoregressively or in parallel. With tokenisation, both architectures train stably with little tuning. In evaluations, both models produce realistic reconstruction and simulation objects, with the autoregressive model particularly faithful to output from Geant4. The autoregressive model also outperforms a state-of-the-art particle flow algorithm on many typical jet reconstruction metrics.

</details>


### [14] [The era of charmed resonance searches at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32054)
*Daniel Whiteson*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the feasibility and sensitivity of charm-tagged dijet resonance searches at the LHC, finding that even with current (loose) charm-tagging performance, such searches can outperform inclusive dijet searches for resonances decaying to cc̄, contingent on improved modeling of high-pT charm efficiency and heavy-flavor background composition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: No dedicated dijet resonance search using charm-tagged jets has been performed; the study aims to determine the charm-tagging performance needed to make such searches competitive with inclusive or b-tagged dijet searches.

Method: Uses ATLAS 13 TeV dijet mass spectrum (139 fb⁻¹), simulated jet-flavor fractions, and parameterized charm/bottom/light-jet tagging efficiencies to predict charm-tagged spectra; performs profile-likelihood sensitivity estimation in exclusive one- and two-charm-tag categories.

Result: At the loosest working point of the current charm tagger, charm-tagged searches yield improved expected sensitivity to cc̄ resonances across the full mass range studied—though quantitative gains depend critically on uncertain high-pT charm-tagging efficiency and simulated heavy-flavor background composition.

Conclusion: Charm-tagged dijet searches are viable and potentially superior to inclusive searches for cc̄ resonances; results set concrete performance targets for future charm taggers and justify initiating dedicated charm-tagged resonance searches at the LHC.

Abstract: The LHC dijet program has searched extensively for resonances decaying to inclusive jets and, more recently, to bottom-quark jets, where the sensitivity enhancement from suppression of the background outweighs the loss of signal efficiency in bottom-quark tagging. However, no dedicated search for dijet resonances containing charm-tagged jets has been performed. We study the charm-tagging performance required to make such searches competitive with the inclusive dijet search. Using the ATLAS 13 TeV dijet mass spectrum with 139 fb$^{-1}$, we predict the expected charm-tagged spectra from simulated jet-flavor fractions and parameterized charm-, bottom-, and light-jet tagging efficiencies, and estimate the sensitivity of a profile-likelihood search in exclusive one- and two-tag categories. At the loosest working point of the current tagger, charm-tagged searches improve the expected sensitivity to a resonance decaying to two charm quarks across the full resonance mass range considered, although the quantitative sensitivity depends on the poorly constrained high-$\pt$ behavior of charm-tagging efficiencies and on the simulated heavy-flavor composition of the dijet background. These results provide performance targets for charm taggers and motivate the development of dedicated charm-tagged dijet resonance searches at the LHC.

</details>


### [15] [Beam-polarization-enabled witness of four-dimensional entanglement in $Ω^-\barΩ^+$ pairs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32419)
*Qiu-Yan Zhang,Peng-Cheng Hong,Wei-Min Song*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates four-dimensional spin entanglement in Ω⁻Ω̄⁺ pairs produced in e⁺e⁻ → ψ(3686), using beam polarization to control helicity interference and the negativity criterion (N > 1) to witness entanglement; it identifies critical polarizations (~0.65 transverse, ~0.57 longitudinal), finds near-maximal negativity (N_peak ≈ 1.50) under full transverse polarization, and shows angular witness extent is more phase-sensitive than peak negativity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether Ω⁻Ω̄⁺ spin entanglement requires the full 4×4 (d=4) Hilbert space—i.e., genuine high-spin (s=3/2) entanglement beyond effective qubit descriptions—and to explore how beam polarization manipulates helicity coherence to probe dimensionality.

Method: Analyzes helicity amplitudes from e⁺e⁻ → ψ(3686) → Ω⁻Ω̄⁺ using BESIII-measured helicity parameters; applies negativity N as an entanglement witness in the full 4D spin space; computes N as a function of beam polarization (transverse/longitudinal), accounts for production-rate-weighted angular acceptance, and compares with constituent-quark model benchmarks to assess phase sensitivity.

Result: Critical beam polarizations for entanglement witnessing are found: P_T ≈ 0.65 (equal transverse) and P_L ≈ 0.57 (equal-and-opposite longitudinal); full transverse polarization yields N_peak ≈ 1.50 and 60% angular fraction satisfying N > 1; destructive interference at optimal angles suppresses unequal-helicity components, yielding near-equal Schmidt coefficients; angular witness extent is more sensitive to relative phases than N_peak.

Conclusion: Four-dimensional spin entanglement is experimentally accessible in high-spin baryon pairs via polarization-controlled helicity interference; the negativity witness confirms genuine d=4 entanglement, and beam polarization serves as a tunable tool to probe high-dimensional quantum correlations in heavy-flavor hadron production.

Abstract: We study the entanglement structure of $Ω^-\barΩ^+$ pairs produced in $e^+e^-\toψ(3686)$, focusing on whether the entanglement requires the full four-dimensional spin space of each spin-$3/2$ baryon. Beam polarization controls the parent-state populations and coherence, thereby modifying helicity interference. Using the negativity criterion $\Neg>1$ and the measured BESIII helicity parameters, we find critical polarizations of about $0.65$ per beam for equal transverse polarization and $0.57$ per beam for equal-and-opposite longitudinal polarization. Full transverse polarization gives a near-maximal peak negativity, $\Neg_{\rm peak}\simeq1.50$, with a production-rate-weighted angular fraction of $0.60$ satisfying the witness. At the optimal production directions, destructive interference removes the unequal-helicity channels, while the near equality of the relevant equal-helicity amplitudes yields nearly equal Schmidt coefficients. Constituent-quark benchmarks further show that the angular extent of the witness is more sensitive to relative phases than the peak negativity. These results link polarization-controlled helicity interference to four-dimensional entanglement in high-spin baryon production.

</details>


### [16] [Escaping Solar Capture: Majoron mediated Elastic Dark Matter for the LZ 248~keV Event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32435)
*Salvador Centelles Chuliá,Ayan Chattaraj,Rahul Srivastava,Surabhi Srivastava*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper interprets the 248 keVnr nuclear-recoil candidate from LZ as elastic scattering of dark matter mediated by a light Majoron — a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson from spontaneous lepton number violation — and constructs a scotogenic UV model where the lightest singlet Majorana fermion is the DM, with couplings tied to neutrino mass generation; the resulting nonrelativistic operator O₆ explains the high-energy recoil without overproducing low-energy events, avoids dominant solar capture channels, and matches both relic abundance and LZ data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous 248 keVnr nuclear-recoil event observed by the LZ experiment via a theoretically motivated, minimal dark matter scenario that links the signal to neutrino mass generation and evades stringent astrophysical constraints (e.g., solar capture).

Method: Model-independent effective operator analysis followed by construction of a scotogenic ultraviolet-complete theory featuring a light Majoron, a singlet Majorana fermion dark matter candidate, vector-like up-type quark, and type-I Dirac seesaw in the quark sector; calculation of low-energy nuclear scattering via the nonrelativistic operator O₆, and evaluation of solar capture rates and thermal relic abundance.

Result: The model successfully reproduces the LZ 248 keVnr event via O₆-mediated scattering, suppresses low-energy recoil excess, evades solar capture bounds (especially on heavy elements and hydrogen), and yields correct thermal relic density without fine-tuning.

Conclusion: A light Majoron-mediated scattering scenario within a scotogenic UV framework provides a consistent, predictive, and testable explanation for the LZ anomaly while embedding dark matter naturally in neutrino mass generation.

Abstract: We interpret the $248~\mathrm{keV_{nr}}$ nuclear-recoil candidate reported by LZ through elastic dark matter scattering mediated by a light Majoron. The Majoron provides a natural origin for the required pseudoscalar interaction: it is the pseudo Nambu-Goldstone boson associated with spontaneous lepton number breaking, directly connecting the recoil signal to neutrino mass generation. After a model independent analysis of this idea, we present a concrete realization in a scotogenic ultraviolet completion in which the lightest singlet Majorana fermion is dark matter and acquires both its mass and Majoron coupling from the symmetry breaking dynamics. A vector like up type quark and a type-I Dirac seesaw in quark sector communicate the Majoron interaction to the up quark. At low energies, the resulting portal generates the nonrelativistic operator $\mathcal O_6$, whose momentum dependence reproduces the high-recoil event without overpopulating the lower energy bins. Its spin structure removes the dominant heavy element solar capture channels, while capture on hydrogen is suppressed by poor kinematic matching and small momentum transfer. This construction simultaneously reproduces the LZ recoil spectrum and the observed thermal relic abundance while strongly suppressing solar capture.

</details>


### [17] [Heavy Inert Doublet Reconciles LZ and IceCube](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28819)
*Hitoshi Murayama,Bea Noether*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes inert doublet dark matter (IDM) with mass 11.3–20.3 TeV to simultaneously explain the LZ direct detection signal and satisfy IceCube neutrino constraints, leveraging velocity-dependent scattering and suppressed solar capture; viable parameter space exists below the Landau pole, with electroweak precision tests still satisfied.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the tentative LZ direct detection excess with stringent IceCube neutrino limits—typically in tension for standard WIMP scenarios—by exploring non-standard dark matter mass and coupling regimes within the inert doublet model.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of IDM parameter space: calculation of direct detection rates (LZ), solar capture and neutrino yield (IceCube), thermal freeze-out annihilation cross section, Landau pole constraints, and electroweak precision observables (S, T parameters).

Result: A narrow IDM mass window (11.3–20.3 TeV) with enhanced Higgs-inert doublet coupling (~0.8–1.2) satisfies LZ signal rate, evades IceCube bounds via suppressed solar capture (due to high infall velocity), achieves correct relic density, remains perturbative below ~10^6 GeV, and complies with S/T-parameter limits.

Conclusion: Heavy inert doublet dark matter provides a consistent, testable framework reconciling current anomalies without violating established constraints, highlighting the role of kinematic suppression and UV-sensitive couplings in multi-experiment consistency.

Abstract: We show that the inert doublet dark matter can reconcile the purported LZ direct detection signal and IceCube constraints. The preferred dark matter mass is 11.3--20.3~TeV, an order of magnitude heavier than the standard higgsino target. The smaller number density at a high mass can reproduce the LZ event given the exponential sensitivity to the velocity, while suppressing the capture rate in the Sun because of the higher infall velocity. On the other hand, the high mass requires a large coupling between the inert doublet and Higgs boson to have the correct annihilation cross section for the freeze-out. There is about an order of magnitude of validity range below the Landau pole, beyond which a UV completion is needed. Electroweak precision observables are below the experimental limits despite the large coupling.

</details>


### [18] [Wide-Mass-Scanning-Range Setup and Phase-Resolved Protocol for Axion Dark Matter Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32554)
*A. Cottet,T. Kontos*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a quantum-enhanced interferometric method using non-linear cavities and gyromagnetic modes to detect axion dark matter, improving sensitivity by ≥4 orders of magnitude and mass coverage by ≥2 orders over standard haloscopes—applicable also to dark photons and galactic masers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome sensitivity and mass-range limitations of conventional haloscope-based axion searches by leveraging quantum-limited interferometry and broad-band transduction.

Method: Quantum-limited interferometric measurement of axion field amplitude and phase via a non-linear cavity; integration of gyromagnetic modes as wide-mass-range transducers compatible with standard haloscope geometries.

Result: Projected improvement of ≥10⁴ in figure of merit and ≥10² in accessible axion mass window relative to state-of-the-art haloscopes; protocol is generalizable to dark photon and galactic maser detection.

Conclusion: The proposed paradigm enables significantly faster, broader, and more sensitive searches for ultralight bosonic dark matter and related phenomena, marking a shift from power-based to quantum-coherent field sensing.

Abstract: We propose a paradigm for quantum enhanced axion dark matter search, which does not rely on power measurements. We propose to measure directly the axion amplitude and phase in an interferometric protocol at the quantum limit, using a non-linear cavity. In addition, we introduce gyromagnetic modes as wide mass range transducers for axion signals compatible with standard haloscope designs. We expect this scheme to offer an improvement of at least 4 orders of magnitude in figure of merit and at least 2 orders of magnitude in mass window with respect to standard haloscopes. Owing to its generality, our proposed protocol has the potential to speed up axion search but also the search for dark photons or other cosmological objects, such as galactic masers.

</details>


### [19] [Dissecting the moat regime at low energies II: Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32563)
*Fabian Rennecke,Shi Yin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how the 'moat regime'—a feature in low-energy QCD with two-flavor quark-meson coupling—affects meson and quark correlation functions and the quark-antiquark potential, revealing distinct oscillatory behavior (unrelated to Friedel oscillations), enhanced spacelike meson spectral functions (including vector mesons and eta), quark insensitivity, and potential shifts in the chiral phase boundary and critical endpoint.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the physical implications of the moat regime—a non-perturbative infrared feature in the quark-meson model—for hadronic correlations, confinement signatures (e.g., quark-antiquark potential), and thermodynamic phase structure at high density and zero temperature.

Method: Uses the two-flavor quark-meson model within random-phase approximation (RPA) to compute meson and quark propagators, spectral functions, and the static quark-antiquark potential; analyzes analytic structure (poles on unphysical Riemann sheets) to distinguish moat-induced oscillations from Friedel-type ones.

Result: Moat-induced oscillations in the quark-antiquark potential arise from unphysical-sheet poles—not Friedel oscillations; pions, sigma, vector mesons, and eta all exhibit enhanced spacelike spectral strength; quark correlators remain unaffected; chiral phase boundary and critical endpoint location may shift even without inhomogeneous instabilities.

Conclusion: The moat regime is a genuine non-perturbative phenomenon influencing mesonic dynamics and phase structure significantly, while leaving quark correlations intact—highlighting its role as a meson-driven infrared anomaly with consequences for QCD phase diagram predictions.

Abstract: We study the effects of the moat regime on meson and quark correlation functions and the quark-antiquark potential in low-energy QCD, building on the two-flavor quark meson model in a random-phase approximation. We investigate the relation between the moat regime and Friedel oscillations in the quark-antiquark potential at large density and zero temperature. As it turns out, oscillations from the moat regime are a distinct phenomenon, arising from poles on unphysical Riemann sheets in this region. Furthermore, we find that in addition to pions and the sigma meson, also other mesons, including vector mesons and the eta, are sensitive to the moat regime. This leads in particular to an enhancement of the meson spectral function in the spacelike region. The quarks, on the other hand, appear to be essentially insensitive. This can have far-reaching consequences for the phase diagram, as for example the chiral phase boundary, including the location of the critical endpoint, can be affected in the moat regime even in absence of inhomogeneous instabilities.

</details>


### [20] [Quantum tomography at the Electron-Ion Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32800)
*Michael Fucilla,Yuxun Guo,Yoshitaka Hatta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes quantum state tomography of quark-antiquark pairs at the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) using azimuthal angular correlations from Collins and dihadron fragmentation, enabling measurement of full spin density matrices and probing entanglement and Bell-nonlocality—especially maximal entanglement in heavy-quark systems manifesting as deviations from the standard cos(φ+φ′) Collins modulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable experimental access to quantum information properties (e.g., entanglement, Bell-nonlocality) of QCD-produced quark-antiquark pairs via complete spin density matrix reconstruction at the EIC—a capability not previously realized for massless or massive qq̄ states.

Method: Theoretical derivation showing that all elements of the spin density matrix for qq̄ pairs can be extracted from azimuthal angular correlations between hadron and dihadron final states, mediated by the Collins and dihadron fragmentation functions; analysis includes both massless and massive (heavy-quark) cases.

Result: All spin density matrix components are experimentally accessible; maximal entanglement in the heavy-quark sector induces a pronounced deviation from the canonical cos(φ+φ′) Collins angular dependence—serving as a measurable signature.

Conclusion: Quantum state tomography of qq̄ pairs is feasible at the EIC using existing fragmentation-based observables, opening a new frontier for testing quantum information concepts in high-energy QCD.

Abstract: We initiate the study of quantum state tomography---the experimental reconstruction of the complete spin density matrix---of massless and massive quark-antiquark pairs produced at the future Electron-Ion Collider (EIC). We show that all components of the spin density matrix can be accessed through azimuthal angular correlations between hadron and dihadron pairs generated by the Collins and dihadron fragmentation functions. This enables the experimental investigation of a variety of quantum information properties at the EIC, such as entanglement and Bell-nonlocality. We predict that the recently discovered maximal entanglement in the heavy-quark sector is signaled by a strong modification of the usual Collins angular dependence $\cos(φ+φ')$

</details>


### [21] [Machine learning for the LHC physics program: a 2025-2026 stocktake](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32874)
*Jesse Thaler*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews the state of machine learning in LHC physics (HEPML) from May 2025–May 2026 using an AI-agentic living review pipeline; it concludes that ML has transitioned from a tool-building research area to essential infrastructure for LHC physics, and identifies six key claims about trends in simulation-based inference, AI agents, trustworthiness, equivariance adoption, and theory-ML advances.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically assess the evolution and impact of machine learning in high-energy physics—particularly for the LHC program—over a defined 12-month period, using scalable, reproducible, AI-assisted literature synthesis rather than traditional manual review.

Method: An agentic AI pipeline processed 569 post-May-2025 HEPML papers from the HEPML Living Review: abstracts were ranked by citations, recency, theme, and collaboration involvement; 103 papers (95 recent + 8 baseline) were read in full and annotated with structured notes; six field-level claims were synthesized from notes and validated via independent reviewer agents and source re-verification.

Result: Six empirically grounded claims: (1) HEPML is now production infrastructure (e.g., ATLAS/CMS rely on NNs for published results; ALEPH data revived); (2) simulation-based inference and foundation models are converging; (3) AI agents represent a novel frontier (47 papers, no adopted measurements yet); (4) trust-related topics (uncertainty, calibration, interpretability) dominate ~20% of recent work; (5) slowing trends (e.g., equivariance research) reflect maturation into tools; (6) theory-ML crossed capability thresholds across multiple domains.

Conclusion: ML is no longer auxiliary but foundational to LHC physics. The shift demands new norms for validation, reproducibility, and human-AI collaboration—and raises concerns about opacity, accountability, and methodological drift in AI-assisted science.

Abstract: The first sentence of this abstract--and the introduction to these proceedings--was authored by a human, but the bulk of this document was generated by an agentic AI system. In this talk, I take stock of machine learning (ML) for the LHC physics program over the twelve months from May 2025 to May 2026. The corpus is the HEPML Living Review, split at May 2025 into 1,756 earlier papers and 569 later ones. An AI pipeline surveyed the 569 abstracts, ranked them by citations, recency, theme, and collaboration involvement, and read 103 papers in full (95 from after the split, plus 8 earlier baseline papers), producing a structured note for each. The notes were then synthesized into six claims about the state of the field, checked by independent reviewer agents, and re-verified against the source papers. The headline claim is that (1) ML for high-energy physics (HEPML) stopped being a research area that builds tools and became infrastructure that the LHC physics program depends on: ATLAS and CMS now publish physics results that depend on neural networks, and the archived ALEPH data have re-entered production. The other five claims are: (2) simulation-based inference and foundation models are two revolutions starting to merge; (3) AI agents are the genuinely new front, with 47 papers in twelve months and no adopted measurement yet; (4) "do we trust it?" is the fastest-growing agenda, with one recent paper in five about uncertainty, calibration, or interpretability; (5) what is slowing down is informative, since equivariance was absorbed into a tool and model-specific phenomenology ceded ground to model-agnostic searches; and (6) theory ML crossed a capability threshold in multiple research areas. I close with what is settled, what is incoming, and what is open, and briefly discuss the concerns raised by this way of working with AI.

</details>


### [22] [$ \bar K^0 d $ Bound State and Correlation Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32986)
*M. Bayar,P. Encarnación,A. Feijoo,E. Oset*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the $\bar{K}^0d$ system using a unitarity-preserving fixed-center approximation, predicting a bound state interpretable as a $Λ(1380)N$ configuration—complementary to the $K^-pp$ state linked to the higher $Λ(1405)$ pole—and computes its correlation function as a Coulomb-free observable for ALICE tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the nature of the $Λ(1405)$ resonance and its possible manifestations in few-body systems, particularly whether a $\bar{K}^0d$ bound state exists and how it relates to the two-pole structure of $Λ(1405)$, using a rigorously unitary approach.

Method: A new formulation of the fixed-center approximation (FCA) that preserves elastic unitarity is applied to solve the $\bar{K}^0d$ three-body problem; scattering amplitude and correlation function are computed; two different $\bar{K}N$ interaction models are used to test robustness.

Result: A bound state below threshold is predicted in the $\bar{K}^0d$ system; interpreted as a $Λ(1380)N$ configuration; scattering parameters agree with recent $K^-d$ data; correlation function shows distinct bound-state signature; predictions are consistent across two $\bar{K}N$ models.

Conclusion: The $\bar{K}^0d$ system supports a bound state tied to the lower $Λ(1405)$ pole, offering a complementary probe to $K^-pp$; the predicted correlation function provides a clean, Coulomb-free experimental signature for validation in heavy-ion collisions at ALICE.

Abstract: We study the $\bar{K}^0d$ system using a new formulation of the fixed-center approximation that preserves elastic unitarity. The resulting scattering amplitude predicts a bound state below threshold, which can be interpreted as a $Λ(1380)N$ configuration complementary to the $K^-pp$ state associated with the higher $Λ(1405)$ pole. The interaction is attractive, with scattering parameters of the same order as recent $K^-d$ determinations. We further calculate the $\bar{K}^0d$ correlation function, which exhibits a characteristic structure associated with the bound state and provides a Coulomb-free observable that can be tested in future ALICE measurements. Results obtained with two different $\bar{K}N$ models are very similar, indicating the robustness of the predictions against the choice of the underlying interaction.

</details>


### [23] [Constraining Ultra-Light Vector Bosons via Solar Neutrino Oscillations: The Gauged $L_μ- L_τ$ Model and Prospects for JUNO and XLZD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33877)
*Ilídio Lopes*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} extension of the Standard Model with an ultra-light Z′ boson, using solar neutrino data to constrain its mass < 5.7×10⁻¹⁷ eV and coupling; it improves χ² over standard 3ν oscillations and links to ultra-light dark matter and small-scale structure problems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an independent phenomenological framework for solar neutrino anomalies using the anomaly-free U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} gauge symmetry—historically motivated by (g−2)_μ—but here applied to solar neutrino flavor conversion via new matter potentials.

Method: Introduces a gauged U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} model with an ultra-light Z′ boson; incorporates kinetic mixing with photons to generate an effective matter potential in the Sun; performs global fit to solar neutrino data (Borexino, SNO, Super-K) + standard solar model to constrain Z′ mass and coupling; computes χ² for best-fit and compares with standard 3ν oscillation scenario.

Result: Z′ mass constrained to m_{Z′} < 5.7×10⁻¹⁷ eV; best-fit coupling η₀ ≈ −2 yields χ²_ν = 2.7 (vs. 3.1 in standard 3ν); effective four-fermion coupling g_{Z′}²ε/m_{Z′}² ≈ 6.6×10⁻²³ eV⁻² (~5.7×G_F); implies g_{Z′} ~ 8×10⁻¹² for ε≈1 and m_{Z′}=1 eV; projections given for JUNO and XLZD.

Conclusion: The U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} model with ultra-light Z′ provides a viable, testable extension explaining solar neutrino data better than standard oscillations, while simultaneously offering an ultra-light dark matter candidate potentially resolving CDM small-scale issues; terrestrial long-baseline experiments offer complementary probes.

Abstract: We present an extension of the Standard Model based upon the gauged $U(1)_{L_μ- L_τ}$ symmetry, featuring an ultra-light vector boson that couples to the difference of muon and tau lepton numbers. This anomaly-free construction, historically motivated by the muon's anomalous magnetic moment, is employed here as an independent phenomenological framework for solar neutrino physics. Through kinetic mixing with the photon, the $Z^\prime$ boson generates an effective matter potential within the solar interior, thereby modifying neutrino flavour oscillations via the Mikheyev--Smirnov--Wolfenstein mechanism. Utilising recent solar neutrino measurements from Borexino, SNO, and Super-Kamiokande, together with an up-to-date standard solar model, we constrain the boson mass to be below $5.7\times 10^{-17}\,{\rm eV}$, a potential ultra-light dark matter candidate. The optimal model, with coupling parameter $η_o \approx -2$, yields $χ_ν^2 = 2.7$, compared with $χ_ν^2 = 3.1$ for the standard three-flavour scenario. This best fit corresponds to an effective four-fermion coupling $g_{Z^\prime}^2 \varepsilon / m_{Z^\prime}^2 \approx 6.6 \times 10^{-23}\,{\rm eV}^{-2}$, approximately $5.7$ times the Fermi constant, equivalent for $\varepsilon \approx 1$ and a reference mediator mass of $1\,{\rm eV}$ to a gauge coupling $g_{Z^\prime} \approx 8\times 10^{-12}$. We also present projections for forthcoming experiments: the Jiangmen Underground Neutrino Observatory (JUNO), operating since August 2025 with world-leading precision on oscillation parameters, together with the XLZD consortium, should substantially refine these constraints. Such ultra-light particles may address long-standing discrepancies in galaxy simulations within the cold dark matter paradigm, whilst terrestrial long-baseline neutrino experiments provide complementary probes of this parameter space.

</details>


### [24] [Resonantly-Enhanced Baryogenesis through Asymmetric Capture by Primordial Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33031)
*Fayez Abu-Ajamieh,Xiaoyan Huo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel mechanism where resonant CP violation in a mixed system of two nearly degenerate unstable fermions, coupled with primordial black hole (PBH) capture and evaporation, generates the observed baryon asymmetry of the Universe (BAU); it achieves agreement with η_B^obs after thermal and continuous-evaporation corrections, and predicts detectable MHz-frequency gravitational-wave signatures via U-DECIGO.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous PBH-based BAU mechanisms suffer from loop-suppressed CP-violating scattering asymmetries, yielding insufficient baryon asymmetry; this work addresses that limitation by introducing resonant CP violation to enhance the asymmetry generation efficiency.

Method: The authors construct a model with two nearly degenerate unstable fermions Y₁, Y₂ whose mass splitting is comparable to their decay widths, enabling resonant CP-violating interference in coherent Y₁–Y₂ state production and decay; they embed this CP source into a PBH-capture transport framework, compute BAU both in zero-temperature sudden-evaporation and realistic finite-temperature continuous-evaporation regimes, and calculate associated scalar-induced gravitational-wave spectra from PBH-forming primordial fluctuations.

Result: The model yields η_B^{T=0} ≃ 2.37×10⁻¹⁰ (2.72×η_B^obs) in the zero-temperature limit; including thermal corrections and continuous PBH evaporation reduces it to η_B = 8.72×10⁻¹¹ — matching the observed value; even without evaporation, η_B^no evap ≃ 9.1×10⁻¹² (~10.5% of observed); predicted GW spectra peak at f ≃ 0.16–0.17 MHz with h²Ω_GW^peak ≃ (3–7)×10⁻⁸, detectable by U-DECIGO at SNR > 10 under idealized sensitivity.

Conclusion: Resonant CP violation in a degenerate fermion system provides a viable, non-loop-suppressed pathway for PBH-mediated baryogenesis; the model quantitatively reproduces the observed BAU and predicts testable high-frequency gravitational-wave signals, linking early-universe baryogenesis to near-future GW observatories.

Abstract: Primordial black holes (PBHs) can generate the Baryon Asymmetry of the Universe (BAU) through asymmetric capture of baryon-charge-carrying particles, but previous realizations of this mechanism typically yield an asymmetry well below the observed value because the relevant CP-violating scattering asymmetry is loop suppressed. We show that this limitation can be overcome through resonant CP violation in a mixed system of two nearly degenerate unstable fermions, $Y_{1,2}$, whose mass splitting is comparable to their decay widths. Rare production of the coherent $Y_1$--$Y_2$ state, followed by CP-asymmetric return and loss decays, generates an $\mathcal{O}(0.1)$ difference in integrated decay probabilities while preserving the global baryon charge at the Lagrangian level. Embedding this resonant source in the PBH-capture transport system yields the observed baryon asymmetry. We first reproduce the mechanism in a zero-temperature, sudden-evaporation treatment, which gives $η_B^{T=0}\simeq2.37\times10^{-10}\simeq2.72 η_B^{\rm obs}$. We find that after including thermal corrections and continuous PBH evaporation and entropy production, the resulting BAU becomes $η_B = 8.72\times 10^{-11}$ in agreement with the observed value. An interesting finding of the model is that even if PBH absorption remains active, we would still have BAU $η_{B}^{\text{no evap}} \sim 9.1 \times 10^{-12} \sim 0.105 η_B^{\rm obs}$. We further investigate gravitational-wave signatures associated with the primordial fluctuations responsible for PBH formation. The corresponding scalar-induced gravitational-wave spectra peak at $f \sim 0.16\text{--}0.17~\mathrm{MHz}$, with $h^2Ω_{\rm GW}^{\rm peak} \sim (3\text{--}7)\times 10^{-8}$. Under idealized sensitivity assumptions, U-DECIGO can achieve signal-to-noise ratios above 10 for the benchmark spectra.

</details>


### [25] [Gottfried-Jackson polarization filters for holographic dilaton and transverse-traceless gluon responses in near-threshold $J/ψ$ photoproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33908)
*Arkadiy I. Syamtomov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies dilaton-null spin-projection channels in the Hatta-Yang holographic graviton-dilaton amplitude for near-threshold J/ψ photoproduction, uses polarization observables to isolate gluonic contributions and test tensor structure, and finds the fixed-shape holographic model insufficient to globally describe modern GlueX/CLAS12 data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate and test the gluonic (dilaton-mediated) contribution to near-threshold J/ψ photoproduction using spin projection and polarization observables, distinguishing it from competing mechanisms and assessing the validity of the holographic graviton-dilaton amplitude.

Method: Analysis of the Hatta-Yang holographic graviton-dilaton amplitude in the Gottfried-Jackson frame; identification of dilaton-null spin channels (m=0, m=−λ_γ); derivation of spin-density-matrix elements under beam polarization; extraction of TT-only and dilaton-TT interference terms; comparison with differential cross-section data from GlueX and CLAS12.

Result: The m=0 and m=−λ_γ amplitudes are dilaton-null; the m=0 channel with transverse photon polarization isolates A_g(t)+B_g(t) and requires nucleon spin transfer; beam-polarization observables yield TT-only controls and a bilinear interference term; the fixed-shape holographic amplitude fails global description of near-threshold data with a single normalization.

Conclusion: Polarization filters serve as stringent tests of the production tensor structure—not predictions from a fully validated reaction model—highlighting the need for dynamical or shape-modified holographic amplitudes to describe current experimental data.

Abstract: Within the Hatta-Yang holographic graviton-dilaton amplitude for near-threshold $J/ψ$ photoproduction, we identify exact dilaton-null spin-projection channels in the Gottfried-Jackson basis. The $m=0$ and $m=-λ_γ$ amplitudes receive no dilaton contribution; for photon polarization normal to the reaction plane the $m=0$ channel further isolates $A_g(t)+B_g(t)$ and requires nucleon spin transfer. Standard beam-polarization spin-density-matrix elements then furnish TT-only controls and a bilinear containing linear dilaton-TT interference. A comparison with modern GlueX and CLAS12 cross sections shows that the original fixed-shape holographic amplitude cannot describe the full near-threshold data with one overall normalization, making the polarization filters tests of the production tensor rather than predictions of a globally validated reaction model.

</details>


### [26] [Stress in static force fields and the sign of the D-term](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33151)
*Adam Freese*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates the sign of the D-term in hadrons and finds it is determined by the spin of the mediating force field—not mechanical stability—explaining why hadrons (bound by spin-1 gluons) have negative D-terms, unlike the hydrogen atom (bound by spin-1 EM field but with positive D-term due to its abelian, long-range nature); confinement effects may further enforce negativity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the persistent but misleading speculation that the negative D-term in hadrons reflects a universal mechanical stability condition, especially given counterexamples like the hydrogen atom’s positive D-term.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic analysis decomposing the D-term into contributions from static abelian force fields classified by spin parity (spin-even vs. spin-odd), and examining how stress tensor components couple to field quanta spin.

Result: Spin-even fields yield negative D-term contributions; spin-odd (e.g., electromagnetic) fields yield positive contributions; identical potentials from different-spin fields produce same wave functions but opposite D-term signs—hence D-term sign is unrelated to stability.

Conclusion: The negative D-term in hadrons arises not from stability but from the spin-1 (odd-spin) nature of the non-abelian gluon field—yet its negativity remains atypical compared to abelian spin-1 fields, suggesting color confinement modifies the effective stress distribution to enforce negativity.

Abstract: The sign of the D-term is negative for a variety of hadrons, including the proton and pion. Speculation that this is the result of a mechanical stability condition has persisted despite counterexamples, including the positive D-term of the hydrogen atom. In this work, I explore how the sign of the D-term is influenced by the stress carried by static abelian force fields, finding the contributions from spin-even fields to be negative and the contributions from spin-odd fields (such as the electromagnetic field) to be positive. These contributions correlate with the signs of the D-terms of the fields' respective quanta. Since fields with different spin can produce identical potentials---thus resulting in equally-stable composite systems with identical wave functions---the sign of the D-term has nothing to do with stability. Since hadrons are bound by spin-one gluons, their negative D-terms appear atypical and require explanation. I briefly speculate on how color flux confinement could produce a negative hadronic D-term.

</details>


### [27] [Meson photoproduction at Jefferson Lab: from two-meson final states to meson-baryon spin-density matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35045)
*Vincent Mathieu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent progress in meson photoproduction studies, particularly $ηπ$ and $ρ^-Δ^{++}$ channels, using JPAC, GlueX, and CLAS data; it derives the full angular distribution for $γp → ρ^-Δ^{++}$ with linearly polarized photons, identifying 56 spin-density matrix elements, and shows simplification to 14 parameters under single Regge pole exchange.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance the search for exotic hybrid mesons—especially the $π_1(1600)$—by identifying promising reaction channels such as $γp → b_1πΔ^{++}$, with $γp → ρ^-Δ^{++}$ serving as a necessary preparatory step due to shared kinematic and spin-structure features.

Method: Derivation of the complete angular distribution for $γp → ρ^-Δ^{++}$ using a linearly polarized photon beam; construction of the 56-element double-index spin-density matrix encoding spin correlations between $ρ$ and $Δ$ decay products; analysis under factorization assumptions (e.g., single Regge pole exchange) to reduce parameter space.

Result: The full intensity distribution for $γp → ρ^-Δ^{++}$ is expressed in terms of 56 independent spin-density matrix elements; under Regge factorization, it simplifies to a product of standard $ρ$ and $Δ$ decay distributions dependent on only 14 parameters.

Conclusion: The $γp → ρ^-Δ^{++}$ channel provides a well-controlled framework for testing spin-density formalism and Regge dynamics, laying groundwork for future exotic searches in $b_1πΔ^{++}$, and confirming that $ηπ$ photoproduction is now quantitatively understood across resonance and high-mass regimes.

Abstract: Recent progress in the understanding of meson photoproduction, obtained from the joint effort of the Joint Physics Analysis Center (JPAC) and the GlueX and CLAS experiments at Jefferson Lab, is reviewed. The photoproduction of the $ηπ$ system is now well understood, from the $a_2(1320)$ resonance region to the high-mass region dominated by double Regge exchanges. Since the lightest hybrid meson, the $π_1(1600)$, is expected to decay predominantly into $b_1π$, reactions with a recoiling $Δ^{++}$ are promising channels for exotic searches. As a first step towards $γp \to b_1 πΔ^{++}$, the complete angular distribution of $γp \to ρ^-Δ^{++}$ with a linearly polarized photon beam is derived. It involves 56 double-index spin-density matrix elements, which describe the correlations between the $ρ$ and the $Δ$ decays. When the production amplitudes factorize, as is the case for the exchange of a single Regge pole, the intensity reduces to the product of the standard $ρ$ and $Δ$ decay distributions and depends on only 14 parameters.

</details>


### [28] [Probing Solar Chameleons with XENONnT Ionization-Only Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33198)
*Guan-Wen Yuan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents the first search for solar chameleons using low-energy S2-only data from XENONnT, extending sensitivity to ~0.04 keV_ee and setting an upper limit log10 β_eff < −3.64 in the disformal-dominated regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe screened scalar field models (e.g., chameleons) linking dark energy to laboratory-scale new physics, leveraging solar production as a direct source and ionization-only (S2-only) data to access sub-keV electronic recoils beyond conventional ER analyses.

Method: Analysis of 7.83 tonne-year XENONnT ionization-only (S2-only) data across SR0–SR2; forward modeling of predicted chameleon-induced electronic-recoil spectrum through run-dependent detector response; joint binned likelihood over seven corrected-S2 (cS2) bins; Bayesian sampling of five-dimensional parameter space {β_e, β_γ, M_e, Λ, n} to constrain conformal and disformal electron couplings.

Result: First solar chameleon search using low-energy S2-only data; recoil threshold extended to ~0.04 keV_ee; upper limit log10 β_eff < −3.64 in disformal-dominated regime; weaker constraint than prior ER analysis due to steeply falling disformal absorption rate and rising background at low energies.

Conclusion: Low-energy ionization data constitute a viable new experimental channel for testing solar chameleons and screened dark-energy models, despite current sensitivity limitations at sub-keV energies.

Abstract: Screened scalar fields provide a possible connection between dark energy and laboratory-scale new physics while remaining compatible with existing fifth-force constraints. Solar production offers a direct probe of these models, and ionization-only measurements extend the sensitivity of liquid-xenon detectors into the sub-keV regime. We present the first search for solar chameleons using low-energy S2-only data, based on the 7.83 tonne-year XENONnT ionization-only data set. The predicted electronic-recoil spectrum is folded through the run-dependent detector response and tested directly in corrected-S2 (cS2) space. A joint binned likelihood combines the seven cS2 bins of science runs SR0, SR1, and SR2. We sample the five-dimensional parameter space $\{β_e,β_γ,M_e,Λ,n\}$ and derive posterior constraints on the conformal and disformal electron couplings. The data extend the recoil-energy reach to approximately $0.04~{\rm keV}_{ee}$, where the few-electron detector response and instrumental backgrounds must be modeled explicitly. In the disformal-dominated regime, the result is expressed in terms of $β_{\rm eff}\equivβ_γ({\rm eV}/M_e)^4$. We obtain the upper limit $\log_{10}β_{\rm eff}<-3.64$. Although the present analysis reaches a lower energy threshold than our previous XENONnT electronic-recoil analysis, the resulting constraint is weaker, primarily because the disformal absorption rate decreases rapidly toward low recoil energies, where the background rate also becomes larger. This analysis establishes low-energy ionization data as a new experimental channel for testing solar chameleons and screened dark-energy models.

</details>

Abstract: We compute the Higgs thermal mass and the electroweak and colour Debye mass parameters to full $\mathcal{O}(g^6)$ in dimensional reduction, where $g$ represents a gauge coupling or the top Yukawa. We employ new methods to evaluate previously unknown mixed-signature sum-integrals.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Abstract: We investigate mono-$Z$ signature at the future electron-positron collider within the dark matter effective field theory (DMEFT) framework, considering operators up to dimension six for scalar, fermionic, and vector dark matter (DM) candidates. The operators are classified according to their underlying production mechanisms. We identify the phenomenologically viable parameter space by imposing the observed DM relic abundance together with direct and indirect detection constraints, Higgs invisible decay limits, and LHC mono-$Z$ bounds obtained through a recasting analysis. We study the discovery prospects of the surviving DM operators at the future $e^-e^+$ collider including initial and final state radiation, detector simulation, and the reconstruction of both leptonic and hadronic $Z$-boson decay modes. We exploit missing energy, missing transverse momentum, recoil mass, $Z$-boson pseudorapidity, together with beam polarization, which play a significant role to distinguish the signal from the Standard Model background. We find that fermionic dipole, scalar leptophilic, vector Higgs-portal, and gauge-portal DM operators offer robust mono-$Z$ discovery potential, probing multi-TeV effective scales.

</details>


### [31] [Scheme Transformations in Non-Linear QCD Evolution: Towards a Stable NLO BK Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33821)
*Renaud Boussarie,Paul Caucal,Piotr Korcyl,Yacine Mehtar-Tani*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper resolves the instability in NLO high-energy QCD evolution by introducing a systematic scheme-transformation framework via the Doi-Peliti formalism, eliminating double collinear logarithms in the BK equation and enabling stable numerical evolution up to rapidity Y=10.

Abstract: High-energy QCD evolution at NLO suffers from large collinear logarithms that make the perturbative expansion of the Balitsky-Kovchegov (BK) equation unstable. We develop a systematic scheme-transformation framework using the Doi-Peliti formalism to relate projectile ($k^+$) and target ($k^-$) factorization schemes. The transformation of high-energy operators and the evolution kernel generates NLO terms that cancel the problematic double collinear logarithm. The resulting NLO BK equation is free of this instability, with only single collinear and double anti-collinear enhancements remaining. Numerical solutions show stable evolution up to $Y=10$ for both Golec-Biernat-Wusthoff and McLerran-Venugopalan initial conditions, providing a consistent path toward combining stable NLO evolution with rotated NLO impact factors.

</details>

These values can be achieved by a possible out-of-equilibrium decay of a modulus $φ$ with mass $m_φ\gg m_{\tilde h}$ and a non-vanishing branching ratio into $\tilde h$'s which plays a decisive role in reducing $T_{\rm rh}$ below its upper limit via the non-thermal $\tilde h$ production. The modulus $φ$ can be identified with the scalar component of the goldstino superfield which is involved in the generation of the $μ$ parameter of MSSM (with $μ\simeq m_{\tilde h}$) via the Giudice-Masiero mechanism.

### [33] [Sub-MeV gamma-ray lines from an endothermic interpretation of the LZ recoil candidate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33952)

  <summary>Details</summary>

Result: A robust correlation is established: E_R ∝ δ from upscattering recoils and E_γ ≃ δ from radiative decay, yielding a testable sub-MeV gamma-ray line prediction aligned with COSI’s 2027 launch and sensitivity.

Conclusion: If the LZ candidate is confirmed as dark matter, it strongly motivates targeted searches for sub-MeV gamma-ray lines—COSI offers a near-term, high-sensitivity probe to validate or falsify this split-dark-matter interpretation.

Abstract: Endothermic upscattering of heavy dark matter offers a compelling interpretation of the high-energy nuclear-recoil candidate recently reported by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ), with a characteristic recoil energy $E_R \propto δ$, where $δ$ is the mass splitting between the dark matter states. Cosmological and experimental constraints motivate the depletion of the primordial heavier state, which can be regenerated in the Galactic halo. We show that, if the heavier state decays radiatively, the same mass splitting sets the energy of a gamma-ray line, $E_γ\simeq δ$. For our benchmark, this leads to a sub-MeV line within the projected reach of the Compton Spectrometer and Imager (COSI), scheduled for launch in 2027. The confirmation of the LZ candidate event as a dark matter signal would therefore motivate dedicated searches for sub-MeV gamma-ray lines, with COSI providing a timely opportunity to test this interpretation.

</details>


### [34] [Finite-Size Effects on Symmetry Restoration in a Rotating Scalar Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34038)

TL;DR: This paper studies how rotation affects Z2 symmetry restoration in a finite-sized λφ⁴ quantum field theory, using Dirichlet boundary conditions and one-loop effective potential with ring resummation; it finds that the critical temperature depends explicitly on both angular velocity and transverse size due to spectral coupling, not just light-cylinder causality.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how rigid rotation and finite-volume geometry jointly influence spontaneous symmetry breaking and restoration in quantum field theory—beyond standard thermal or infinite-volume approximations.
