Result: The symmetry-restoration temperature acquires an explicit dependence on both angular velocity Ω and transverse radius R (via the discrete spectrum), deviating from predictions based solely on the light-cylinder constraint (ΩR = c). The finite-size spectral structure fundamentally modifies rotational effects on phase transitions.

Conclusion: Finite geometry and rotation are non-separable in determining critical behavior: the interplay between rotational kinematics and boundary-induced quantization leads to a richer phase structure where the transition temperature encodes both causal and spectral features of the rotating finite system.

Abstract: We investigate $\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetry restoration in a real $λφ^4$ theory confined to a finite cylindrical region undergoing rigid rotation. The finite transverse extent is imposed through Dirichlet boundary conditions, resulting in a discrete Fourier-Bessel spectrum and an explicit radial dependence of the fluctuation propagator. Using the background-field method, we derive the one-loop effective potential while retaining the spatial structure induced by the finite geometry. At finite temperature, rotation enters through the angular-momentum-dependent shifts of the thermal mode energies, coupling the rotational state to the discrete transverse spectrum. The resulting coincident-point propagator defines a position-dependent thermal self-energy, which is incorporated into the effective potential through ring resummation. This yields a spatially resolved effective potential whose structure depends simultaneously on the background field, temperature, angular velocity, and transverse size. We then use its spatial average to determine the symmetry-restoration temperature and study its dependence on the rotational state and the finite-volume spectrum. The results show that the rotational modification of the transition temperature retains an explicit dependence on the transverse size, reflecting the spectral structure of the finite rotating system rather than solely the causal constraint associated with the light cylinder.

</details>


### [35] [Pseudospectra in Holographic QCD Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34084)
*Luiz F. Ferreira*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes spectral stability of quasinormal modes (QNMs) in nonconformal holographic QCD models (soft-wall and analytic IHQCD) using pseudospectral methods; finds temperature-dependent instability growth, linking increasing Im(ω) to hadronic decoherence and melting, with pseudospectrum offering a complementary diagnostic to spectral functions for dissociation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify spectral stability of quasinormal modes in nonconformal holographic QCD models—where intrinsic dimensionful scales (e.g., confinement scale, temperature) break conformal invariance—and assess how instability under perturbations relates to physical phenomena like hadronic melting.

Method: Formulates equations of motion for scalar/vector fields (soft-wall) and tensor glueball fluctuations (IHQCD) as linear eigenvalue problems; applies pseudospectral analysis to compute the pseudospectrum and quantify sensitivity of QNMs to small perturbations; compares pseudospectral instability with spectral function behavior across temperature.

Result: Quasinormal mode spectra exhibit nontrivial temperature-dependent pseudospectral instability: |Im(ω)| increases with T, indicating faster decay and loss of coherence; at high T, ω ∼ cT emerges only after hadronic states have melted; pseudospectral broadening correlates with and complements spectral function suppression as a signature of dissociation.

Conclusion: Pseudospectral instability provides a robust, quantitative, and complementary probe of spectral degradation in holographic QCD, capturing the onset and progression of hadronic melting beyond what conventional spectral functions alone reveal.

Abstract: We investigate the spectral stability of quasinormal modes in nonconformal holographic QCD models using pseudospectral analysis. We first consider the soft-wall model, focusing on scalar and vector fields, and then extend the analysis to the analytic Improved Holographic QCD (IHQCD) model, where we study tensor glueball fluctuations. In both cases, the equations of motion are formulated as linear eigenvalue problems, allowing the instability of the quasinormal spectrum to small perturbations to be quantified through the pseudospectrum. We find that the spectral stability of the quasinormal modes exhibits a nontrivial dependence on temperature, reflecting the presence of intrinsic dimensionful scales in holographic QCD models. As the temperature increases, the magnitude of the imaginary part of the quasinormal frequencies grows, signaling an increasing decay rate and the progressive loss of coherence of the corresponding hadronic excitations. At sufficiently high temperatures, the quasinormal frequencies approach an approximately linear dependence on temperature, although this regime is reached only after the associated hadronic states have melted. We further compare the pseudospectral instability with the behavior of the corresponding spectral functions. Our results indicate that the growth of pseudospectral instability provides a complementary characterization of the loss of spectral stability and can serve as a useful diagnostic of hadronic dissociation and melting in holographic QCD.

</details>


### [36] [Freeze-in of anapole and charge-radius inelastic dark matter: X-ray probes of a decoupled vector portal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34230)
*Krzysztof Jodłowski,Chih-Ting Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies freeze-in production and experimental detection of inelastic dark matter (iDM) coupled via dimension-6 anapole and charge-radius operators—arising from a heavy dark photon portal—and focuses on sub-dielectron-threshold mass splittings where the excited state χ₁ decays dominantly via χ₁ → χ₀ + 3γ, yielding a distinctive keV–MeV photon spectrum; it computes the decay rate and spectrum analytically (Euler–Heisenberg limit) and with full one-loop corrections, then combines X-ray constraints (INTEGRAL/SPI, NuSTAR), freeze-in relic density targets, direct detection (including LZ anomaly), and accelerator limits to probe the reheating temperature and vector portal decoupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore viable iDM scenarios with small mass splittings (Δ < 2mₑ) where χ₁ is cosmologically long-lived and decays via a loop-suppressed three-photon channel, enabling unique X-ray signatures; and to assess whether such models can simultaneously satisfy freeze-in production, X-ray bounds, direct detection anomalies (e.g., LZ), and accelerator constraints.

Method: Analytical computation of χ₁ → χ₀ + 3γ decay rate and photon spectrum in the Euler–Heisenberg effective theory; inclusion of finite-electron-mass corrections via full one-loop QED amplitude; combination of observational constraints: X-ray (INTEGRAL/SPI, NuSTAR), freeze-in relic density (requiring specific reheating temperature T_R), direct detection (exothermic downscattering), and accelerator searches; parameter-space scanning over Δ (100–900 keV), DM mass, and coupling strength.

Result: X-ray data (especially INTEGRAL/SPI at Δ = 900 keV) strongly constrain high-mass freeze-in branches; NuSTAR limits are weaker at Δ = 100 keV due to Γ ∝ Δ⁹ dependence; exothermic downscattering can explain the LZ excess for specific Δ and couplings; surviving χ₁ population yields testable signals across multiple probes; reheating temperature T_R is constrained to be ~1–100 MeV depending on parameters.

Conclusion: X-ray observations provide a powerful and complementary probe of freeze-in iDM through a decoupled vector portal, tightly linking spectral features (Δ), reheating temperature, and particle physics parameters; the three-photon decay channel and exothermic scattering together enable multi-messenger validation or exclusion of this iDM framework.

Abstract: We study freeze-in production and experimental probes of inelastic dark matter (iDM) coupled to the Standard Model through dimension-6 transition anapole and charge-radius operators. These interactions arise upon integrating out the heavy dark photon of a broken $U(1)_D$ vector portal. We focus on mass splittings below the dielectron threshold, where the excited-state decay is cosmologically long-lived and decays predominantly through $χ_1\toχ_0+3γ$, producing a distinctive keV--MeV photon spectrum. We calculate the decay rate and photon spectrum analytically in the Euler--Heisenberg limit and evaluate finite-electron-mass corrections using the full one-loop amplitude. We then combine X-ray limits with freeze-in relic density targets and complementary direct detection (DD) and accelerator constraints. The surviving $χ_1$ population also leads to exothermic downscattering signatures at DD experiments, including the possibility of explaining the LZ signal, while the three-photon decay is constrained by X-ray data. At $Δ=900\,\mathrm{keV}$, INTEGRAL/SPI probes the Boltzmann-suppressed high-mass branches of the freeze-in targets, whereas the NuSTAR limits at $Δ=100\,\mathrm{keV}$ are substantially weaker because of the steep splitting dependence of the decay width. Our results demonstrate the potential of X-ray observations as a probe of freeze-in through a decoupled vector portal and of the reheating temperature governing the iDM production.

</details>


### [37] [Next-to-Next-to-Leading Order QCD Corrections to $η_t \to HZ$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34364)
*Cai-Ping Jia,Yan-Qing Ma,Huai-Min Yu,Yu-Jie Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the NNLO QCD correction to the short-distance coefficient for the decay η_t → HZ within NRQCD, retaining full Higgs and Z boson mass dependence; it finds slow perturbative convergence (NLO and NNLO suppress LO width by 29% and 19%), validates the result via three consistency checks, and provides a key input for probing top-Higgs coupling near the tt̄ threshold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent LHC observations of a threshold enhancement in the tt̄ system suggestive of toponium-like dynamics motivate precision study of the pseudoscalar toponium state η_t decaying to HZ, to probe the top-Higgs interaction.

Method: Computes the NNLO QCD hard short-distance coefficient for η_t → HZ in the NRQCD factorization framework, including full m_H and m_Z dependence, two-loop virtual corrections, and real double-gluon emission; performs scale dependence analysis and three analytical consistency checks (large-mass limit, γ_5 prescription independence, RG-logarithm structure).

Result: At μ_R = μ_Λ = m_t, NLO and NNLO corrections suppress the LO decay width by ~29% and ~19%, respectively; the two-loop contribution is ~2/3 the one-loop effect and same-sign, indicating slow convergence; the NNLO coefficient is ~52% of LO. Three rigorous checks confirm correctness.

Conclusion: The computed NNLO coefficient is a necessary high-precision input for future threshold analyses of η_t → HZ at colliders and for constraining the top-Higgs Yukawa coupling; its accuracy is robustly validated.

Abstract: Motivated by recent LHC observations of a threshold enhancement in the $t\bar t$ system consistent with toponium-like dynamics, we compute the next-to-next-to-leading order (NNLO) QCD correction to the hard short-distance coefficient for $η_t\to HZ$, where $η_t$ denotes the pseudoscalar color-singlet configuration $t\bar t({}^1S_0^{[1]})$. Within the NRQCD factorization framework, we retain the full dependence on the Higgs- and $Z$-boson masses and include both the two-loop virtual corrections and the real double-gluon-emission channel. At next-to-leading order (NLO), the finite-mass result remains close to its large-mass limit. The NNLO coefficient develops logarithms of both the renormalization scale $μ_R$ and the NRQCD factorization scale $μ_Λ$. For $μ_R=μ_Λ=m_t$, the NLO and NNLO terms suppress the leading-order width by about $29\%$ and $19\%$, respectively; the two-loop contribution is about two thirds of the one-loop effect and of the same sign, so the perturbative series converges slowly and the NNLO prediction amounts to about $52\%$ of the leading-order width. The resulting coefficient provides a necessary ingredient for future threshold studies of $η_t\to HZ$ and for assessing the sensitivity of this channel to the top-Higgs interaction. The accuracy of the result is further supported by three checks: reproduction of the known large-mass limit at one loop, independence of the extracted coefficient on the $γ_5$ prescription, and consistency of its scale logarithms with the renormalization-group structure.

</details>


### [38] [Radial Excitation Spectra of Light Pseudoscalar and Vector Mesons in Light-Front Holographic QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34644)
*Abhisth Srivastava*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper improves Light-Front Holographic QCD by extending the transverse confining potential with Coulombic and logarithmic terms, reducing average mass deviation for radial meson excitations from 7.6% to 5.2% vs. experiment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The standard soft-wall LFHQCD model yields ~17–18% deviations for pion radial excitations; improved description of short- and intermediate-range confinement is needed.

Method: Extended transverse confining potential (soft-wall + Coulombic + logarithmic terms); numerical solution of light-front Schrödinger equation; combined with Brodsky-de Téramond longitudinal prescription to compute full meson mass spectra.

Result: Average deviation from experimental masses reduced from 7.6% to 5.2% for nine radially excited states (π, ρ, K*); Regge trajectories better reproduced; Coulombic (short-range) and logarithmic (intermediate-range) contributions are essential.

Conclusion: Adding Coulombic and logarithmic corrections to the transverse potential significantly enhances LFHQCD’s predictive power for radial excitations, validating the physical relevance of multi-scale confinement in the holographic framework.

Abstract: The non-perturbative regime of Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) poses significant challenges for analytical treatment. Light-Front Holographic QCD (LFHQCD) offers an effective semiclassical framework for exploring hadronic structure through a holographic dual description of confinement. While the standard soft-wall model, along with the Brodsky-de Téramond (BdT) longitudinal prescription, provides satisfactory results for meson ground states, it shows deviations of around 17 to $18\%$ for radial excitations of the pion family. In this work, we extend the transverse confining potential by adding Coulombic and logarithmic contributions beyond the standard soft-wall framework. We solve the resulting light-front Schrödinger equation numerically, and we combine the transverse mass spectrum with the BdT longitudinal prescription to obtain the complete meson mass spectrum. We compute the radial excitation spectra of the $π$, $ρ$, and $K^{*}$ meson families and analyze their Regge trajectories. The modified framework reduces the overall average deviation from experimental data from about $7.6\%$ to $5.2\%$ compared to the standard soft-wall model, representing a $\sim 32\%$ improvement for the fitted nine-state dataset in describing radially excited states. The results show that short-range Coulombic and intermediate-range logarithmic contributions are important for refining the radial meson spectrum within the light-front holographic approach.

</details>


### [39] [Soft-Collinear Chiral Perturbation Theory for $B \to π$ form factors at large recoil](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34696)
*Thorsten Feldmann,Jack Jenkins,Jaime del Palacio Lirola*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a hadronic effective theory for B→π decays at large recoil by combining heavy-quark and chiral symmetries with SCET factorization, introduces local soft/collinear chiral symmetries to match non-local SCET operators onto hadronic ones, and computes chiral-logarithmic corrections to form factors—finding inconsistency with Hard-Pion χPT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve theoretical inconsistencies in describing B→π form factors at large recoil (E_π ≈ M_B/2), where both heavy-quark symmetry and chiral dynamics are relevant, and where existing frameworks like Hard-Pion Chiral Perturbation Theory fail to capture the correct chiral-logarithmic structure from SCET-based factorization.

Method: Constructs a hadronic effective theory embedding quasi-static heavy-meson fields coupled to soft and collinear pions; exploits SCET’s soft-collinear factorization to identify emergent duplicated chiral symmetries; promotes soft and collinear chiral transformations to local symmetries to systematically match non-local SCET four-quark operators onto local hadronic operators below Λ_χSB; computes one-loop matrix elements of leading hadronic operators to extract pion-mass dependence.

Result: Derives the logarithmically enhanced m_π² ln m_π² dependence of B→π form factors at large recoil; shows that the chiral-logarithmic pattern arising from non-local transition currents in the new hadronic theory disagrees with predictions of Hard-Pion χPT.

Conclusion: A consistent hadronic description of B→π at large recoil requires explicit treatment of soft-collinear factorization and local chiral symmetries in distinct kinematic sectors—Hard-Pion χPT is insufficient due to its inability to reproduce the correct SCET-motivated chiral-log structure.

Abstract: We develop an effective hadronic theory for heavy-meson decays into energetic pions, organized by the approximate heavy-quark and chiral symmetries. The dynamical degrees of freedom of the theory consist of quasi-static heavy-meson fields coupled to soft and collinear pions, relevant for exclusive $B \to π$ transitions at large recoil energy ${E_π\simeq M_B/2}$. Our approach exploits the factorization of soft and collinear quarks in Soft-Collinear Effective Theory (SCET), leading to an emergent duplication of chiral symmetry in the two kinematic sectors. Promoting the soft and collinear chiral transformations to local symmetries enables us to systematically match non-local four-quark operators in SCET -- governing the soft-overlap contribution to heavy-to-light form factors -- onto effective operators constructed from hadronic fields below the scale of chiral symmetry breaking. As a first application, we compute the logarithmically enhanced pion-mass dependence of the $B \to π$ form factors at large recoil by calculating the one-loop matrix elements of the leading hadronic operators. We find that the non-local transition currents in our effective theory lead to a pattern of chiral-logarithmic corrections that is not compatible with the existing formulation provided by Hard-Pion Chiral Perturbation Theory.

</details>


### [40] [Termination of the Radial Ladder in Mixed-Flavor Heavy Mesons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34734)
*Wen-Xuan Zhang,Wen-Nian Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether radial excitations of heavy mesons (Ds, Bs, Bc) terminate due to confinement screening by light-quark pair creation, using a screened Godfrey–Isgur Hamiltonian solved via Gaussian expansion; it predicts mass saturation thresholds and identifies safe radial quantum number ranges (nsafe ≈ 4–6 for Ds, 5–7 for Bs, 9–11 for Bc), with experimental testability at BESIII, Belle II, and LHCb.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the open question of whether the radial excitation ladder of heavy mesons terminates due to confinement screening by light-quark pair creation—a fundamental issue in hadron spectroscopy.

Method: Solved a screened Godfrey–Isgur Hamiltonian using the Gaussian expansion method; screening length determined from threshold-dependent string-breaking analysis and calibrated to charmonium and bottomonium spectral anchors.

Result: Predicted saturation masses at 3.36 GeV (Ds), 6.78 GeV (Bs), and 8.19 GeV (Bc); RMS radii diverge and level spacings drop below 10 MeV; mass–radius decoupling yields nsafe ≈ 4–6 (Ds), 5–7 (Bs), 9–11 (Bc); discovery windows close above 3.49, 6.86, and 8.24 GeV respectively.

Conclusion: The radial excitation ladder terminates first in Ds, then Bs, and last in Bc due to screening-induced saturation; this termination is experimentally falsifiable with upcoming high-statistics data from BESIII, Belle II, and LHCb.

Abstract: A fundamental open question in hadron spectroscopy is whether the radial excitation ladder of heavy mesons terminates at a finite level once confinement is screened by light-quark pair creation. We address this question for the mixed-flavor mesons $D_s$, $B_s$, and $B_c$ by solving a screened Godfrey--Isgur Hamiltonian with the Gaussian expansion method. The screening length is fixed by threshold-dependent string breaking analysis, and is matched to the charmonium and bottomonium anchors. The calculated spectra saturate at $3.36$, $6.78$, and $8.19~{\rm GeV}$ in $D_s$, $B_s$, and $B_c$ families, while the RMS radii grow by several fm and mass gaps fall below $10~{\rm MeV}$. Mass-radius decoupling places the end of the safe radial ladder at $n_{\mathrm{safe}}\approx 4$--$6$ for $D_s$, $5$--$7$ for $B_s$, and $9$--$11$ for $B_c$, so the ladder terminates first in $D_s$, then in $B_s$, and last in $B_c$. Above the limiting-mass windows, at $3.49$, $6.86$, and $8.24~{\rm GeV}$, states of the radial ladder cannot be discovered. This termination is directly testable with forthcoming high-statistics data from BESIII, Belle II, and LHCb.

The flavour-singlet cases include, as already obvious from the present partial results, analytical structures not seen in the splitting functions up to now. The numerical effects (and their uncertainties, in the singlet cases) of the four-loop contributions to the scale derivatives amount to about $1\%$ or less down to momentum fractions $x$ as low as about $10^{-4}$ at a standard benchmark point with $α_{\rm s} = 0.2$, with the exception of the total valence quark distribution.

Abstract: We investigate the twist-4 generalized transverse momentum dependent parton distributions (GTMDs) of $\bar{u}$ and $\bar{d}$ quarks inside the proton at zero skewness by adopting the overlap representation within the light-cone formalism. Using the light-cone wave functions of the proton derived from the baryon-meson fluctuation model in terms of the $|q\bar{q}B\rangle$ Fock states, we calculate the twist-4 GTMDs of $\bar{u}$ and $\bar{d}$ quarks. Additionally, the intricate relations between the GTMDs and the generalized parton distributions are also explored in our research. Numerical results for these twist-4 GTMDs and twist-4 GPDs at zero skewness are analyzed and presented.

Abstract: Super-leading logarithms from Glauber exchange complicate the resummation of non-global observables at hadron colliders. In this Letter, we extend Marzili, a Monte Carlo framework for non-global resummation based on effective field theory, to resum the leading nonzero colour contribution involving two Glauber exchanges. This is the first Monte Carlo resummation of super-leading logarithms. Together with Marzili's treatment of non-global logarithms, this opens a path towards studying their interplay and incorporating Glauber exchange into parton showers.

</details>


### [44] [Parameter degeneracy and information loss in inverse flavor mapping for high-energy astrophysical neutrinos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35132)
*Zi-Qiang Chen,Zhi-zhong Xing,Ye-Ling Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the inverse flavor mapping problem in high-energy astrophysical neutrinos, focusing on degeneracies and information loss when reconstructing source flavor ratios from observed ones due to neutrino oscillations — especially near the determinant-zero condition of the mixing matrix P. It analyzes parameter correlations, incorporates μ–τ reflection symmetry, derives constraints on observable vs. source flavor ratios, and applies the framework to IceCube data (5 TeV–10 PeV) under a common-source-flavor assumption.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the fundamental limitation in inferring astrophysical neutrino source flavor composition (η) from measured flavor ratios (f) at Earth due to oscillation-induced degeneracy and the ill-conditioned nature of the propagation matrix P, particularly when det P ≈ 0 — a scenario permitted by current oscillation data.

Method: Analytical derivation of the condition det P = 0 in terms of standard neutrino mixing parameters (θ₁₂, θ₁₃, θ₂₃, δ), including explicit treatment of μ–τ reflection symmetry; formulation of linear constraints linking f_e, f_μ to η_e, η_μ under this degeneracy; application of these constraints to IceCube’s all-sky high-energy neutrino data assuming uniform source flavor composition.

Result: Identification of precise correlation conditions for det P = 0, derivation of reduced-dimensional constraints (e.g., linear relations between f_e, f_μ and η_e, η_μ) that persist despite degeneracy, and quantitative illustration—using IceCube data—that only certain regions of source flavor space remain viable, with strong sensitivity to θ₂₃ octant and CP phase δ.

Conclusion: The inverse flavor mapping is fundamentally limited near det P = 0, but nontrivial, testable constraints on source flavor ratios survive; combining high-statistics flavor measurements with precise oscillation parameter knowledge (especially θ₂₃ and δ) can break degeneracies and improve astrophysical source diagnostics.

Abstract: Given a high-energy astrophysical neutrino flux, its source flavor ratios $η= \{η^{}_e, η^{}_μ, η^{}_τ\}$ are correlated with the ones $f = \{f^{}_e, f^{}_μ, f^{}_τ\}$ measured at a neutrino telescope via $f = P η$, where the elements of $P$ consist of four lepton flavor mixing parameters. But realistic {\it inverse} flavor mapping $η= P^{-1} f$ encounters unavoidable parameter degeneracy and information loss, especially in or near the $\det P = 0$ limit allowed by current neutrino oscillation data. In this work we explore the parameter correlation condition for $\det P = 0$ including the $μ$-$τ$ reflection symmetry case, formulate the corresponding constraints on $f^{}_e$ and $f^{}_μ$ versus $η^{}_e$ and $η^{}_μ$, and illustrate to what extent the source flavor distribution can be mapped by using the recent IceCube all-sky neutrino flux data ranging from 5 TeV to 10 PeV under the assumption that the relevant sources have a common flavor composition.

</details>


### [45] [The $Dπ$ and $D^*π$ femtoscopy puzzle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35133)
*Pablo Encarnación,Miguel Albaladejo,Albert Feijoo,Juan Nieves*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a relativistic momentum-space framework for femtoscopic correlations including strong and Coulomb interactions in coupled channels, applies it to Dπ and D*π systems, identifies inconsistencies in ALICE's experimental extraction due to improper high-momentum assumptions, and calls for reanalysis to establish femtoscopy as a quantitative tool.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies between theoretical femtoscopic predictions and ALICE experimental extractions for Dπ correlations, rooted in inadequate treatment of the low-energy coupled-channel dynamics and Coulomb asymptotics.

Result: Theoretical Dπ correlation functions disagree significantly with ALICE’s extracted ones; the discrepancy arises because ALICE assumes unity correlation above 100 MeV, whereas the lower D₀*(2300) pole still influences that region; extracted scattering parameters are highly window-dependent; similar issues appear in D*π due to heavy-quark spin symmetry; tension also persists in Kπ femtoscopy.

Conclusion: Current experimental extraction procedures for hadronic femtoscopic correlations lack robustness in coupled-channel, low-momentum regimes; a dedicated reanalysis of ALICE data—incorporating proper relativistic, coupled-channel, and Coulomb physics—is essential to validate femtoscopy as a precision tool for low-energy hadron interactions.

Abstract: We present a relativistic momentum-space framework for femtoscopic correlation functions including strong and Coulomb interactions in coupled channels. The approach extends the Vincent--Phatak prescription to the three-dimensional Bethe--Salpeter equation, combining a fully relativistic treatment with the correct Coulomb asymptotics through wave-function matching. We apply it to the $Dπ$ system using a coupled-channel heavy-meson chiral interaction that reproduces the established two-pole structure of the $D_0^*(2300)$. Our correlation functions agree with previous theoretical calculations but differ significantly from those extracted by the ALICE Collaboration. We trace this tension to the assumption made in the experimental analysis that the genuine correlation function reaches unity above a relative momentum of $100$ MeV, whereas this region remains affected by the lower $D_0^*(2300)$ pole. Repeating the extraction over different fitting windows reveals a strong dependence of the extracted signal on the chosen range, demonstrating that the resulting $Dπ$ scattering parameters are not robust and motivating a dedicated reanalysis of the experimental data. We extend the analysis to the $D^*π$ system, where the same issue arises in close connection with heavy-quark spin symmetry. Together with the unresolved tension between theoretical calculations and the ALICE $Kπ$ femtoscopic measurements, this highlights the need to clarify the origin of this puzzle, with the aim of establishing femtoscopy as a reliable quantitative tool for precision studies of low-energy hadron interactions.

</details>


### [46] [Perturbative QCD correction to the nonleptonic weak rate in dense quark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35165)
*Matti Heikinheimo,Hanna Lempiäinen,Risto Paatelainen,Tomi Ruosteoja,Kimmo Tuominen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the first leading-order QCD correction to the nonleptonic weak flavor-equilibration rate in dense quark matter using real-time thermal field theory and the Kadanoff-Baym equation; the result is gauge-invariant, finite, enhances the tree-level rate by 30–40% at low temperatures relevant to compact stars, and improves bulk-viscosity modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the accuracy of microscopic input for bulk-viscosity calculations in dense quark matter—specifically, to go beyond the tree-level nonleptonic weak flavor-equilibration rate used phenomenologically for over three decades.

Method: Uses the Kadanoff-Baym kinetic equation within real-time thermal field theory to compute the leading perturbative QCD correction; ensures gauge invariance and finiteness by explicitly canceling soft and collinear divergences between real and virtual corrections, in line with the Kinoshita-Lee-Nauenberg theorem.

Result: The leading QCD correction is fully finite and gauge invariant; in the low-temperature, high-density regime pertinent to compact-star dynamics, it enhances the tree-level equilibration rate by 30–40%.

Conclusion: The corrected flavor-equilibration rate provides a more accurate, first-principles input for transport calculations—especially bulk viscosity—in dense quark matter, advancing the theoretical foundation for neutron star and quark-star modeling.

Abstract: We present the first calculation of the leading perturbative quantum chromodynamics (QCD) correction to the nonleptonic weak flavor-equilibration rate in dense quark matter. The computation is based on the Kadanoff-Baym kinetic equation and performed using real-time thermal field theory. The resulting QCD correction is both gauge invariant and fully finite. In particular, all soft and collinear divergences cancel after combining real and virtual corrections, in accordance with the Kinoshita-Lee-Nauenberg theorem. In the low-temperature regime relevant to compact-star dynamics, the correction enhances the tree-level rate by $30 - 40\%$ over the high-density range where perturbative QCD is applicable. The corrected rate improves on the tree-level result used in phenomenological calculations for more than three decades, providing a more accurate microscopic input for bulk-viscosity calculations in dense quark matter.

</details>


### [47] [Purely flavon driven leptogenesis for exactly degenerate Dirac or Majorana neutrino mass matrix in the type-I seesaw model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35299)
*Yan Shao,Zhen-hua Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes flavon fields—introduced in flavor-symmetry-based neutrino mass models—as a natural solution to revive leptogenesis when conventional CP asymmetry vanishes due to degeneracy in the Dirac ($M_D$) or right-handed neutrino ($M_R$) mass matrices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Flavor symmetries explain observed neutrino mixing but often predict highly constrained mass structures (e.g., $M_D \propto I$ or $M_R \propto I$) that suppress CP asymmetry in standard leptogenesis, rendering it nonviable.

Method: The authors introduce flavon–RHN interactions for $M_D \propto I$, enabling two-body decays $N_I \to N_J \phi$ with new CP-violating contributions; for $M_R \propto I$, they employ three-body decays $N_I \to L_\alpha H \phi$ mediated by heavy vectorlike fermions to generate CP asymmetry despite exact RHN mass degeneracy.

Result: Flavon dynamics restore viable leptogenesis in both degenerate scenarios, establishing a direct link between neutrino flavor structure (encoded in flavons) and baryogenesis via leptogenesis.

Conclusion: The flavon sector, already required for neutrino mass generation in flavor-symmetry models, naturally enables leptogenesis even under otherwise fatal degeneracy conditions—unifying neutrino phenomenology and baryon asymmetry generation.

Abstract: While the type-I seesaw model provides an attractive framework for simultaneously explaining the origin of neutrino masses and the baryon asymmetry of the Universe, flavor symmetries offer a natural approach to understanding the observed neutrino mixing pattern. However, the highly constrained neutrino mass structures predicted by flavor symmetries may also prevent conventional leptogenesis to work. In particular, when the Dirac neutrino mass matrix $M^{}_{\rm D}$ or the Majorana right-handed-neutrino (RHN) mass matrix $M^{}_{\rm R}$ is exactly degenerate (namely, they are proportional to the identity matrix $I$), the CP asymmetry of RHN decays will vanish either due to the orthogonality of different neutrino Yukawa coupling columns or the exact degeneracy of RHN masses. In this work, we propose that the flavon fields (which are inherently present in flavor-symmetry neutrino mass models to be responsible for generating the nontrivial neutrino flavor structure) can naturally overcome these obstacles. For the case of $M^{}_{\rm D} \propto I$, flavon--RHN interactions induce additional decay channels $N_I \to N_J φ$ and generate new CP-violating contributions to RHN decays. For the case of $M^{}_{\rm R} \propto I$, three-body decays $N_I\rightarrow L^{}_αHφ$ mediated by heavy vectorlike fermions provide a CP asymmetry source even when the RHN masses are exactly degenerate. Our results demonstrate that the flavon sector provides a natural connection between neutrino flavor structure and leptogenesis, offering a new mechanism for leptogenesis in flavor-symmetry-based neutrino mass models.

</details>


### [48] [System-size dependence of jet quenching from light to heavy-ion collisions at LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35321)

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies single-inclusive hadron suppression across O+O, Ne+Ne, Xe+Xe, and Pb+Pb collisions at LHC energies using an NLO pQCD framework with nuclear PDFs, higher-twist energy loss, and a temperature-dependent jet transport coefficient $\hat q/T^3$ calibrated via Bayesian analysis of RHIC/LHC data. It successfully describes $R_{AA}$ across all systems and centralities, and shows consistent system-size scaling—suggesting QGP formation even in small light-ion collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain jet quenching dynamics and parton energy loss mechanisms by investigating system size dependence of hadron suppression, motivated by recent LHC light-ion collision experiments (O+O, Ne+Ne).

Method: Next-to-leading-order perturbative QCD model incorporating nuclear-modified initial-state parton distribution functions (nPDFs), final-state parton energy loss via the higher-twist formalism, and a temperature-dependent jet transport coefficient $\hat q/T^3$ inferred from Bayesian analyses of prior RHIC and LHC $R_{AA}$ data.

Result: The model simultaneously reproduces measured $R_{AA}$ for charged hadrons across O+O, Ne+Ne, Xe+Xe, and Pb+Pb collisions over broad centrality ranges and in minimum-bias events; the ratios $R_{\mathrm{NeNe}}/R_{\mathrm{OO}}$ and $R_{AA}$ vs. $\langle N_{\mathrm{coll}} \rangle$ exhibit universal scaling at fixed $p_T$, indicating QGP formation even in smallest systems.

Conclusion: Systematic hadron suppression across varying nuclear sizes supports coherent jet quenching behavior governed by a universal, temperature-dependent $\hat q$, implying that QGP is formed not only in large (Pb+Pb) but also in small (O+O, Ne+Ne) collision systems at LHC energies.

Abstract: Single inclusive hadron suppression in high-energy heavy-ion collisions provides a sensitive probe of parton energy loss and jet transport coefficient in quark-gluon plasma (QGP). System size dependence of jet quenching can provide further constraints on the dynamics of jet quenching and parton energy loss. Motivated by recent experiments on light-ion collisions at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC), we perform a systematic study of single-inclusive hadron suppression in O+O, Ne+Ne, Xe+Xe and Pb+Pb collisions at the LHC energies. The calculations are carried out within the next-to-leading-order perturbative QCD model, incorporating the nuclear modified initial-state parton distributions, final-state parton energy loss in the higher-twist approach and the temperature dependence of the jet transport coefficient $\hat q/T^3$ from the Bayesian analyses of previous experimental data at the Relativistic Heavy-ion Collider (RHIC) and LHC. Our model calculations can simultaneously describe the measured single-inclusive charged hadron $R_{AA}$ in O+O, Ne+Ne, Xe+Xe and Pb+Pb collisions over a broad range of event centralities and in the minimum-bias events. We further illustrate the system size dependence of jet quenching through the ratio $R_{\mathrm{NeNe}}/R_{\mathrm{OO}}$ and the modification factors $R_{AA}$ as a function of $\langle N_{\mathrm{coll}} \rangle$ which follow a common behavior at fixed $p_{\rm T}$, indicating the formation of QGP even in the smallest collision systems.

</details>


### [49] [Resummation-scale uncertainties in PDF determinations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35344)
*Hamed Abdolmaleki,Valerio Bertone,Giuseppe Bozzi,Francesco Giuli,Alexander Glazov,Valentina Guglielmi,Francesco Hautmann,Fred Olness,Pavel Starovoitov,Oleksandr Zenaiev*

  <summary>Details</summary>

Abstract: We investigate the impact of resummation-scale variations on parton distribution function (PDF) determinations. These variations introduce an additional source of theoretical uncertainty related to the evolution of PDFs and strong coupling $α_s$ that has never previously been accounted for in PDF fits. Using the open-source xFitter framework, we quantify this uncertainty in fits to HERA deep-inelastic scattering data and examine its implications for predictions of top-quark pair production at the LHC. We show that the resulting resummation-scale uncertainties have a sizable impact on LHC observables, highlighting the importance of including this source of uncertainty in precision QCD analyses.

</details>

### [51] [Exploring $U_A(1)$ symmetry restoration in a magnetic field by meson screening masses in the NJL model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35468)

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies axial $U_A(1)$ symmetry restoration in a magnetic field at finite temperature using a lattice-improved (2+1)-flavor NJL model, finding axial magnetic catalysis at low T and axial inverse magnetic catalysis near the chiral crossover—yet all three axial order parameters yield decreasing pseudo-critical temperatures with increasing magnetic field.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Conclusion: The decrease of axial pseudo-critical temperature with magnetic field is robust across distinct order parameters—even when some observables (e.g., transverse $m_{\text{scr}}$ difference) increase with $eB$—indicating that axial symmetry restoration is generally facilitated by magnetic fields, albeit through non-uniform dynamical mechanisms.

Abstract: We investigate the effective axial $U_A(1)$ symmetry restoration at finite temperature in an external magnetic field using meson screening masses, as well as meson susceptibilities, in a $(2+1)$-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model. To incorporate inverse magnetic catalysis, we employ a magnetic-field-dependent four-quark coupling constrained by the lattice QCD chiral pseudo-critical temperature, with the 't Hooft coupling fixed. In the lattice-improved NJL model, the longitudinal screening mass difference between the $U_A(1)$ partners $δ^0$ and $π^0$ and their normalized meson susceptibility difference exhibit axial magnetic catalysis at low temperatures and axial inverse magnetic catalysis near the chiral crossover, whereas the transverse screening mass difference increases with $eB$ at all temperatures. Despite these different responses, the axial pseudo-critical temperatures extracted from the thermal inflection points of these three alternative order parameters decrease with $eB$, implying that a decreasing pseudo-critical temperature can coexist with an increasing transverse screening mass difference at fixed temperature.

</details>


### [52] [Magnetic thresholds and spin-resolved spectral moments of $φ$ and $K^{*0}$ in a hadronic model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35503)
*Lang Yu,Tao-Tao Sui,Xinyang Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates spin-resolved spectral functions of φ and K*0 mesons in a thermal hadronic medium under a constant magnetic field, using a retarded propagator formalism incorporating vacuum matching, Landau quantization, and thermal scattering; it identifies narrow eV-scale threshold peaks, analyzes their sensitivity to spectral width and windowing, and quantifies field-dependent responses relevant for spin alignment measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how magnetic fields modify spin-resolved spectral structures of vector mesons (φ and K*0) in hot hadronic matter—particularly the impact of Landau-level-induced thresholds on spectral moments relevant for experimental spin alignment observables.

Method: Constructs rest-frame retarded propagators by combining real-vacuum matching, exact Landau level quantization, connection contacts, and thermal scattering terms; validates against collision kernels and known limits; computes spectral functions, analyzes threshold peak areas via direct kernel integration and smooth-weighted moments; studies effects of finite spectral width on zero crossings of Re[1/D] and integrated weight; employs weighted identities with bounded Taylor remainders to evaluate window-response under endpoint replacements.

Result: Identifies narrow, eV-scale peaks from closed transverse thresholds; peak area is well-approximated by leading threshold estimate; finite spectral width suppresses zeros of Re[1/D] when spread exceeds detuning, while integrated weight remains stable; smooth-weighted response for φ at low fields is negative and ∝ b²; hard-window response oscillates; pseudoscalar mixing alters smooth-response coefficient.

Conclusion: Spectral projection details—especially threshold resolution, spectral width, and weighting scheme—are critical for interpreting spin-resolved observables in strong magnetic fields; current results quantify baseline sensitivity but require incorporation of microscopic daughter widths and experimentally matched source conditions for quantitative predictions of spin alignment.

Abstract: We study the spin-resolved spectral functions of $φ$ and $K^{*0}$ in a thermal hadronic model with a constant magnetic field. We construct the rest-frame retarded propagators by combining real vacuum matching with exact Landau levels, connection contacts, and thermal scattering terms. The resulting stable-daughter calculation is checked against independent collision kernels and published expressions in common limits. We find that closed transverse thresholds give rise to narrow peaks with eV-scale detunings, whose areas must be resolved in the calculation of small spin spectral moments. We test the leading threshold estimate for the peak area by an independent integration of the full kernel. Using positive compact daughter spectra, we then investigate how a finite spectral spread changes the local threshold structure. The disappearance of zeros of the real part of the inverse propagator is controlled by the spread relative to the detuning, whereas the weight integrated over a finite patch can remain nearly unchanged. We relate this redistribution to smooth accepted moments through a weighted identity with a bounded Taylor remainder, and evaluate the complete-window response to three endpoint replacements, including the patch complement. For the stable-daughter $φ$ spectra with a fixed smooth weight, the response at the two smallest sampled fields is negative and compatible with $b^2$ scaling. Hard-window results instead oscillate in sign, and pseudoscalar mixing substantially changes the smooth response coefficient. These results quantify the sensitivity to thresholds and spectral projection; microscopic daughter widths and source-matched experimental spin alignment require additional input.

</details>


### [53] [Heavy Quark Recombination Across Collision Systems: Meson and Baryon production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35546)
*Vincenzo Minissale,Salvatore Plumari,Maria Lucia Sambataro,Gloria Calà,Vincenzo Greco*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews and extends the coalescence-plus-fragmentation framework for heavy-flavor hadronization in QCD matter, emphasizing its success in explaining baryon/meson ratios (e.g., Λc/D⁰) in AA and pp collisions, and presents new predictions for multi-charmed (Ξcc, Ωccc) and beauty baryons (Λb/B⁰, etc.), highlighting their sensitivity to charm/bottom thermalization and enhanced recombination effects—especially for heavier quarks—and outlines ongoing efforts to unify open and hidden heavy-flavor descriptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address limitations of vacuum fragmentation in describing heavy-flavor baryon enhancement (e.g., Λc/D⁰, Ωccc/D⁰) observed at RHIC/LHC, and to probe QGP properties—particularly charm/bottom thermalization and non-equilibrium dynamics—via recombination-sensitive observables.

Method: Extends the coalescence-plus-fragmentation model to predict yields of multi-charmed (Ξcc, Ωccc) and beauty baryons (Λb, Ξb, Ωb) across collision systems (PbPb, KrKr, ArAr, OO, pp); incorporates charm/bottom phase-space distributions from transport or hydrodynamic simulations; applies same framework to explore quarkonium production in small systems.

Result: Predicts strong system-size and mass dependence of multi-charmed baryon yields—especially Ωccc—with high sensitivity to charm thermalization; finds larger baryon enhancement and stronger recombination effects for beauty than charm; shows feasibility of unifying open and hidden heavy flavor via coalescence in small systems.

Conclusion: Coalescence is essential for heavy-flavor hadronization across system sizes and quark masses; multi-charmed and beauty baryons serve as precision probes of QGP transport properties; a unified coalescence framework bridges open and hidden heavy flavor, advancing understanding of parton dynamics in strongly coupled QCD matter.

Abstract: Measurements of heavy-flavor baryons in nucleus--nucleus and proton--proton collisions have provided strong evidence that heavy-quark hadronization cannot be described solely in terms of vacuum fragmentation. In particular, the large enhancement of the $Λ_c/D^0$, $Ξ_c/D^0$ and $Ω_c/D^0$ ratios observed at RHIC and LHC energies has been successfully described within a coalescence plus fragmentation approach, supporting the relevance of recombination mechanisms in both large and small collision systems. Building on these results, we discuss recent developments in the coalescence hadronization framework. Particular emphasis is given to the production of multi-charmed baryons, namely $Ξ_{cc}$, and $Ω_{ccc}$, whose yields are predicted in a wide range of collision systems from PbPb to KrKr, ArAr and OO collisions. The strong sensitivity of these states, especially $Ω_{ccc}$, to the underlying charm-quark distribution makes them valuable probes of charm thermalization and non-equilibrium effects in the QGP. We also present recent extensions of the model to the beauty sector, including predictions for $Λ_b/B^0$, $Ξ_b/B^0$ and $Ω_b/B^0$ production. The results indicate sizeable recombination effects and a stronger baryon enhancement than in the charm sector, highlighting the increasing role of coalescence for heavier quarks. Finally, we briefly comment on ongoing developments toward a unified description of open and hidden heavy flavor through the application of the same framework to quarkonium production in small collision systems.

</details>


### [54] [The LZ event with a two-state dark matter halo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35650)
*Vinicius Oliveira*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reanalyzes the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment's single nuclear-recoil candidate at ~248 keV in the context of pseudo-Dirac dark matter, where an excited state χ₂ may constitute a non-negligible fraction f of the dark matter halo; it shows that for f ≳ 10⁻⁵–10⁻², exothermic χ₂N → χ₁N scattering dominates, drastically reducing the required cross section (by up to 1.6×10⁴) and eliminating the kinematic cutoff of the endothermic signal—rendering pure χ₁-only interpretations inconsistent with limits when f = 1/2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The LZ experiment’s single nuclear-recoil event was interpreted as inelastic scattering of ground-state dark matter (χ₁N → χ₂N), but pseudo-Dirac models allow a cosmologically stable excited state χ₂, enabling exothermic scattering (χ₂N → χ₁N); standard LZ statistical analysis does not account for such a mixed-halo scenario, necessitating a revised inference framework.

Method: The authors reconstruct and validate LZ’s statistical inference procedure against published exclusion intervals, then extend it to a two-component dark matter halo with fractional abundance f of χ₂; they compute expected event spectra, likelihoods, and confidence intervals for combined endothermic (χ₁→χ₂) and exothermic (χ₂→χ₁) channels across δ = 250–350 keV mass splittings.

Result: For f ≳ 10⁻⁵–10⁻² (depending on δ), exothermic scattering dominates the signal; at f = 1/2, the cross section needed to explain the event drops by factors of 80–1.6×10⁴ for δ = 250–350 keV, and the endothermic kinematic cutoff vanishes; a benchmark model fitting the event with χ₁ alone is excluded by a factor of ~30 when f = 1/2.

Conclusion: The LZ single-event interpretation is highly sensitive to the dark matter halo composition; assuming a pure χ₁ halo is unjustified in pseudo-Dirac scenarios—exothermic contributions from χ₂ can dramatically relax cross-section constraints and invalidate standard exclusions, urging reinterpretation of low-statistics direct detection signals in multi-state DM frameworks.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment has reported a single nuclear-recoil candidate at $E_R \simeq 248$keV. LZ interprets it in terms of inelastic dark matter that scatters endothermically from its ground state, $χ_1 N \to χ_2 N$. In pseudo-Dirac models the excited state $χ_2$ can survive to the present day, and exothermic scattering, $χ_2 N \to χ_1 N$, is then also open. The published LZ intervals do not describe this case. We rebuild the LZ statistical inference and validate it against the published intervals. We then apply it to a halo that contains a fraction $f$ of $χ_2$. For $f \gtrsim 10^{-5}$--$10^{-2}$, depending on the mass splitting, the exothermic contribution dominates. For $f=1/2$ the cross section required by the candidate drops by factors of $80$ to $1.6\times10^4$ for $δ= 250$--$350$~keV, and the kinematic cutoff of the endothermic signal disappears. A benchmark that explains the event with $χ_1$ alone lies a factor of $30$ above the upper limit when half of the halo is $χ_2$.

</details>


### [55] [Factorization and virtuality evolution of jet functions in heavy-ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35653)
*Carlos Lamas,Carlos A. Salgado,Bin Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a virtuality-resolved framework for jet propagation in heavy-ion collisions, extending the BDMPS-Z formalism to incorporate virtuality-differential transverse momentum broadening and medium-induced gluon emission; it identifies the ratio of medium-to-jet formation times as the key control parameter for initial virtuality contributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the role of virtuality in jet evolution inside quark-gluon plasma, bridging vacuum-like and medium-induced dynamics beyond integrated (coarse-grained) descriptions.

Method: Derives a factorized jet cross section defining an in-medium jet function; incorporates virtuality as a resolved scale in perturbative QCD; analyzes transverse momentum broadening and soft gluon spectra for finite and zero medium formation time τ₀.

Result: Recovery of standard BDMPS-Z results upon full virtuality integration; emergence of virtuality-dependent interplay between vacuum evolution and medium effects; identification of τ₀ / t_jet as the critical ratio governing relevant initial virtualities.

Conclusion: Virtuality resolution provides a more complete, scale-separated description of jet quenching, enabling systematic matching between vacuum showering and in-medium energy loss, with implications for precision jet tomography in heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: We develop a new framework to perform a virtuality-resolved description of jet propagation in heavy-ion collisions using perturbative techniques. The role of virtuality is rigorously identified by first deriving a factorized jet cross section that defines an in-medium jet function. This new definition allows to extend the usual BDMPS-Z formalism to include the virtuality-differential transverse momentum broadening distribution and medium-induced soft gluon spectrum. We study both the cases of a medium which is created with some delay time $τ_0$ after the hard collision as well as the case in which $τ_0\to 0$. Integration over the complete virtuality range recovers the standard BDMPS-Z results, while finite virtuality reveals the interplay between vacuum-like evolution and medium-induced dynamics. We identify the ratio between the medium formation time and the jet formation time as the variable controlling which initial virtualities contribute appreciably to the jet function.

</details>


### [56] [GAIA meets LZ: a high velocity tail-tale sign for dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35737)
*Anirban Das,Subhabrata Majumdar,Manibrata Sen,Amogh Srivastav*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the inelastic dark matter interpretation of a high-energy nuclear recoil event (LZ230616) observed by LZ, using a Gaia-informed galactic velocity distribution—departing from the Standard Halo Model—and assesses implications for exothermic vs. endothermic scattering, including LMC-induced high-velocity tail effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-energy nuclear recoils in direct-detection experiments like LZ are sensitive to the high-velocity tail of the DM velocity distribution; standard assumptions (e.g., Standard Halo Model) may be inadequate given new astrophysical constraints from Gaia and LMC dynamics.

Method: The authors employ a Gaia-informed galactic model to derive a realistic DM velocity distribution (with lower escape speed, suppressed high-velocity tail, and higher local density), compute inelastic scattering rates for LZ230616 under exo-/endothermic scenarios, and augment the analysis with N-body simulations modeling gravitational perturbations from the Large Magellanic Cloud on the DM halo.

Result: Exothermic interpretations remain robust under the updated velocity distribution, whereas endothermic interpretations require larger cross sections and smaller mass splittings; inclusion of LMC-induced high-velocity tail extends viable endothermic parameter space toward lower DM masses and larger splittings.

Conclusion: Astrophysically motivated velocity distributions—especially those incorporating Gaia kinematics and LMC perturbations—qualitatively reshape particle-physics interpretations of high-energy recoil events, underscoring the necessity of joint analysis of direct detection and galactic dynamics.

Abstract: The interpretation of high-energy nuclear recoils in direct-detection experiments can depend sensitively on the high-velocity tail of the galactic dark matter (DM) distribution. The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment has recently reported an event, LZ230616, with nuclear recoil energy 248 $\pm$ 23 (stat) $\pm$ 23 (sys) keV, while the Gaia mission has provided an unprecedented view of the kinematics and mass distribution of the Milky Way. We bring these two probes together to investigate the inelastic DM interpretation of LZ230616, using a velocity distribution inferred from Gaia-informed galactic model. Compared to the Standard Halo Model, the resulting distribution has a lower escape speed and a significantly suppressed high-velocity tail, with a higher local density. We find that while the exothermic interpretation is largely insensitive to this change, the endothermic interpretation requires substantially larger scattering cross sections and smaller mass splittings. Furthermore, using simulation-based study of the gravitational effects of the Large Magellanic Cloud, we show that the high-velocity tail can extend the allowed endothermic parameter space towards lower DM masses and larger splittings. Our results demonstrate how combining direct searches for DM detection with precision measurements of galactic dynamics can qualitatively affect the particle-physics interpretation of high-energy nuclear recoils.

</details>


### [57] [Covariant Contrastive Learning for Uncertainty-Aware Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35755)
*Shelley Tong,Philip Harris,Gaia Grosso*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a likelihood-regularized contrastive embedding method that jointly learns physics-aware latent representations and a downstream classifier with nuisance parameters, enabling robust, interpretable anomaly detection under continuous systematic uncertainties in LHC data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Systematic uncertainty propagation in neural embeddings for anomaly detection is poorly understood, limiting real-world applicability in high-energy physics analyses like those at the LHC.

Method: The authors propose a joint training framework combining supervised contrastive learning for physics-aware embeddings with a parameterized downstream classification task, where continuous systematic uncertainties are incorporated as nuisance parameters via likelihood-based regularization.

Result: On simulated CMS Level-1 trigger data, the method successfully models systematic shifts in a 4D latent space using a linear parametric classifier, improving both interpretability and statistical robustness of anomaly detection.

Conclusion: The framework provides a general, scalable approach to modeling and controlling systematic uncertainties in latent spaces, enabling reliable unsupervised/supervised anomaly detection workflows for LHC and other HEP experiments.

Abstract: Machine-learning-based anomaly detection (AD) offers a promising, model-agnostic alternative to traditional LHC analyses, allowing to search for many signals at once. Recent advances in representation learning motivate the use of neural embeddings to map high-dimensional physics observables into low-dimensional latent spaces better suited to statistical inference. However, the propagation of systematic uncertainty in embedded spaces remains poorly understood, severely limiting the application of these methods to real LHC analyses. We address this gap with a machine learning strategy that uses a likelihood-based regularization to improve the structure of embedded spaces. Building on previous work using supervised contrastive learning for physics-aware embeddings, our approach trains them jointly with a downstream parameterized classification task that incorporates continuous uncertainties as nuisance parameters. This method returns a latent space that covaries predictably with systematic shifts, and a model of such distortions that can be exploited in the downstream statistical test for anomaly detection. Using simulated CMS Level-1 trigger data, we show that our method successfully models continuous uncertainties in a 4D latent space with a linear parametric downstream classifier, improving both the interpretability and robustness of the statistical anomaly detection task. This framework offers a general way to study and control systematic uncertainties in latent spaces, opening the way to a new scalable statistical analysis workflow for anomaly detection at the LHC, and other high energy physics experiments.

</details>
