### [58] [Locality of KMS states for infinite fermionic lattice systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31858)
*Lennart Becker*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes stability of thermal equilibrium (KMS) states of infinite fermionic lattices under extensive Hamiltonian perturbations, proving that exponential decay of correlations and local indistinguishability imply stretched-exponential decay in the perturbed state; it introduces a C*-algebraic formulation of Quantum Belief Propagation and unifies key locality properties via Lieb–Robinson bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish stability of KMS states—especially in non-unique regimes (e.g., phase coexistence)—against extensive perturbations, bridging locality concepts (correlation decay, local response, indistinguishability) without assuming uniqueness or translation invariance.

Method: Develops Quantum Belief Propagation directly on the $C^*$-algebra of the fermionic lattice; combines it with Lieb–Robinson bounds to derive equivalences among correlation decay, local perturbation response ('local perturbations perturb locally'), and local indistinguishability; applies uniform clustering and support conditions for extensive perturbations.

Result: 1) Stretched-exponential decay of correlations for the perturbed KMS state, given exponential decay and exponential local indistinguishability of the unperturbed state; 2) A differentiable path of local equilibrium expectations under extensive perturbations, with linear bound on change in terms of interaction norm; 3) Closed equivalence cycle among three locality properties under uniform clustering.

Conclusion: Thermal equilibrium states of infinite fermionic systems are stable under extensive perturbations when initial locality conditions hold—even without uniqueness—providing a robust, algebraic framework for quantum statistical mechanics beyond mean-field or gapped assumptions.

Abstract: We study how thermal equilibrium states of infinite fermionic lattices respond to perturbations of the Hamiltonian. Our main result concerns extensive perturbations of a chosen KMS state. We show that exponential decay of correlations and exponential local indistinguishability under the extensive perturbation yield stretched-exponential decay of correlations for the perturbed state. Neither the initial nor the perturbed state is required to be unique, allowing stability to be studied in the presence of phase coexistence. Our approach uses Quantum Belief Propagation (QBP), which we establish directly on the underlying $C^*$-algebra. Combining QBP with Lieb--Robinson bounds, we show implications between decay of correlations, the principle that local perturbations perturb locally, and local indistinguishability, closing the cycle for perturbations of the tracial state under the stated decay, support and uniformity assumptions. For extensive perturbations under uniform clustering conditions, we also obtain a differentiable path of local equilibrium expectations and bound their change linearly in the interaction norm of the perturbation.

</details>


### [59] [Local Determinant Defects and Low-Energy Subspace Stability in Semifinite von Neumann Algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32095)
*Seyed Mahmoud Manjegani*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a finite-temperature stability test for low-energy spectral subspaces using a local determinant defect of the alpha-z Rényi kernel between two Gibbs states, bounding their tracial L² distance; it relies on a spectral-flattening principle in semifinite von Neumann algebras, applies to trace-class Gibbs operators without requiring Hamiltonian proximity in operator norm, and yields exact ground-state overlap for qubits, principal-angle remainders for matrices, and uniform error bounds for effective low-energy models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a robust, finite-temperature criterion for stability of low-energy spectral subspaces—especially when Hamiltonians are not close in operator norm—enabling rigorous analysis of thermal perturbations in quantum many-body systems and effective low-energy models.

Method: Develops a spectral-flattening principle for bounded positive injective operators in semifinite von Neumann algebras; uses the local determinant defect of the alpha-z Rényi kernel between two Gibbs states; derives bounds on the tracial L² distance between low-energy projections; proves determinant inequalities, equality cases, and z-monotonicity across matrix, finite, and semifinite settings.

Result: A finite-temperature stability bound for low-energy subspaces expressed via the local determinant defect; exact ground-state overlap formula for qubits; sum-of-principal-angle remainders for finite-dimensional matrices; uniform error bound for bounded observables in effective low-energy models; extension to unbounded Hamiltonians with trace-class Gibbs operators; validity without operator-norm closeness of Hamiltonians.

Conclusion: The local determinant defect of the alpha-z Rényi kernel provides a sharp, physically meaningful finite-temperature stability criterion for low-energy spectral subspaces, generalizing beyond norm-based perturbation theory and enabling rigorous analysis in quantum statistical mechanics and quantum information.

Abstract: We give a finite-temperature test for the stability of low-energy spectral subspaces. The test uses a local determinant defect for the alpha-z Renyi kernel of two Gibbs states and bounds the tracial L2 distance between their low-energy projections. The main tool is a spectral-flattening principle for bounded positive injective operators in a semifinite von Neumann algebra. It reduces the local product defect to an exact two-projection formula. The thermal result applies to lower-bounded affiliated Hamiltonians with trace-class Gibbs operators and does not require the Hamiltonians to be close in operator norm. We give an infinite-level example with unbounded Hamiltonians. For matrices, the result gives a sum of principal-angle remainders. For qubits, the defect and the thermal gaps determine the ground-state overlap exactly. The same estimate gives a uniform error bound for bounded observables in an effective low-energy model. We also prove the determinant inequalities, their equality cases, and z-monotonicity in matrix, finite, and semifinite settings.

</details>


### [60] [The Hamilton--Jacobi and Symplectic Analysis for Extended Hořava Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32218)
*Diana Vanessa Castro-Luna,Alberto Escalante*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs Hamilton-Jacobi and Faddeev-Jackiw analyses of a modified linearized non-projectable λR gravity model, including BPS and Cotton-squared terms; it derives full constraint structure, generalized brackets, gauge symmetries, and quantum path integral measure, highlighting critical differences at λ = 1/3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the constrained structure, gauge symmetry, and quantum consistency of a modified Hořava–Lifshitz-like gravity model—specifically the linearized non-projectable λR theory extended with Blas-Pujolàs-Sibiryakov (BPS) and Cotton tensor squared terms—by comparing two complementary canonical approaches (HJ and FJ) and identifying the role of the coupling λ.

Method: Hamilton-Jacobi analysis: derivation of complete set of Hamiltonians, generalized (Dirac-like) brackets, fundamental differential, characteristic equations, and gauge transformations. Faddeev-Jackiw analysis: construction of symplectic 2-form (symplectic tensor), identification of fundamental brackets from its inverse, and computation of the path integral measure via its Pfaffian. Both frameworks are applied separately to cases λ ≠ 1/3 and λ = 1/3.

Result: The HJ and FJ fundamental brackets coincide; a consistent symplectic structure is obtained; the quantum path integral measure is explicitly derived from the symplectic tensor; the case λ = 1/3 exhibits distinct constraint counting and altered measure due to enhanced degeneracy in the symplectic form.

Conclusion: The equivalence of HJ and FJ formalisms confirms the robustness of the constraint and gauge structure; the λ = 1/3 point is a critical value where the theory’s degrees of freedom and quantum measure change qualitatively, signaling a structural transition in the phase space geometry.

Abstract: The Hamilton-Jacobi [HJ] analysis and the Faddeev-Jackiw [FJ] quantization for the linearized, non-projectable $λR$ model, extended by the lowest-order term of Blas, Pujolàs, and Sibiryakov and the higher-order term involving the square of the Cotton tensor, were carried out. The cases $λ\neq \frac{1}{3}$ and $λ= \frac{1}{3}$ are analyzed. Within the Hamilton-Jacobi framework, we obtain the complete set of Hamiltonians, generalized brackets, fundamental differential, characteristic equations, and gauge transformations of the theory. In the Faddeev-Jackiw framework, a symplectic tensor is constructed from which the fundamental brackets are identified. The HJ and FJ brackets coincide, and from the symplectic tensor, the measure for the quantum path integral is calculated. The differences between the cases $λ\neq \frac{1}{3}$ and $λ= \frac{1}{3}$ at the quantum path integral are discussed.

</details>


### [61] [Absolute and relative invariants for low-dimensional topological insulators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32442)
*Federico Manzoni,Domenico Monaco,Gabriele Peluso*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies projection-valued maps on 1D and 2D tori under AZC symmetries, comparing Murray-von Neumann, unitary, and homotopy equivalences; it identifies absolute invariants (Chern number ℤ, Fu-Kane-Mele ℤ₂) for the first two, and relative homotopy invariants in minimal Hilbert spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivated by topological insulators, the paper seeks a unified classification of gapped quantum systems via symmetric projection-valued maps on low-dimensional tori under Altland-Zirnbauer-Cartan (AZC) symmetries.

Method: Constructs symmetric bases for projection ranges under pseudo-periodicity constraints; analyzes phase holonomies around torus cycles; distinguishes equivalence relations using operator-algebraic (Murray-von Neumann), unitary, and homotopy-theoretic tools; examines dependence on ambient Hilbert space dimension.

Result: For d=1: all AZC classes admit fully periodic symmetric bases → no topological obstruction. For d=2: topological obstructions yield absolute ℤ (Chern) or ℤ₂ (Fu-Kane-Mele) invariants classifying Murray-von Neumann and unitary equivalence; homotopy equivalence yields relative invariants only in minimal ambient spaces, sensitive to unitary conjugation.

Conclusion: Provides a geometric unification of topological classification for gapped quantum systems in d ≤ 2: absolute invariants govern algebraic/unitary equivalence, while relative homotopy invariants emerge only in minimal Hilbert spaces, reflecting physically meaningful phase differences between maps.

Abstract: [An extended abstract is provided in-text]
  Motivated by topological insulators, this paper analyzes classification schemes for projection-valued maps on $d$-dimensional tori, $d \le 2$, subject to Altland-Zirnbauer-Cartan (AZC) symmetries (time-reversal, particle-hole, and chiral). The main objective is to compare three notions of equivalence for these maps: Murray-von Neumann, unitary, and homotopy equivalence. The analysis relies on constructing symmetric bases for the projection ranges under a pseudo-periodicity constraint, where select basis vectors pick up a phase when looped around the torus. If $d=1$, fully periodic symmetric bases exist across all AZC classes. If $d=2$, topological obstructions appear as non-trivial phases characterized by $\mathbb{Z}$ (Chern number) or $\mathbb{Z}_2$ (Fu-Kane-Mele) invariants. These serve as absolute invariants that classify both Murray-von Neumann and unitary equivalences. For homotopy equivalence, the behavior depends on the ambient Hilbert space: if this has non-minimal dimension, homotopy equivalence reduces directly to unitary equivalence, while in minimal ambient spaces additional relative homotopy invariants emerge. Their precise values change under unitary conjugation, leaving only relative topological phases between maps well-defined. Ultimately, this provides a unified geometric framework for gapped quantum systems in low dimensions, demonstrating how distinct equivalence relations generate specific absolute and relative topological invariants.

</details>


### [62] [Poisson eigenvalue statistics and dynamical delocalization for fractional and long-range Anderson models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33084)
*Peter D. Hislop,Rodrigo Matos,Constanza Rojas-Molina*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that long-range Anderson models (including the fractional Anderson model) in the strong disorder regime and for β > d/2 exhibit Poisson local eigenvalue statistics and dynamical delocalization — i.e., absence of dynamical localization — contrary to the standard nearest-neighbor Anderson model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the spectral and dynamical behavior of long-range random operators, particularly testing the conjecture by Disertori et al. that such models may not localize dynamically despite strong disorder, unlike short-range Anderson models.

Method: Analysis of fractional moments of Green's functions; derivation of sharp lower bounds on these moments; use of eigenvector decay and spectral rigidity to establish Poisson statistics; application of moment methods to prove absence of dynamical localization.

Result: For β > d/2 and strong disorder: (i) local eigenvalue statistics are Poisson with intensity n(E); (ii) dynamical localization fails — large moments of position operator diverge; (iii) specifically, in d = 1, the fractional Anderson model with α ∈ (1/2, 1) shows Poisson statistics and dynamical delocalization.

Conclusion: Long-range hopping fundamentally alters localization behavior: strong disorder no longer implies dynamical localization, and eigenvalue statistics become uncorrelated (Poisson), distinguishing these models from the conventional Anderson model.

Abstract: We study local eigenvalue statistics and dynamical (de)-localization properties for long-range Anderson models, including the fractional Anderson model. These systems are random Anderson-type perturbations of operators with long-range (non random) hopping terms of the form $|T(n,m)| \sim \|n-m\|^{-(d+2β)}$ for $β>0$, on the $d$-dimensional lattice. In the presence of a strong enough random potential, these models exhibit dense pure point spectrum with polynomially decaying eigenvectors, almost surely. We show that in this strong disorder regime, and for $β>d/2$, the local eigenvalue statistics centered at any $E$ in the deterministic spectrum is a Poisson point process with intensity given by the density of states function $n(E)$. Moreover, we prove that, at strong disorder, there is no dynamical localization for these models, verifying a conjecture of Disertori et al. We achieve this by establishing explicit, sharp lower bounds on the Green's functions fractional moments which imply that large moments of the position operator are infinite. Hence, these models exhibit Poisson eigenvalue statistics and dynamical delocalization, that is, the absence of dynamical localization. In particular, this shows that in dimension $d=1$, the fractional Anderson model, a random perturbation of the fractional Laplacian $(-Δ)^α$ with $α\in (0,1)$, exhibits Poisson eigenvalue statistics and dynamical delocalization if the disorder is strong and the exponent $\frac{1}{2}<α<1$. This is in stark contrast with what is known for the usual Anderson model that has only nearest-neighbor hopping.

</details>


### [63] [Minimal output entropy for the channels that add or remove a box of a Young diagram, and a Pauli principle for every permutation symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33498)
*Robin Reuvers*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes quantum channels connecting irreducible representations of U(d) differing by a single box in their Young diagrams, proving coherent states minimize output entropy via majorization and computing optimal outputs using hook lengths; it also derives sharp spectral bounds for single-particle reduced density matrices under permutation symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize entropy minimization and spectral constraints for quantum channels arising from representation-theoretic transitions in U(d), with applications to symmetric multi-particle systems and reduced state spectra.

Method: Representation-theoretic analysis of U(d) irreps via Young diagrams; majorization theory applied to quantum channel outputs; explicit computation of optimal outputs using hook-length formulas; spectral analysis of reduced density matrices under permutation symmetry.

Result: Coherent states majorize all other outputs for four specific covariant quantum channels, minimizing output entropy; optimal outputs are explicitly given in terms of hook lengths; full characterization of entropy minimizers (including non-coherent pure states) for two channels; sharp spectral bounds and exact reachable spectra for single-particle reduced density matrices of N-particle symmetric/antisymmetric states.

Conclusion: Coherent states universally minimize output entropy for these representation-linked quantum channels, and the spectral constraints for reduced states under permutation symmetry are fully determined—recovering and extending the Pauli principle and Coleman's theorem for fermions.

Abstract: Two irreducible representations of $U(d)$ whose Young diagrams differ by a single box are connected by four covariant quantum channels: the box can be removed or added, and one keeps either the new diagram or the box ($\mathbb{C}^d$ or its dual). We prove that for each of these channels the output of a coherent state majorizes every other output, so that coherent states minimize the output entropy, and we compute the optimal output explicitly in terms of hook lengths. For the two channels that keep the box we also determine all minimizers; these need not be coherent, even when they are pure. As an application we consider $N$ particles with a given permutation symmetry and find sharp bounds on the spectrum of the reduced density matrix of a single particle, as well as the exact set of spectra that mixed states can reach (for fermions these are the Pauli principle and Coleman's theorem).

</details>


### [64] [Effective atom-dimer bands of three-body lattice systems at strong coupling: exact critical mass ratios and a statistics-quasimomentum duality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33576)
*Mikhail V. Dolgopolov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes three-particle contact interactions on the square lattice across the full Brillouin zone, deriving exact effective Hamiltonians via Feshbach-Schur reduction, solving the atom-dimer threshold problem via lattice resistance-based 4×4 Hermitian criteria, and establishing rigorous onset laws, dualities, critical mass ratios (including exact value π/(π−2) at zero quasimomentum), and renormalization-group interpretations of binding and zone boundaries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend previous studies—limited to trimers and zero quasimomentum—to the full Brillouin zone and general statistics (bosonic, mixed fermionic/bosonic), resolving the atom-dimer threshold problem rigorously and uncovering universal scaling laws and dualities in lattice few-body physics.

Method: Feshbach-Schur reduction onto the unit eigenvalue subspace of the pair-contact operator; derivation of explicit effective Hamiltonians with proved first-order error; reduction of threshold condition to a 4×4 Hermitian matrix criterion built from two-point resistances of the square lattice; algebraic solution via quartic polynomial; independent verification using lattice quadrature, computer algebra, exact diagonalization, and infinite-lattice solvers.

Result: Complete analytical characterization of the atom-dimer zone and critical mass ratio across the Brillouin zone; closed-form expressions for zones and thresholds at all symmetry points; exact critical mass ratio π/(π−2) at zero quasimomentum; proof of at most two atom-dimer bound states per (quasimomentum, mass ratio); onset laws featuring essential singularity at zone boundary and linear-over-logarithm behavior in pure p-wave; two exact statistics–quasimomentum dualities; global determination of second fermionic threshold; RG interpretation identifying inverse lattice Green function as marginal s-wave coupling and zone boundary as marginal (BCS/Kondo) type.

Conclusion: The three-particle contact problem on the square lattice is fully solvable analytically across the Brillouin zone for all statistics; the atom-dimer threshold is governed by lattice resistance geometry and admits a renormalization-group structure where binding arises via dimensional transmutation, with implications for universality beyond lattice models.

Abstract: We study three particles on the square lattice with contact interactions of infinite strength, for all total quasimomenta, for three statistics: three identical bosons, two identical fermions plus a third particle, and two identical bosons plus a third particle. Previous work treated only the trimer and zero quasimomentum. Here the atom-dimer sector is analyzed on the whole Brillouin zone. A Feshbach-Schur reduction onto the infinitely degenerate eigenvalue 1 of the pair-contact operator gives explicit effective Hamiltonians with proved first-order error. The threshold problem reduces to a 4x4 Hermitian matrix criterion built from the two-point resistances of the square lattice, and hence to a quartic polynomial. This yields the complete atom-dimer zone and the critical mass ratio on the Brillouin zone, with bound between 1 and pi/(pi-2), and closed forms at all symmetry points. We prove: the zones in closed form; the critical mass ratio with exact value pi/(pi-2) at zero quasimomentum, reproducing a numerical value; at most two atom-dimer states for every quasimomentum and mass ratio; the onset laws, an essential singularity on the zone boundary and a linear-over-logarithm onset in the pure p-wave; two exact dualities showing that a change of statistics is equivalent to a shift of quasimomentum by pi; and the global range of the second fermionic threshold. The onset laws admit a renormalization-group reading: the inverse lattice Green function is a marginal s-wave coupling, binding energies arise by dimensional transmutation with lattice scale 16, and the zone boundary is of marginal (BCS/Kondo) type rather than of Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless type. All constants are verified independently by lattice quadrature, computer algebra, exact diagonalisation, and infinite-lattice solvers.

</details>


### [65] [$p$-adic spectral zeta functions via the inverse Stieltjes transform](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33660)
*Su Hu,Min-Soo Kim*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the $p$-adic spectral zeta function framework from discrete to continuous spectra using the inverse Stieltjes transform, defines $p$-adic spectral distributions and associated bosonic/fermionic zeta functions and determinants, establishes their analytic properties and symmetries, and embeds the prior discrete case as a special limit; an application is given to the position operator in $p$-adic quantum mechanics on compact subsets of $\mathbb{C}_p$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the previously introduced $p$-adic spectral zeta function—defined only for discrete spectra—to continuous spectra, enabling regularization of functional determinants for bounded operators in $p$-adic quantum physics.

Method: Uses the inverse Stieltjes transform applied to the resolvent of a bounded operator to construct a generalized $p$-adic spectral distribution; defines $p$-adic bosonic and fermionic zeta functions and functional determinants; derives analytic properties, special value formulas, and Stirling-type asymptotic expansions; verifies consistency with the discrete-spectrum case.

Result: A rigorous $p$-adic spectral distribution is constructed for continuous spectra; bosonic and fermionic $p$-adic zeta functions and determinants are defined and shown to possess meromorphic continuation, explicit special values, and Stirling expansions; a duality/symmetry between bosonic and fermionic cases emerges; the discrete-spectrum framework is recovered as a limiting case; the formalism is applied to the position operator in $p$-adic quantum mechanics on compact subsets of $\mathbb{C}_p$.

Conclusion: The extension to continuous spectra successfully unifies and generalizes $p$-adic spectral regularization, revealing intrinsic boson–fermion symmetry and providing a foundation for determinant-based quantization in $p$-adic quantum systems with continuous observables.

Abstract: Spectral zeta functions provide a standard tool for regularizing determinants of differential operators in quantum physics. In a previous paper (J. Math. Phys. 66: 083505, 2025), we introduced a $p$-adic spectral zeta function for discrete spectra via a locally analytic interpolation function. In this paper we extend the framework to continuous spectra using the inverse Stieltjes transform. Starting from the resolvent of a bounded operator, we construct a generalized distribution, called the $p$-adic spectral distribution, and define bosonic and fermionic zeta functions and functional determinants. We establish their analytic properties, special value formulas, and Stirling expansions, and show that our previous framework is embedded into the present one in the discrete case. In this approach, the bosonic and fermionic cases exhibit an interesting symmetry. As an application, we consider the position operator in $p$-adic quantum mechanics on an arbitrary compact subset of $\mathbb{C}_{p}$.

</details>


### [66] [Homogenized graphs with spectrally embedded defect states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33907)
*Kirill D. Cherednichenko,Stephen P. Shipman*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs bound states in the continuum (BICs) in effective media derived from homogenized high-contrast periodic metric graphs, using coupled lattice graphs and spectral decoupling of hybrid state spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize bound states at energies embedded in the continuous spectrum — a phenomenon with applications in waveguiding and resonance engineering — via homogenization of structured graph-based media rather than relying on ad hoc or symmetry-based constructions.

Method: Homogenization of high-contrast periodic metric graphs by simultaneously embedding two identical lattice graphs and coupling them via connecting edges; operator analysis via Fourier interpolation to map graph ODEs to spatial PDEs; spectral decomposition into orthogonal hybrid state spaces enabled by internal degrees of freedom in the effective medium; use of O(ε) operator-norm estimates for reduced resolvents to ensure persistence of BICs at finite scale.

Result: Existence of robust bound states in the continuum in the effective medium, arising from spectral band–gap overlap between two orthogonal hybrid state subspaces, sustained under ε-scale discretization.

Conclusion: High-contrast coupling and spectral hybridization in homogenized metric graphs provide a rigorous, analysis-based mechanism for generating and stabilizing bound states in the continuum, extending beyond traditional symmetry or parameter-tuning approaches.

Abstract: By homogenizing high-contrast periodic metric graphs, we create continuous media that admit bound states produced by local defects at energies embedded in the continuous spectrum. This is achieved by fitting two copies of a lattice graph simultaneously into space and coupling them by connecting edges. The homogenization of an ODE on a graph to a PDE in space is achieved by representing the associated operators in a common functional space through interpolation by Fourier analysis. The two coupled graphs result in an effective medium with internal degrees of freedom that allow a decomposition into two orthogonal spaces of hybrid states. Critical high contrast retains microscopic resonances in the effective medium and allows a spectral band of one hybrid state space to overlap a spectral gap of the other. This serves as the mechanism for creating bound states at frequencies embedded in the continuous spectrum of the effective medium. These bound states in the continuum persist for $\varepsilon$-scale metric graphs due to $O(\varepsilon)$ operator-norm estimates for reduced resolvents on the defect region.

</details>


### [67] [The equivalence of topological indices for $\mathrm{U}(1)\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_2$-symmetric quantum spin chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34119)
*Hal Tasaki*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the equivalence between Ogata's operator-algebraic index and Tasaki's elementary twist index for integer-spin chains with U(1)⋊ℤ₂ symmetry and a symmetric, locally-unique gapped ground state—without assuming translation invariance or MPS structure. It provides a rigorous, computable formula for the Ogata index via charge-fluctuation estimates and a group commutator identity, with an explicit error bound. As an application, it confirms the Ogata index is (−1)^S for the antiferromagnetic Heisenberg chain under stated gap and uniqueness assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether two distinct topological indices—Ogata’s general operator-algebraic index and Tasaki’s twist index requiring U(1) symmetry—are equivalent in their overlapping domain, and to provide a physically transparent, computable characterization of SPT order in quantum spin chains beyond idealized settings (e.g., no translation invariance or MPS assumption).

Method: Uses charge-fluctuation estimates to construct half-chain rotations in the strong limit with fixed phases; derives an exact group commutator identity linking local twist expectations to the Ogata index; establishes convergence with explicit error bounds using spectral gap and ground-state expectation data.

Result: Proves exact equivalence of Ogata and twist indices for integer-spin chains with U(1)⋊ℤ₂ symmetry and symmetric locally-unique gapped ground states; delivers a computable formula for the Ogata index with quantifiable accuracy; applies it to confirm Ogata index = (−1)^S for the antiferromagnetic Heisenberg chain under explicit gap/uniqueness conditions.

Conclusion: The Ogata and twist indices are rigorously equivalent in the shared physical setting, unifying two foundational approaches to SPT classification; the resulting formula enables robust numerical and analytical index computation without restrictive structural assumptions.

Abstract: Symmetry-protected topological (SPT) phases in quantum spin chains are distinguished by topological indices. Ogata's operator-algebraic indices apply to a broad range of symmetry classes without requiring continuous symmetry, whereas the elementary twist index of Tasaki requires $\mathrm{U}(1)$ symmetry. We prove their equivalence in the common setting of integer-spin chains with on-site $\mathrm{U}(1)\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetry and a symmetric locally-unique gapped ground state, without assuming translation invariance or a matrix product representation. A charge-fluctuation estimate yields a strong-limit construction of half-chain rotations with their phases fixed. An exact group commutator identity then identifies the Ogata index with the limit of local twist expectations and gives an explicit error bound. This provides a computable formula when a lower bound on the gap and the relevant ground-state expectations are available. As an application, a previous twist-index calculation determines the Ogata index to be $(-1)^S$ for the antiferromagnetic Heisenberg chain under explicit uniqueness and boundary-field gap assumptions.
  The author formulated the problem and proposed the basic strategy, while ChatGPT made substantial contributions to the development of the proof.

</details>


### [68] [Discontinuity of Continuum Fermion Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34402)
*Oliver Siebert*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that the Heisenberg dynamics of continuum fermions with nonconstant pair interactions is not pointwise-norm continuous on the gauge-invariant CAR algebra, implying dynamical non-invariance of both the full and gauge-invariant CAR algebras for almost all times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the continuity and algebraic invariance properties of quantum dynamics for interacting fermionic systems in the continuum, particularly whether standard C*-algebraic frameworks (CAR algebra and its gauge-invariant subalgebra) remain physically meaningful under interaction-driven time evolution.

Method: Functional-analytic analysis of Heisenberg dynamics on the CAR algebra; application of automatic-continuity theorems; assumptions on dispersion relation, external potential, and class of pair potentials; proof of failure of pointwise-norm continuity.

Result: Pointwise-norm continuity fails on the gauge-invariant CAR algebra for a large class of nonconstant pair potentials; consequently, neither the CAR algebra nor its gauge-invariant subalgebra is invariant under the dynamics for almost all times.

Conclusion: Standard algebraic formulations of fermionic quantum statistical mechanics break down dynamically under generic interactions—highlighting limitations of the CAR algebra as a domain for interacting Heisenberg evolutions in the continuum.

Abstract: We study the Heisenberg dynamics of continuum fermions in $\IR^d$ interacting through a pair potential. For a large class of nonconstant pair potentials and under suitable assumptions on the dispersion relation and external potential, we prove that the dynamics fails to be pointwise-norm continuous even on the gauge-invariant CAR algebra. An automatic-continuity argument then shows that neither the CAR algebra nor its gauge-invariant subalgebra is invariant under the dynamics, with non-invariance occurring for almost all times.

</details>


### [69] [Classical BV Pushforward](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34533)
*Xin Geng,Andrey Losev,Vyacheslav Lysov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper defines the classical BV pushforward and proves it preserves solutions of the classical master equation (CME) and BV canonical equivalence under isolation conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous framework for reducing BV structures under geometric or field-theoretic dimensional reduction, particularly preserving key algebraic and cohomological properties like CME solutions and canonical equivalence.

Method: Definition and analysis of the classical BV pushforward operator; proofs via homological perturbation theory and local analysis near isolated critical points; verification using explicit examples.

Result: The classical BV pushforward maps CME solutions on the total space to CME solutions on the base space, and preserves BV canonical equivalence when critical points are isolated.

Conclusion: The classical BV pushforward is a well-defined, structure-preserving operation essential for consistent classical BV reduction in gauge theories and topological field theories.

Abstract: We define the classical BV pushforward and establish its two main properties. First, we prove that it maps solutions of the classical master equation (CME) on the total space to solutions of the CME on the smaller space. Second, we prove that, for isolated critical points, it maps BV canonically equivalent solutions to BV canonically equivalent solutions. We illustrate the construction with examples and discuss its applications.

</details>


### [70] [Scattering Measures and Information Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34721)
*Tatsuro Oda*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops an information-geometric framework for scattering experiments using parameterized finite measures, decomposes Fisher information into mass and shape components, models measurement processes as (sub-)probability kernels that contract Fisher information, and links scattering to optimal experimental design and quantum measurements with incomplete detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify scattering experiments, quantum measurement theory, and optimal experimental design under a common information-theoretic framework grounded in Fisher information geometry.

Method: Formulates scattering experiments as parameterized finite measures on outcome spaces; derives Fisher metric on the space of such measures; decomposes Fisher information via total mass and normalized shape; models measurements as probability/sub-probability kernels; analyzes Fisher information contraction under these kernels.

Result: A geometric decomposition of Fisher information distinguishing scattering strength (mass) from outcome redistribution (shape); proof that measurement kernels are Fisher-contractive; unification of classical scattering, quantum incomplete detection, and optimal design via Fisher geometry.

Conclusion: Scattering experiments admit a natural finite-measure representation whose Fisher geometry provides a rigorous, general framework for quantifying and comparing information content across classical and quantum measurement scenarios, enabling principled design and analysis of scattering experiments.

Abstract: We formulate scattering experiments in terms of parameterized finite measures on outcome spaces and develop the associated information geometry defined by the Fisher metric. The decomposition of a scattering measure into total mass and normalized shape separates Fisher information into contributions from changes in overall scattering strength and redistribution among outcomes. Measurement processes are represented by probability or sub-probability kernels acting on the scattering measure. Such kernels contract Fisher information, placing a broad class of experimental operations within a common information-theoretic framework. This formulation connects scattering experiments to the theory of optimal experimental design through the Fisher geometry of scattering measures. The same finite-measure structure arises naturally in quantum measurements with incomplete detection, clarifying its place in the hierarchy from quantum measurement to classical outcome statistics.

</details>


### [71] [Notes on Magnetostatic Interactions in $\mathbb R^3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34766)
*Vladimir Gol'dshtein,Wolfgang H. Müller,Reuven Segev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives a compact magnetostatic force functional from first principles, introducing an asymmetric active stress field and pressure term beyond classical Lorentz and Kelvin forces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate magnetostatics without relying on constitutive (e.g., aether) relations, and to rigorously derive mechanical force and couple densities from variational principles applied to magnetic dipole distributions described via current density potential fields.

Method: Uses a virtual work principle: equates virtual work done by the vector potential field during virtual motion of a magnetic dipole distribution to minus the time derivative of a newly defined potential energy functional; the dipole distribution is encoded in a current density potential field; no constitutive laws are assumed.

Result: Derives a generalized mechanical force functional comprising Lorentz and Kelvin force densities plus an asymmetric active stress field — whose skew-symmetric part yields the mechanical couple density — and an additional pressure term.

Conclusion: A constitutive-relation-free, energetically consistent formulation of magnetostatic forces emerges, revealing intrinsic coupling between force, torque, and pressure via an asymmetric stress tensor, enriching the classical framework.

Abstract: A compact formulation of magnetostatics is presented. Without using constitutive relations, including the aether relations, an expression is proposed for the potential energy of a region carrying a distribution of magnetic dipoles under a vector potential field, where the dipole distribution is specified by a current density potential field. Assuming that during a virtual motion of the dipole distribution the virtual work expended by the field is equal to minus the time derivative of the potential energy, an expression for the mechanical force functional is derived. In addition to the Lorentz and Kelvin force densities, the force functional contains an asymmetric active stress field, the skew-symmetric part of which is the mechanical couple density, and a pressure term.

</details>

### [72] [LaMET-Agent: An Agent Framework for Large-Momentum Effective Theory Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32225)
*Jinchen He,Xiangyu Jiang,Fei Yao,Dian-Jun Zhao*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: Introduces lamet-agent, an open-source LLM-based framework automating the multi-stage LaMET workflow for lattice QCD calculation of parton distributions, validated on pion/kaon PDFs and DAs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reduce expert-dependent, repetitive effort in implementing the mature but complex LaMET workflow for light-cone parton distribution calculations from lattice QCD.

Method: Develops lamet-agent—a modular, executable, reproducible LLM agent framework that orchestrates correlator analysis, renormalization, Fourier transformation, perturbative matching, and multiple extrapolations (continuum, physical pion mass, infinite-momentum).

Result: Validated on four end-to-end analyses: pion PDFs (gauge-invariant and Coulomb-gauge), and pion/kaon distribution amplitudes—yielding results consistent with published literature.

Conclusion: lamet-agent enables robust, inspectable automation of LaMET-based parton physics calculations; future releases will extend support to TMDs, GTMDs, and gluonic distributions.

Abstract: Large-momentum effective theory (LaMET) provides a first-principles framework for computing the $x$ dependence of light-cone parton distributions from lattice QCD. Over the past decade, theoretical and numerical advances have established a mature multi-stage workflow for systematic calculation of parton physics, although its implementation still requires expert judgment and substantial repeated effort. We present lamet-agent, an open-source large language model (LLM) agent framework that organizes this workflow into an executable, reproducible, and inspectable analysis pipeline. The present release supports collinear quark distributions and implements correlator analysis, renormalization, Fourier transformation, perturbative matching, continuum, physical pion mass and infinite-momentum extrapolations, and automated result review. We validate it on four end-to-end analyses: pion parton distribution functions in the gauge-invariant and Coulomb-gauge formulations, and pion and kaon distribution amplitudes, obtaining results consistent with the published calculations. Extensions to transverse-momentum-dependent distributions, generalized transverse-momentum-dependent distributions, and gluonic distribution functions are planned for subsequent releases.

</details>


### [73] [Measuring Mass Splittings in Baryon SU(2) Multiplets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34647)
*Constantia Alexandrou,Simone Bacchio,Antonio Evangelista,Christos Iona,Christian Schneider*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper applies the RM123 method to compute isospin-breaking corrections to baryon mass splittings in lattice QCD, focusing on spin-1/2 and spin-3/2 SU(2) multiplets using near-physical twisted-mass ensembles, at leading order in electromagnetic and quark-mass-difference effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Increasing precision of lattice QCD observables (approaching ~1%) necessitates inclusion of isospin-breaking effects from electromagnetic interactions and the up-down quark mass difference.

Method: RM123 method applied to baryon masses; calculations performed on twisted-mass lattice ensembles tuned near physical quark masses; only valence-quark contributions included; leading-order expansion in isospin-breaking parameters.

Result: Preliminary results for mass splittings within spin-1/2 and spin-3/2 baryon SU(2) multiplets.

Conclusion: The RM123 approach is viable for quantifying isospin-breaking effects in baryon spectroscopy at current lattice precision; valence-level leading-order results provide a baseline for future inclusion of sea-quark and higher-order effects.

Abstract: The inclusion of isospin-breaking corrections arising from electromagnetic interactions and the up-down quark mass difference is becoming increasingly important in lattice QCD calculations, as the precision of observables is at or approaching the percent level. In this context, the RM123 method has been employed in recent years to take into account such isospin corrections. In this work, we study baryon mass splittings using the RM123 approach. Preliminary results are presented for the mass splittings within spin-1/2 and spin-3/2 baryon SU(2) multiplets, obtained using twisted-mass ensembles tuned close to physical quark masses. We consider only valence contributions, with the splittings computed at leading order in the isospin-breaking parameters.

</details>


### [74] [The Peskin-Takeuchi $S$ parameter and vector meson decay constants in $N_f=8$ QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35371)
*Yasumichi Aoki,Tatsumi Aoyama,Ed Bennett,Toshihide Maskawaa,Kohtaroh Miura,Hiroshi Ohki,Enrico Rinaldi,Akihiro Shibata,Koichi Yamawaki,Takeshi Yamazaki*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the Peskin-Takeuchi S parameter in eight-flavor SU(3) lattice gauge theory—a walking technicolor candidate—using two complementary methods (first time moment of current correlators and vacuum polarization), with careful finite-volume corrections. It finds S ≈ 0.281(5)(17) at the lightest fermion mass, slightly lower than scaled-up QCD, but no strong suppression toward chiral limit once FV effects are included. Key low-energy constants (e.g., $L_{10}^r$), meson decay constants ($F_ρ$, $F_{a_1}$), and sum rules (Weinberg, DMO) are consistent with real-world QCD expectations; the $ρππ$ coupling and techni-rho width to weak bosons are also predicted.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess viability of eight-flavor SU(3) QCD as a walking technicolor model by precisely computing the electroweak S parameter and related chiral dynamics, addressing discrepancies in prior estimates and finite-volume artifacts.

Method: Lattice gauge theory simulations with multiple volumes; two independent S-parameter extraction methods: (i) conventional vacuum-polarization integral and (ii) first time moment of vector–axial vector current correlators; finite-volume corrections estimated via chiral perturbation theory–motivated formula; spectral sum rules (Weinberg, Das-Mathur-Okubo) used for cross-validation; determination of $L_{10}^r(M_ρ)$, $F_ρ$, $F_{a_1}$, and $F_ρ/F_π$.

Result: S = 0.281(5)(17) (one-doublet) at smallest fermion mass; $L_{10}^r(M_ρ)$ matches real-world QCD; $F_ρ/F_π$ stable and consistent with KSRF relation; $Γ_ρ/M_ρ \lesssim 0.24/N$; Weinberg and DMO sum rules satisfied, confirming consistency of S and $L_{10}^r$; no monotonic suppression of S toward chiral limit after FV correction.

Conclusion: Eight-flavor QCD exhibits QCD-like chiral dynamics and yields a phenomenologically viable S parameter for technicolor model building, though not strongly suppressed; results support its use in BSM scenarios, contingent on further checks of IR conformality vs. chiral symmetry breaking.

Abstract: We use lattice gauge theory to investigate the Peskin-Takeuchi S parameter in SU(3) gauge theory with eight light fundamental fermions (eight-flavor QCD), a candidate for walking technicolor. We develop a new approach to computing S using the first time moment of current correlators, in addition to the conventional vacuum-polarization method, and obtain consistent results. Finite-volume (FV) corrections are estimated using data at multiple lattice volumes and a formula motivated by chiral perturbation theory. At the smallest fermion mass, the one-doublet contribution is S = 0.281(5)(17), smaller than the value $\sim 0.35$ in scaled-up 2+1-flavor QCD (without subtracting the SM contribution). However, contrary to naive expectations, S toward smaller fermion masses is not necessarily suppressed once FV corrections are included. The resulting Gasser-Leutwyler parameter $L_{10}^r(M_ρ)$ is similar to that in real-world QCD. We also determine the vector and axial-vector meson decay constants, $F_ρ$ and $F_{a_1}$. The ratio $F_ρ/F_π$ is stable against the fermion mass and consistent with the KSRF estimate, implying a $ρππ$ coupling similar to that in real-world QCD. We predict the techni-rho decay width into weak bosons to be $Γ_ρ/M_ρ\lesssim 0.24/N$, where N is the number of weak doublets. We further find that the rho- and $a_1$-dominated Weinberg spectral-function and Das-Mathur-Okubo sum rules are satisfied, yielding S and $L_{10}^r(M_ρ)$ consistent with our main results. We discuss implications for BSM model building.

</details>


### [75] [Multilevel Plaquette-Space Sampling for Lattice Gauge Theories with Local Constraint Solves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35722)
*Ankur Singha,Jacob Finkenrath,Karl Jansen,Vipul Arora,Shinichi Nakajima*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces a multilevel normalizing-flow sampler for lattice gauge theories that operates directly in plaquette space—respecting exact Bianchi constraints—achieving superior sampling efficiency over link-space methods, especially in the weak-coupling regime critical for continuum limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome critical limitations of Monte Carlo (e.g., critical slowing down, topological freezing) and link-space generative models (e.g., long-range correlations at weak coupling), while enabling efficient, constraint-respecting sampling in the more physically natural plaquette representation.

Method: A coarse-to-fine multilevel normalizing flow that factorizes the global Bianchi constraint into local solves: at each refinement level, each determined plaquette depends on at most four newly sampled variables, ensuring constraint satisfaction exactly and scalability independent of lattice size.

Result: The Plaquette-Space Sampler (PSS) outperforms link-space baselines across U(1) 2D/4D and SU(2) 2D benchmarks, with increasing advantage toward weak coupling—where traditional and generative methods struggle most.

Conclusion: Direct, constraint-exact generative modeling in plaquette space via multilevel flows is feasible and highly effective, offering a scalable path toward first-principles simulations of asymptotically free gauge theories in the continuum limit.

Abstract: Lattice gauge theories are an important class of physical models with high-dimensional structured distributions that underpin first-principles calculations in particle, nuclear, and condensed-matter physics. Recent advances in generative modeling have opened new avenues for sampling Boltzmann distributions in lattice gauge theories, offering the potential to alleviate limitations of traditional Monte Carlo methods, including critical slowing down and topological freezing. However, generative samplers for lattice gauge theories are typically constructed in link space, where correlations become increasingly long-ranged toward weak coupling, posing a challenge for learning. Plaquettes provide a more natural representation, as the action is local in these variables. However, exact Bianchi constraints restrict them to a lower-dimensional manifold, complicating direct generative modeling. We introduce a multilevel normalizing-flow construction that samples directly in plaquette space while satisfying these constraints exactly. The key idea is a coarse-to fine factorization that transforms a globally coupled constraint problem into a sequence of local solves: at each refinement, every determined plaquette depends on at most four newly generated variables, while the size of the constraint problem remains independent of the lattice size. We validate the construction for $U(1)$ in two and four dimensions and for $SU(2)$ in two dimensions. Our multilevel Plaquette-Space Sampler (PSS) substantially outperforms link-space baselines, with the advantage increasing toward weak coupling, where the gauge coupling becomes small. This regime is particularly challenging for generative sampling and, in asymptotically free gauge theories, is relevant to continuum studies.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

# hep-th [[Back]](#toc)

### [76] [Cardy random matrix theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31825)
*Marco Ambrosini,Alexandre Belin,Jan de Boer,Julian Sonner*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces a 'Cardy random matrix model' constrained solely by S-modular invariance, regularized via coarse-graining over fixed-energy windows; it reproduces the Cardy density of states macroscopically and GUE-level statistics microscopically, with emergent scale hierarchy and Thouless time—offering a computational framework for 3D quantum gravity / 2D CFT duality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a minimal random matrix model encoding only the fundamental constraint of S-modular invariance—reflecting maximal ignorance about UV details—thereby bridging 2D conformal field theory (CFT) spectral constraints (e.g., Cardy formula) and random matrix theory (RMT), with implications for the matrix/tensor model dual of 3D quantum gravity.

Method: Imposes S-modular invariance as a coarse-grained constraint over fixed-width energy windows; derives an effective single-matrix potential whose eigenvalues represent CFT primary scaling dimensions; performs Monte Carlo simulations to sample the ensemble; analyzes spectral statistics across macroscopic (window-scale), mesoscopic (within-window), and microscopic (level-spacing) scales; identifies the scaling limit where Cardy behavior and RMT universality coexist.

Result: The coarse-grained spectral density matches the Cardy formula above a black-hole threshold gap; nearby eigenvalues exhibit Gaussian Unitary Ensemble (GUE) statistics; a hierarchical scale separation emerges: frozen macroscopic occupations (fixed by bootstrap), Vandermonde-driven mesoscopic repulsion, and resolved microscopic levels; an effective Thouless time arises, inversely proportional to the coarse-graining window width.

Conclusion: The Cardy random matrix model successfully unifies modular bootstrap constraints with RMT universality in a controlled scaling limit, providing a concrete, simulatable framework that realizes key features of holographic CFTs and constitutes a foundational step toward numerical approaches to 3D quantum gravity via matrix models.

Abstract: We construct and study a ``Cardy random matrix model'': a random matrix theory whose only input, in a maximal-ignorance prescription, is the constraint of $\mathbb{S}$-modular invariance. We regularize this constraint by coarse-graining over energy windows of fixed width, and obtain a potential for a single random matrix whose eigenvalues play the role of the scaling dimensions of primaries in a 2d CFT. The potential drives the coarse-grained density of states to the Cardy profile above a gap at the black hole threshold. We perform Monte Carlo simulations to confirm this behavior and find good agreement for the coarse-grained density of states, while at the same time revealing GUE statistics for nearby eigenvalues. We identify the scaling limit in which these phenomena emerge. In this limit, the model realizes a hierarchy of scales: at the macroscopic scale given by the window size, the occupations are frozen and the bootstrap constraint rigidly fixes the spectrum to the Cardy profile, while at the mesoscopic scale (inside a window) the Vandermonde repulsion rearranges the eigenvalues as in a random matrix theory. It is only at the microscopic scale of the mean level spacing that the individual levels are resolved. Accordingly, our random matrix model displays an effective Thouless time, set by the inverse energy width of the coarse-graining window. Our model provides a first step towards solving the matrix/tensor model dual to 3D quantum gravity on a computer.

</details>


### [77] [Localizing the M-Theory Path Integral](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31829)
*Pietro Benetti Genolini,Florian Gaar,Jerome P. Gauntlett,Jaeha Park,James Sparks*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives a supersymmetric localization formula for the M-theory path integral's perturbative part, yielding closed-form analytic expressions for ABJM and V^{5,2} partition functions—including previously numerical Airy constants—and provides all-genus Type IIA/topological string and integrated correlator results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain exact, closed-form analytic results for perturbative M-theory observables—particularly partition functions and indices—in strongly coupled ABJM and non-toric V^{5,2} theories, where field-theoretic methods fail or yield only numerical data.

Method: Supersymmetric localization in eleven-dimensional M-theory backgrounds; equivariant extension of Chern–Simons couplings for localized action; tangent-cotangent character for one-loop determinant on M2-brane backgrounds; reduction to integral over equivariant flux data.

Result: First closed-form expression for full perturbative ABJM partition function at arbitrary squashing and chemical potentials; new analytic topologically twisted index for black holes; closed analytic Airy constant for non-toric V^{5,2}; all-genus Type IIA expansion as equivariant topological string constant map; extracted integrated correlators.

Conclusion: The proposed localization framework unifies and extends known results, delivers novel analytic predictions beyond current field theory reach, and establishes a robust computational tool for quantum gravity observables in AdS4/CFT3.

Abstract: We propose a supersymmetric localization formula for the local perturbative contribution to the M-theory path integral, reducing it to an integral over equivariant flux data. Relative equivariant extensions of the eleven-dimensional Chern--Simons couplings determine the localized action, while for M2-brane backgrounds a tangent-cotangent character computes the one-loop determinant. This gives the first closed form expression for the complete perturbative ABJM partition function at general squashing and chemical potentials, and new analytic results for the ABJM topologically twisted index describing black holes. Applied to the non-toric $V^{5,2}$ theory, the same prescription yields a closed analytic Airy constant previously known only numerically. Our formulas reproduce all available independent field theory results, and provide analytic predictions at general parameters where no field theory answer is currently known. We derive an all-genus result for the Type IIA expansion which is an equivariant form of the usual topological string constant map result, and also extract integrated correlators.

</details>


### [78] [Nonlinear electroweak sphaleron clouds around Kerr black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31845)
*Carlos Herdeiro,Jutta Kunz,Hendrik Mennenga,Eugen Radu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs nonlinear electroweak sphaleron clouds around Schwarzschild and Kerr black holes in the probe limit, revealing a rich branch structure and finite mass gap—distinct from superradiant linear clouds—and provides a closed-form leading-order Higgs solution on an exact Yang-Mills configuration in Schwarzschild spacetime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the interplay between two fundamental nonlinear solutions—Kerr black holes (GR) and electroweak sphalerons (Standard Model)—by studying how sphaleronic configurations behave in strong gravitational fields.

Method: Uses the probe limit to construct nonlinear sphaleron cloud solutions around Schwarzschild and Kerr black holes; revisits flat-spacetime static/spinning multi-sphalerons with physical electroweak parameters; analyzes domain of existence, branch structure, and physical properties; derives a closed-form leading-order Higgs solution on an exact Yang-Mills background in Schwarzschild geometry.

Result: Sphaleron clouds exhibit a rich branch structure and retain a finite mass gap; they do not reduce to linear clouds (unlike superradiant cases); a closed-form analytic Higgs solution is obtained for a specific Yang-Mills configuration in Schwarzschild spacetime.

Conclusion: Nonlinear sphaleron–black hole configurations form stable, nontrivial families distinct from known superradiant clouds, suggesting novel phenomenology at the interface of gravity and electroweak physics.

Abstract: Kerr black holes and electroweak sphalerons are remarkable solutions of two pillars of modern physics: General Relativity and the Standard Model of particle physics, respectively. In this work, we investigate their interplay by constructing nonlinear sphaleron clouds around Schwarzschild and Kerr black holes in the probe limit. We first revisit static and spinning (multi-)sphalerons in flat spacetime, using the measured electroweak parameters. We then explore the domain of existence and physical properties of the corresponding black-hole configurations, revealing a rich branch structure. Remarkably, the sphaleron clouds retain a finite mass gap and do not approach linear clouds within the families studied, distinguishing them from the familiar clouds associated with the onset of superradiant instabilities. We also obtain a closed-form leading-order Higgs solution on an exact Yang-Mills configuration in a Schwarzschild background.

</details>


### [79] [Quantum vacuum interaction between semitransparent plates in a Pöschl-Teller background: an exact relative TGTG formula](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31866)
*Jose M. Muñoz-Castañeda,Inés Cavero-Peláez,Gonzalo Sancho-Garrido*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives an exact background-dressed TGTG formula for the one-loop quantum vacuum interaction between two semitransparent Dirac-delta plates embedded in a one-soliton Pöschl–Teller background, defining the interaction via a relative determinant that subtracts individual one-plate contributions; it shows position- and separation-dependent energy, absence of infrared divergence from the zero mode for repulsive plates, and extends results to planar geometries with position-dependent forces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously compute the quantum vacuum interaction between localized objects (delta plates) in a nontrivial solitonic background—beyond flat-space approximations—while properly handling infrared issues and background subtraction ambiguities.

Method: Exact spectral analysis using the TGTG (T-matrix, Green’s function, trace regularization) formalism adapted to a solitonic background; construction of a relative determinant that isolates the two-body interaction by subtracting one-plate contributions computed in the same background; careful treatment of zero modes and renormalization.

Result: An explicit background-dressed TGTG formula for the interaction energy; demonstration that the energy depends on absolute plate positions and their separation—not just separation alone; proof that the normalizable zero mode does not cause infrared divergence for repulsive plates; extension to planar plates yielding position-dependent Casimir forces.

Conclusion: The relative determinant approach successfully defines a finite, physically meaningful one-loop vacuum interaction in a solitonic background; position dependence is intrinsic and unavoidable in curved or inhomogeneous backgrounds, distinguishing it from standard Casimir setups; the framework is robust against zero-mode divergences under repulsion.

Abstract: We derive an exact background-dressed TGTG formula for the one-loop quantum vacuum inter- action between two semitransparent Dirac-delta plates in the one-soliton Pöchl-Teller background. The interaction is defined through a relative determinant in which the two isolated one-plate contributions are subtracted in the same background. As a result, the energy depends on the individual plate positions as well as on their separation. For repulsive plates, the normalizable zero mode does not induce an infrared divergence in the relative interaction energy. We also extend the construction to planar plates and analyze the corresponding position-dependent interaction forces.

</details>


### [80] [Spread Complexity for Local Operator Quenches with Conserved Momentum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31867)
*Ali Fatemiabhari*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes locally excited states in 2D CFTs with independent chiral/anti-chiral regulators, computes Lanczos coefficients and spread complexity analytically and numerically, and finds a mismatch between its time derivative and the holographic dual (infalling particle's proper momentum).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand quantum complexity growth in locally excited states of 2D CFTs and test holographic proposals relating spread complexity to bulk infalling particle momentum.

Method: Analytical computation of early-time Lanczos coefficients and spread complexity for locally excited states on plane/cylinder/finite temperature; numerical extension to higher Krylov indices; comparison with holographic prediction based on proper momentum of infalling bulk particle.

Result: Early-time spread complexity is analytically obtained; numerical results extend to larger Krylov indices and longer times; time derivative of spread complexity does not match the holographically predicted proper momentum of an infalling particle.

Conclusion: The spread complexity of locally excited CFT states grows in a manner inconsistent with the proposed holographic dual involving the proper momentum of an infalling bulk particle, challenging that specific duality mapping.

Abstract: We study a family of locally excited states in two-dimensional conformal field theories in different setups, defined on the plane, on the cylinder, or at finite temperature. These states are constructed by assigning independent chiral and anti-chiral regulators to a local primary operator, leading to excitations carrying net momentum. We analytically calculate the first few Lanczos coefficients and the early time behaviour of the spread complexity associated with these states. Then, using numerical methods, we obtain the coefficients reaching larger Krylov indices and find the associated spread complexity for a longer range of time evolution. We also compare the rate of change of the resulting spread complexity with the proposed holographic dual quantity, related to the proper momentum of an infalling particle in the dual bulk, but we observe a mismatch.

</details>


### [81] [Cylinder Defect Casimir Energy and Weyl Anomalies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34529)
*Yang Zhou*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies and distinguishes two Casimir observables for conformal surface defects—circular and great cylinders—and relates their Casimir energies to displacement and local conformal anomalies, respectively, with verification from free-field and holographic methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the role of conformal anomalies in Casimir energies of surface defects, distinguishing between geometric embeddings (circular vs. great cylinder) and linking each to a specific anomaly type (displacement vs. local).

Method: Analytical computation of Casimir energy for two distinct embeddings of a cylindrical conformal defect—(i) circular cylinder in flat space, using displacement anomaly; (ii) great cylinder via radial quantization, using local conformal anomaly—and cross-verification via free-field theory and holography.

Result: Circular cylinder yields Casimir energy $-d_1\log(R/\varepsilon)/(24R)$ governed by displacement anomaly coefficient $d_1$; great cylinder yields $-b/(12R)$ governed by local anomaly coefficient $b$. Both forms are confirmed in free-field and holographic settings.

Conclusion: Casimir energy of conformal surface defects is embedding-dependent and encodes distinct conformal anomalies: displacement anomaly for extrinsic geometry (circular cylinder), local anomaly for intrinsic radial quantization (great cylinder); the two observables are physically inequivalent but complementary diagnostics of defect CFT data.

Abstract: We distinguish two Casimir observables associated with a conformal surface defect. A circular cylinder embedded in flat space has a logarithmic contribution to its Casimir energy, $-d_1\log(R/ε)/(24R)$, fixed by the displacement anomaly. A great cylinder obtained by radial quantization of the ambient theory instead has Casimir energy $-b/(12R)$ in the conformal defect vacuum with a fixed local anomaly convention. Free-field and holographic calculations provide checks of the proposed relations in both embeddings.

</details>


### [82] [Transient Coherence and Multiscale Spectral Structure after a Mass Quench in a 1+1-Dimensional Scalar Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31884)
*Gabriel Abellán,Nelson Bolívar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a bilocal observable based on the Keldysh correlator smeared with a Morlet wavelet to quantify anomalous pair correlations induced by a mass quench in a 1+1D free scalar field; it shows thermal independence of the relative coherence weight, analyzes finite-time wavelet response, contrasts with Wigner spectrum, establishes a positivity bound, and extends analysis to Hartree dynamics in de Sitter spacetime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate and quantify anomalous pair correlations generated by a sudden mass quench—distinct from final particle occupations—and to understand their thermal robustness and observational dependence on finite-time windows and smearing kernels.

Method: Defines a bilocal observable via Morlet-wavelet smearing of the statistical Keldysh correlator over finite relative time; computes excess over dephased (incoherent) correlator to obtain dimensionless relative coherence weight; analytically derives finite-window response; compares with signed finite-time Wigner spectrum; establishes Gaussian-extension positivity bound; validates against exact smooth-quench solutions; extends to renormalized Hartree approximation in 2D de Sitter background.

Result: The relative coherence weight is exactly independent of initial temperature for Gaussian thermal states under free evolution; the anomalous signal is intrinsically window-dependent; a zero-mean wavelet annihilates the constant-in-relative-time anomalous term on the infinite line; the signed Wigner spectrum differs qualitatively; a strict Gaussian-extension positivity bound is derived; Hartree extension reveals distinct oscillatory vs. nonoscillatory regimes for scalar modes in de Sitter.

Conclusion: Anomalous pair correlations are robust, measurable signatures of quantum quenches that encode non-thermal coherence beyond occupation numbers; their detection requires careful finite-time smearing, and their interpretation necessitates distinction from phase-space distributions like the Wigner function; the framework generalizes to interacting settings via renormalized Hartree dynamics in curved spacetime.

Abstract: A mass quench generates anomalous pair correlations that are absent from the final particle occupations. For a free real scalar field in $1+1$ dimensions, we define a bilocal observable by smearing the statistical Keldysh correlator with a Morlet kernel on the finite post-quench relative-time interval. Its excess over the dephased correlator gives a dimensionless relative coherence weight. At fixed momentum, this weight is exactly independent of the initial temperature for Gaussian thermal states evolved with the same free Hamiltonian: the common thermal amplitude cancels between numerator and denominator. The cancellation is not a purity criterion and is not exclusive to wavelets. We derive the finite-window response and show that the anomalous signal depends essentially on the observation window; an admissible zero-mean wavelet annihilates the relative-time-constant anomalous term on the full line. We compare this response with the signed finite-time Wigner spectrum and derive a distinct Gaussian-extension positivity bound. Exact smooth-quench occupations provide an independent dynamical reference. Finally, we formulate a renormalized Hartree extension and delimit the oscillatory and nonoscillatory regimes of a scalar mode on a fixed two-dimensional de Sitter background.

</details>


### [83] [General Relativity with Finite Boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31928)
*Chawakorn Maneerat*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This thesis studies general relativity on spacetimes with a finite timelike boundary under conformal boundary conditions—fixing the conformal class of the induced metric and trace $K$ of extrinsic curvature—across all $Λ$ signs. It analyzes linearized Einstein equations around Minkowski, dS, and AdS in Lorentzian signature using the Kodama-Ishibashi formalism adapted to boundaries, and computes semiclassical gravitational path integrals in Euclidean signature for bounded black hole and cosmological horizons, defining a conformal canonical ensemble with fixed conformal temperature and $K$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically extend gravitational analysis—including stability, perturbations, and thermodynamics—to spacetimes with finite timelike boundaries, where standard asymptotic (e.g., AdS or asymptotically flat) boundary conditions do not apply, and to unify treatment across all cosmological constant signs under geometrically natural conformal boundary data.

Method: Imposes conformal boundary conditions (fixed conformal class of induced metric and trace $K$) on a finite timelike boundary; employs Lorentzian linearized analysis using the Kodama-Ishibashi formalism generalized to bounded domains; computes Euclidean semiclassical path integral with boundary data interpreted as defining a conformal canonical ensemble (fixed conformal temperature and $K$).

Result: Derives well-posed linearized dynamics for gravity on bounded spacetimes across all $Λ$; identifies mode stability properties for perturbations around Minkowski, dS, and AdS with various boundary geometries; obtains thermodynamic potentials and entropy expressions for black hole and cosmological horizons enclosed by the boundary, showing dependence on $K$ and conformal temperature in the semiclassical limit.

Conclusion: Conformal boundary conditions provide a consistent, geometrically robust framework for formulating general relativity on manifolds with finite timelike boundaries, enabling unified treatment of perturbation theory and horizon thermodynamics irrespective of the sign of $Λ$, and revealing $K$ as a thermodynamic variable conjugate to area in the conformal canonical ensemble.

Abstract: In this thesis, we consider general relativity on a manifold with a timelike boundary of finite size, across all signs of the cosmological constant $Λ$. We impose conformal boundary conditions, whereby the conformal class of the induced metric and the trace of the extrinsic curvature $K$ are fixed at the boundary. In Lorentzian signature, we analyse the linearised Einstein equations about Minkowski, de Sitter (dS), and anti-de Sitter (AdS) space for a variety of boundaries, employing the Kodama-Ishibashi formalism adapted to manifolds with a boundary. In Euclidean signature, we study the thermodynamics of black hole and cosmological horizons enclosed by the boundary, computing the leading semiclassical approximation of the gravitational path integral, for which the boundary data define a conformal canonical ensemble of fixed conformal temperature and $K$.

</details>


### [84] [Wormhole fragmentation and the axion Weak Gravity Conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32063)
*Puxin Lin,Gary Shiu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives the axion Weak Gravity Conjecture (aWGC) from nonperturbative Euclidean wormhole fragmentation into charged defects, fixes the axion action-to-charge bound and its order-one coefficient, relates it to the Imaginary Distance Bound (IDB), extends it to multiple axio-dilaton systems with geometric interpretations, and argues that aWGC-satisfying axions destabilize wormholes—mitigating factorization issues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a first-principles derivation of the axion Weak Gravity Conjecture (aWGC) grounded in nonperturbative quantum gravity effects—specifically, Euclidean wormhole physics—and to resolve associated conceptual problems such as wormhole proliferation and factorization paradoxes.

Method: Analyzes nonperturbative fragmentation of Euclidean wormholes into localized charged defects; computes how fragmentation affects the Euclidean action; imposes action minimization to constrain the axion decay constant versus instanton charge; generalizes to multi-axion and axio-dilaton systems using geometric formulations.

Result: Derives the aWGC bound with a fixed, calculable order-one coefficient; shows coefficient matches IDB without extra scalars but strengthens with them; establishes geometric structure for multi-axion aWGC; demonstrates wormhole instability under aWGC satisfaction.

Conclusion: The aWGC is not merely a swampland conjecture but a necessary consequence of Euclidean wormhole dynamics in quantum gravity; its validity ensures wormhole suppression and consistency of effective field theory with quantum gravity.

Abstract: We derive the axion Weak Gravity Conjecture (aWGC) from the nonperturbative fragmentation of Euclidean wormholes into charged defects. Requiring fragmentation to lower the Euclidean action fixes the axion action-to-charge bound and its order-one coefficient. Without additional scalars, this coefficient coincides with that of the Imaginary Distance Bound (IDB), while additional scalars strengthen the bound. The aWGC in the presence of multiple axio-dilaton pairs is discussed, leading to geometric representations. Axions satisfying the aWGC render the corresponding wormholes unstable, potentially suppressing their proliferation and resolving associated factorization problems.

</details>


### [85] [A backreacted bouncing geodesic in 2D](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32071)
*Gauri Batra,Adam Levine,Stephen H. Shenker*

Main category: hep-th
