TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum corrections to finite-temperature correlation functions in 2D dilaton gravity holography, showing that gravitational backreaction smooths out singularity-induced geodesic singularities in time-domain correlators; instead, singularity physics manifests via a Stokes phenomenon near t=0 (and its thermal image), with structural parallels to forbidden singularities in large-c 2D CFT blocks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum gravitational effects modify the holographic encoding of black hole singularity physics in analytically continued thermal correlators, particularly whether and how singularity signatures persist beyond the classical geodesic approximation.

Method: Analyzes quantum-corrected two-point correlators $G(t)$ in 2D dilaton gravity using gravitational backreaction of null geodesics on the black hole geometry; employs steepest descent analysis of the backreacted geodesic saddle and studies associated Stokes phenomena near the coincident-point singularity at $t=0$ and its thermal periodic images.

Result: Gravitational backreaction smoothes the classical 'bouncing-off-singularity' singularities in $G(t)$; singularity physics is instead encoded non-analytically via a Stokes phenomenon localized near $t=0$ and $t=i\beta$, revealing an intricate structure of Stokes lines analogous to that governing forbidden singularities in large-$c$ Virasoro conformal blocks.

Conclusion: The black hole singularity does not generically produce sharp singularities in analytically continued correlators once quantum gravitational corrections (specifically, worldline backreaction) are included; rather, its imprint is subtle and topological—manifesting through Stokes phenomena that control analytic continuation across critical lines in the complex time plane, suggesting deep connections between bulk singularity resolution and boundary CFT monodromy structure.

Abstract: In holography, analytically continued finite temperature correlation functions $G(t)$ exhibit signatures of the black hole singularity. These take the form of singularities at certain values of $t$ whose bulk origin is a null geodesic worldline "bouncing off" the black hole singularity. We study certain quantum corrections to this effect in 2D dilaton gravity that are captured by gravitational backreaction of the worldline onto the black hole geometry. These corrections smooth out the bouncing geodesic singularities. By following the backreacted geodesic saddle in a steepest descent analysis we find instead that the black hole singularity physics gets encoded in a subtle way involving a Stokes phenomenon near the coincident point singularity of $G(t)$ at $t=0$ (and its thermal image). We point out a parallel between the structure of Stokes lines uncovered in this analysis and the structure present in the analysis of forbidden singularities in large $c$ 2D conformal blocks. Finally we make some preliminary remarks about the significance of quantum corrections in these models beyond backreaction.

</details>


### [86] [Learning Scattering Amplitudes with Transformer Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32096)
*Philip Velie,Tianji Cai,Piyush Jha,Vijay Ganesh,Aishik Ghosh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces a transformer-based RL algorithm enhanced with symbolic symmetries and exact linear relations to compute high-loop scattering amplitudes in planar N=4 SYM, drastically reducing required prior knowledge and ensuring physical consistency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome factorial growth of state space in high-loop amplitude computation and enable generalizable, physically consistent ML predictions beyond brute-force enumeration.

Method: Combines transformer architecture with Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) and hard-coded linear constraints from known symmetries; uses symbology-based sequence-to-integer mapping and constraint propagation for coefficient assignment.

Result: Significantly reduced a priori input requirement; all outputs provably satisfy exact physical relations; enables scalable inference to higher loops where pure transformer or enumeration fails.

Conclusion: Incorporating domain-specific symbolic structure—via constraint propagation and MCTS-guided search—into transformer RL is essential for trustworthy, generalizable ML in high-energy theoretical physics.

Abstract: We introduce a transformer reinforcement learning algorithm that learns to solve high loop-level scattering amplitudes in planar N = 4 Super Yang-Mills theory. Our algorithm improves on previous transformer-only results by incorporating previously derived symmetries and relationships into the learning algorithm. This results in a greatly decreased fraction of the solution that needs to be known a priori to solve the entire problem. An additional benefit is that our algorithm also ensures that every output obeys the set of known relationships. Rather than predicting all coefficients independently, the model proposes assignments that are propagated through exact linear relations, while MCTS searches over assignments when propagation alone is insufficient. This is crucial to the generalization of machine learning approaches to higher loops, as without this, there is no way to overcome the factorially scaling of state sizes and compare to results derived via other methods. Using the symbology representation of the form factor, we frame the problem as learning a mapping between discrete sequences and integer coefficients.

</details>


### [87] [Nonlinear pole topology of slow currents in holographic probe-brane metals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32748)
*Yan Han,Xiang-Qian Li*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the nonlinear electromagnetic response of holographic systems with slow vector modes, classifying third-order response topologies by pole structure and computing exact third-harmonic responses in Dirac–Born–Infeld probe brane models on Lifshitz black-brane backgrounds; it finds that pole topology (constitutive vs. relaxation vs. two-mode) is dynamically selected—not fixed by the DBI action—and identifies infrared scaling, a dc nonlinear crossover field, and implications for higher-form currents and multi-mode slow dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve how low-frequency Drude response relates to current-carrying conserved sectors in strongly correlated systems, and to classify and physically interpret the pole structure of cubic retarded response in systems with slow vector modes—particularly whether nonlinearities arise from constitutive relations, relaxation, or coupling to other slow modes.

Method: Classification of cubic retarded response pole topologies based on symmetry and slow-mode dynamics; holographic computation of full complex third-harmonic conductivity using finite-density Dirac–Born–Infeld probe branes embedded in Lifshitz black-brane backgrounds with dynamical exponents z = 1, 3/2, and 2; analytic and numerical extraction of infrared weights, zero locations, scaling functions, and crossover fields.

Result: In the marginal z = 2 Lifshitz theory, third-harmonic response asymptotically follows pure relaxation topology (p_IR = 0.994 ± 0.002, zero at ω_×/Γ_J = 0.2918); nonmarginal z = 1 and z = 3/2 cases show mixed constitutive–relaxation weights; exact nonlinear dc solution yields parameter-free dense-limit scaling function and T^(3/2)-dependent crossover field E_nl; two-mode coupling introduces pair-frequency poles.

Conclusion: The cubic response pole topology is dynamically selected by infrared physics—not dictated by the DBI square-root structure—revealing a deeper link between emergent slow higher-form currents, nonlinear transport classification, and holographic many-body dynamics; inclusion of additional slow scalars (e.g., energy or strain) generically introduces new pair-frequency poles.

Abstract: Low-frequency Drude response does not by itself determine which nearly conserved sector carries an electric current. We classify the leading cubic retarded response of an isolated, inversion-symmetric slow vector by its pole topology. Constitutive nonlinearity produces only incoming-frequency poles, whereas nonlinear relaxation adds a propagator at the emitted frequency; coupling to a second scalar slow mode generates pair-frequency poles. We then compute the full complex third-harmonic response of finite-density Dirac--Born--Infeld probe branes in Lifshitz black-brane backgrounds. In the marginal $z=2$ theory, the dense infrared response approaches the relaxation topology, with a signed infrared weight $p_{\rm IR}=0.994\pm0.002$ and a normalization-independent zero at $ω_\times/Γ_J=0.2918$. Nonmarginal $z=1$ and $z=3/2$ backgrounds instead realize mixed constitutive--relaxation weights, demonstrating that the topology is selected dynamically rather than imposed by the DBI square root. The exact nonlinear dc solution further yields a parameter-free dense-limit scaling function and a crossover field $E_{\rm nl}\propto T^{3/2}$ at $z=2$. These results connect emergent higher-form slow currents, nonlinear response theory, and holographic transport, while the two-mode extension identifies the additional pole structures expected when energy or deformation modes remain slow.

</details>


### [88] [3d Lovelock gravity and the holographic c-theorem: Proof at all orders and resummation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32866)
*Gokhan Alkac,Luis Guajardo,Hikmet Ozsahin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves that 3D Lovelock–Horndeski gravity admits a holographic c-theorem at all orders and arbitrary couplings; the scalar equation reduces to a total derivative in domain wall backgrounds, enabling construction of a monotonic c-function satisfying the null energy condition; the dual theory is scale- but not conformally invariant; central charges are computed exactly (Gauss–Bonnet) and asymptotically (full tower), matching UV c-function values; resummed Lagrangians, c-functions, and central charges are given in closed form for theories admitting regular black holes, classified by positivity of the effective Newton constant and vacuum uniqueness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a holographic c-theorem for higher-derivative 3D gravity beyond Einstein–Hilbert, specifically for the Horndeski theory arising from regularized Lovelock invariants—motivated by the need to extend renormalization group monotonicity and AdS/CFT correspondence to non-conformal, scale-invariant duals.

Method: Analyzes domain wall (RG flow) solutions in the minisuperspace approximation; exploits the total-derivative structure of the Horndeski scalar equation to algebraically relate scalar and warp factor derivatives; integrates the null energy condition to construct a monotonic c-function; computes the central charge via logarithmic divergence of the on-shell action on S³; derives resummed expressions using generating functions for coupling sequences compatible with regular black hole solutions.

Result: A monotonic, well-defined holographic c-function exists for all orders and couplings; the dual field theory is scale-invariant but breaks special conformal symmetry due to linear scalar profile; central charge is linear in couplings and equals the UV limit of the c-function; closed-form resummed Lagrangians, c-functions, and central charges are obtained for theories with positive effective Newton constant and unique nontrivial vacuum.

Conclusion: 3D Lovelock–Horndeski gravity robustly supports a holographic c-theorem, confirming monotonic RG flow even in non-conformal settings; the structure is governed by two generating functions, enabling classification of physically viable (regular black hole) theories by vacuum uniqueness and gravitational coupling positivity.

Abstract: We prove that 3d Lovelock gravity, the Horndeski theory obtained from the regularized Lovelock invariants in three dimensions, admits a holographic c-theorem at all orders and for arbitrary values of the couplings. In the minisuperspace of domain wall solutions, the equation of the Horndeski scalar is a total derivative at every order, which locks the derivative of the scalar to that of the warp factor along the flows we consider and allows the null energy condition to be integrated into a monotonic c-function. The linear scalar profile of the resulting vacuum breaks the special conformal transformations, so the dual theory is scale-invariant but not conformal, and the central charge is defined as the coefficient of the logarithmic divergence of the on-shell action on the sphere. We compute it exactly for the Gauss-Bonnet case and asymptotically for the full tower, and find that it is linear in the couplings and matches the UV value of the c-function. Since all results depend on the couplings only through two generating functions, we give the resummed Lagrangians, c-functions and central charges in closed form for the choices of couplings that lead to regular black holes, and classify the resummed theories by the positivity of the effective Newton constant on the vacuum and the uniqueness of the vacuum with a non-trivial scalar.

</details>


### [89] [Charged regular AdS$_3$ black holes in a scalar-vector-tensor theory coupled to Born-Infeld electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32878)
*Gokhan Alkac*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs exact, static, regular charged AdS₃ black holes in a 3D scalar-vector-tensor theory derived from regularized Gauss-Bonnet gravity, showing that Born-Infeld electrodynamics eliminates the logarithmic curvature singularity present in the Maxwell counterpart; the solutions are horizon-structure-flexible and serve as singularity-free backgrounds for entropy microstate counting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the logarithmic curvature singularity at the origin in Maxwell-charged 3D AdS black holes within a scalar-vector-tensor extension of regularized Gauss-Bonnet gravity, and to construct physically viable, fully regular black hole geometries suitable for quantum gravity tests.

Method: Exact analytical construction of static, circularly symmetric solutions in three dimensions using a scalar-vector-tensor theory derived from regularized Gauss-Bonnet couplings; replacement of Maxwell electrodynamics with non-linear Born-Infeld electrodynamics to ensure core regularity; explicit computation and analysis of the metric function and curvature invariants.

Result: Exact regular AdS₃ black hole solutions with no curvature singularities; explicit demonstration of smooth core geometry; classification of horizon structures (single, double, extremal, horizonless) based on parameter space; confirmation of finiteness of all curvature invariants including Kretschmann scalar at r = 0.

Conclusion: Born-Infeld electrodynamics successfully cures the central singularity in this 3D higher-curvature gravity framework, yielding a family of physically consistent, singularity-free black holes—making them ideal candidates for microscopic entropy calculations without UV-sensitive singularities.

Abstract: We construct exact, static, circularly symmetric charged regular AdS$_3$ black hole solutions in a three-dimensional scalar-vector-tensor theory obtained from regularized Gauss-Bonnet couplings. While the Maxwell-charged counterpart of this theory suffers from a logarithmic curvature singularity at the origin, we demonstrate that coupling the action to non-linear Born-Infeld electrodynamics cures this pathology. We obtain the metric function and show explicitly the regularity of the core. Depending on the choice of parameters, the solution exhibits various horizon structures, including single-horizon, double-horizon, extremal black holes, and horizonless regular geometries. Finally, we discuss the significance of this regular spacetime as a testbed for microscopic entropy calculations in singularity-free setups.

</details>


### [90] [Accelerating systems as probes of Lorentz violation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33029)
*Quentin G. Bailey,Nils A. Nilsson,Sawyer J. Star*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the Unruh effect in Lorentz-violating effective field theory, computing exact quantization, Wightman functions, and detector response—revealing nonstationary behavior and measurable spectral deviations from standard thermal spectra even for tiny Lorentz-violating coefficients (~10⁻⁷).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum-field effects under Lorentz violation, specifically whether the Unruh effect persists or modifies in a controlled way when boost symmetry is broken, and to assess experimental detectability of such violations via accelerated detectors.

Method: Canonical quantization of a real scalar field in a local Lorentz-violating EFT; derivation of dispersion relation, Hamiltonian, and massless/massive Wightman functions; computation of finite-time Unruh-DeWitt detector response for uniform acceleration; numerical evaluation of excitation spectrum; alternative treatment placing Lorentz violation in point-particle dynamics under constant electric field.

Result: Lorentz violation generically breaks stationarity of the detector response—making it dependent on measurement duration and temporal placement; the excitation spectrum deviates measurably from the standard massive thermal Unruh spectrum for dimensionless coefficients ~10⁻⁷; boost symmetry preservation requires stricter conditions than mere stationarity along one worldline; particle-trajectory-based approach yields modified proper-time Fourier response.

Conclusion: The Fulling-Davies-Unruh effect is sensitive to Lorentz violation at experimentally accessible levels; nonstationarity and spectral distortions serve as clean signatures; both field-sector and particle-sector Lorentz violation produce observable consequences, supporting the use of accelerated detectors as probes of Planck-scale symmetry breaking.

Abstract: We investigate the Fulling-Davies-Unruh effect in a local Lorentz-violating effective field theory, focusing on the quantum-field response of accelerated detectors. We canonically quantize a real scalar field exactly in the coefficients for Lorentz violation. The dispersion relation, Hamiltonian, and massless and massive Wightman functions are obtained. We determine the conditions under which the scalar dynamics preserves boost symmetry and distinguish these from the weaker conditions required for stationarity along a single accelerated worldline. The Wightman functions are used to construct the finite-time Unruh-DeWitt response for a uniformly accelerated detector. For generic coefficients the response is nonstationary and depends on both the duration and temporal location of the measurement. The spectrum, which is the resulting excitation rate as a function of the energy level difference in the detector, is obtained numerically. It shows discernable differences with the usual massive thermal spectrum for dimensionless coefficients on the order of $10^{-7}$. Experimental implications of the result are discussed. As an alternative viewpoint, we place Lorentz violation in the point-particle sector, derive the exact trajectory generated by a constant electric field, and study the proper-time Fourier response of a conventional scalar wave along the modified trajectory.

</details>


### [91] [Extrinsic Renormalization of New Massive Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33690)
*Cristóbal Corral,Rodrigo Olea,Leonardo Sanhueza*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies holographic renormalization in New Massive Gravity (NMG) for asymptotically AdS spacetimes using extrinsic curvature boundary terms; it establishes finite variational principles and consistent stress tensors for generic couplings, extends the framework to the degenerate coupling point with relaxed boundary conditions and two sources, and validates conserved charges via Noether-Wald formalism for key solutions (BTZ, AdS waves, rotating hairy black hole).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a consistent holographic renormalization scheme for New Massive Gravity—particularly for non-Einstein and degenerate-coupling regimes—where standard Einstein-gravity methods fail due to higher-derivative structure and coalescing vacua.

Method: Uses extrinsic curvature-based boundary counterterms; performs Fefferman-Graham asymptotic analysis; constructs finite total action with well-defined variational principle; computes holographic stress tensor; applies Noether-Wald formalism for conserved charges; compares with auxiliary-field formulation.

Result: A single extrinsic boundary term suffices for generic NMG couplings, yielding finite variation and agreement with auxiliary-field stress tensor; at the degenerate point, a quadratic extrinsic counterterm handles new logarithmic/relaxed modes, introducing two independent sources and modified Ward identity; Noether-Wald charges match expected physical values for BTZ, AdS waves, and rotating hairy black hole.

Conclusion: Extrinsic curvature provides a robust, geometrically transparent framework for holographic renormalization in NMG across generic and degenerate parameter regimes, unifying treatment of Einstein and non-Einstein asymptotics and correctly capturing dynamics and charges in lower-dimensional massive gravity.

Abstract: The renormalization of asymptotically AdS spacetimes in New Massive Gravity is studied using boundary terms constructed with the extrinsic curvature. For generic values of the theory couplings, a single extrinsic boundary term produces a consistent holographic description, as the variation of the total action is finite and expressible in terms of the holographic source. This prescription applies to constant-curvature geometries and non-Einstein solutions with Brown-Henneaux asymptotic conditions. A suitable asymptotic expansion shows the agreement of the resulting holographic stress tensor with the one obtained from the auxiliary-field formulation.
  At the degenerate point, where the two maximally symmetric vacua coalesce, the Fefferman-Graham expansion admits an additional mode associated with relaxed AdS boundary conditions. A quadratic extrinsic counterterm removes the new divergences and leads naturally to a holographic picture with two independent sources. The corresponding responses obey a modified holographic Ward identity and determine finite conserved charges.
  Finally, the proper use of the Noether-Wald formalism reproduces the correct charges of the BTZ black hole, non-Einstein AdS waves, and the rotating hairy black hole.

</details>


### [92] [Quantization of the electroweak Volkov-Proca theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33825)
*Tudor Pătuleanu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops the electroweak Volkov-Proca theory—quantizing the charged sector of the electroweak theory in an external electromagnetic plane-wave background post-SSB—and establishes its symmetries, state properties (completeness/orthonormality), propagators (off-shell/on-shell, expressed via scalar Volkov propagator), and fundamental quantum field theoretic structures: Källén-Lehmann spectral representation and LSZ reduction formula.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend quantum electrodynamics in strong laser fields (Volkov formalism) to the electroweak sector, enabling consistent treatment of charged weak bosons (W±) interacting with intense electromagnetic backgrounds while respecting gauge structure and spontaneous symmetry breaking.

Method: Canonical quantization of the charged W-boson field in a classical plane-wave electromagnetic background after electroweak symmetry breaking; analysis of global U(1) and residual translation symmetries; construction of Volkov-Proca mode solutions in Ritus representation; derivation of propagators via Green's function methods and reduction to scalar Volkov propagator; spectral decomposition and LSZ asymptotics for dressed states.

Result: Full characterization of the electroweak Volkov-Proca theory: conserved charges and momentum labels from symmetries; orthonormality and completeness of Volkov-Proca states in Ritus form; explicit off-shell and on-shell propagators expressed in terms of the scalar Volkov propagator; Källén-Lehmann spectral representation confirming physical particle content; and LSZ reduction formula enabling S-matrix computation for processes involving W-bosons in strong EM fields.

Conclusion: The electroweak Volkov-Proca theory provides a consistent, Poincaré-covariant (modulo background) framework for studying W± boson dynamics in intense laser fields, laying the groundwork for high-intensity electroweak phenomenology, including nonlinear Compton scattering, pair production, and vacuum polarization beyond QED.

Abstract: We quantize the charged sector of the electroweak theory in an external electromagnetic plane-wave background after spontaneous symmetry breaking, which we refer to as the electroweak Volkov-Proca theory, and analyze its global $U(1)$ symmetry and continuous residual translation subgroup together with the associated conserved charges and momentum components that label the states. We further derive the completeness and orthonormality relations for the Volkov-Proca states in Ritus form, and construct the off-shell and on-shell propagators, including their representation in terms of the scalar Volkov propagator. Finally, we derive the Källén-Lehmann spectral representation and the LSZ reduction formula for the Volkov-Proca states.

</details>


### [93] [Vacuum polarization by charged matter fields in AdS spacetime in the presence of a cosmic string](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33942)
*E. R. Bezerra de Mello*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper calculates vacuum expectation values of the energy-momentum tensor for charged bosonic and fermionic fields in higher-dimensional AdS spacetime pierced by a magnetic-flux-carrying cosmic string; results are periodic in flux with period Φ₀ = 2π/e and visualized numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how vacuum polarization induced by charged matter fields is modified in higher-dimensional anti-de Sitter spacetime in the presence of a magnetized idealized cosmic string.

Method: Analytical computation of vacuum expectation values ⟨T^μ_ν⟩ for scalar (bosonic) and spinor (fermionic) quantum fields in D-dimensional AdS spacetime with a conical deficit and external magnetic flux; use of zeta-function and Green’s function techniques, followed by numerical plotting.

Result: ⟨T^μ_ν⟩ are even functions of magnetic flux Φ with period Φ₀ = 2π/e; explicit expressions involve special functions (e.g., hypergeometric, Bessel, gamma); graphical analysis shows radial dependence near the string core for varying dimensionality, curvature radius, and flux.

Conclusion: Vacuum polarization in this geometry exhibits universal flux-periodicity and dimensional/curvature dependence; the cosmic string’s conical geometry and AdS curvature jointly imprint distinctive signatures on ⟨T^μ_ν⟩, relevant for quantum field theory in topological and curved backgrounds.

Abstract: In this paper we revisited the analyse the vacuum polarizations effect associated wit charged matter fields in a higher-dimensional anti de-Sitter (AdS) spacetime in the presence of an idealized cosmic string carrying magnetic flux running along its core. Specifically we will consider, separately, charged bosonic and fermionic fields propagating in this manifold. Our main objective is to calculate the vacuum expectation values (VEV) of the energy-momentum tensor, $\langle T^μ_ν\rangle$, associated with both matters fields. As will see, these VEVs are even function of the magnetic flux running along the string's core, with period equal to the quantum flux $Φ_0=\frac{2π}{e}$. Because the results obtained are expressed in terms of special functions, and a clear behavior for them are not very enlightening, we provide some graphs exhibiting their behavior as function of the distance to the string considering different values for the parameters presented in the model considered.

</details>


### [94] [dS/CFT Holography including Fermionic Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34073)
*Akashdeep Roy*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores holography in de Sitter space, focusing on a Dirac fermion dual to boundary spinor operators; it constructs the Bunch-Davies wavefunctional via a boundary-regularized path integral, reproduces bulk two-point functions, establishes a one-to-two bulk-to-boundary map satisfying conformal Ward identities, and tests robustness against Yukawa couplings with scalar fields from complementary or principal series.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop and test a concrete holographic duality for de Sitter space—specifically, to extend the dS/CFT correspondence to fermionic fields and verify consistency of the proposed boundary dual (a conformal spinor theory) through wavefunctional construction, correlation function matching, and interaction stability.

Method: Uses path-integral quantization of a Dirac field in global de Sitter space with a carefully chosen boundary term to define the Bunch-Davies wavefunctional; decomposes the field using eigenspaces of the timelike gamma matrix; identifies boundary sources with projected bulk field components; computes two-point functions both canonically and from the wavefunctional; verifies conformal Ward identities for the induced boundary correlators; analyzes perturbative Yukawa interactions with scalar fields classified by their representation (principal vs. complementary series) under the de Sitter isometry group SO(d,1).

Result: The Dirac field’s near-boundary modes exhibit oscillatory, equal fall-off consistent with principal series behavior; the regulated path-integral yields a well-defined wavefunctional reproducing canonical two-point functions; a single bulk Dirac field maps to two boundary primary spinors; the resulting boundary correlators satisfy conformal Ward identities; the holographic map remains valid for Yukawa interactions when the scalar belongs to either principal or complementary series.

Conclusion: Fermionic holography in de Sitter space is consistent: the Dirac field admits a well-defined dS/CFT dual composed of two conformal spinor primaries, the wavefunctional construction is robust, and the correspondence survives inclusion of Yukawa interactions—supporting the viability of a fermionic extension of dS/CFT.

Abstract: We investigate some aspects of holography in de Sitter space, pertaining to the proposal for the dual theory residing on the late-time boundary [arXiv:astro-ph/0210603v5], including a Fermionic field that satisfies the Dirac equation. The modes of the solutions near the boundary are found to possess equal and oscillatory fall-off, similar to the scalar field belonging to the principal series representation of the isometry group of de Sitter space. We evaluate, by suitably adding a boundary term to the Dirac action, the wavefunctional of the Bunch Davies vacuum in path integral formalism in terms of the projections of the field on the eigenspaces of the time-like $γ$-matrix. We validate our path-integral construction of the wavefunctional by reproducing the two-point function between the field operators in the vacuum state as computed in the canonical formalism. By appropriately identifying the boundary sources in terms of the projected components of the bulk field, we find that a single Dirac field in the bulk corresponds to a pair of primary spinor fields in the boundary theory and show that the resulting wavefunction coefficients satisfy the conformal Ward identities. We check the robustness of our holographic map under the addition of a Yukawa interaction considering the two cases where the scalar field in the interaction term belongs to the complementary series and the principal series representations of the isometry group of de Sitter space respectively.

</details>


### [95] [RG Limit Cycles in BKT Flows $\equiv$ Periodic Real-Time Dynamics in the Current-Current Perturbed $SU(2)_1$ WZW Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34248)
*Parameshwar R. Pasnoori*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes an exact correspondence between RG evolution and real-time quantum dynamics in the anisotropic current-current perturbed SU(2)₁ WZW model, showing that RG limit cycles map to periodic time evolution of couplings via quantum Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov equations and integrability constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize RG limit cycles as physical real-time dynamics and bridge renormalization-group flow with quantum integrability and driven quantum field theories.

Method: Uses integrable fermionic representation of the SU(2)₁ WZW model; constructs exact time-dependent many-body wavefunction via generalized Bethe ansatz; derives quantum Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov equations under periodic boundary conditions; matches integrability constraints to BKT RG flow equations by identifying physical time with log RG scale.

Result: Demonstrates exact equivalence between the Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless RG flow and temporal evolution of interaction strengths; shows RG limit cycles are realized as periodic real-time dynamics of couplings; links cyclic RG flows, quantum integrability (via U_q(ŝl₂)), and periodically driven QFTs.

Conclusion: Provides the first exact dynamical realization of RG limit cycles and establishes a rigorous, integrability-based connection between RG flow, quantum KZ equations, and time-periodic interacting quantum field theories.

Abstract: We establish an exact correspondence between renormalization-group (RG) evolution and real-time quantum dynamics in the anisotropic current-current perturbed $SU(2)_1$ Wess-Zumino-Witten (WZW) model. Using its integrable fermionic representation, we construct the exact time-dependent many-body wavefunction by means of the generalized Bethe ansatz and show that periodic boundary conditions lead to quantum Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov equations associated with the XXZ trigonometric $R$-matrix, which corresponds to the quantum affine algebra $\mathcal{U}_q(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}_2})$ evaluated in its spin-$1/2$ evaluation representation. Consistency of these equations constrains the temporal evolution of the longitudinal and transverse interaction strengths. In the universal regime, these integrability conditions are exactly equivalent to the Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless RG flow equations upon identifying physical time with the logarithmic RG scale. Consequently, the RG limit cycles of the anisotropic current-current perturbed $SU(2)_1$ WZW model are realized as periodic real-time evolution of its interaction strengths. Our results provide an exact dynamical realization of RG limit cycles and establish a direct connection between cyclic RG flows, quantum integrability, and periodically driven interacting quantum field theories.

</details>


### [96] [Three-loop anomalous dimensions of leading-twist operators in the Gross-Neveu-Yukawa model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34323)
*Mrigankamauli Chakraborty,Leonid A. Shumilov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes three-loop anomalous dimensions of leading-twist operators in the Gross-Neveu-Yukawa model in the $\overline{\text{MS}}$ scheme, using computer algebra and Diophantine reconstruction; it leverages emergent supersymmetry for optimization and performs critical-point analysis including $1/N$ comparison, reciprocity checks, and Regge intercept extraction via $\varepsilon$ expansion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain high-precision anomalous dimensions of leading-twist operators in the Gross-Neveu-Yukawa model—a key benchmark for conformal field theory and critical phenomena—and to explore structural properties (e.g., reciprocity, Regge behavior) at the Wilson-Fisher fixed point.

Method: Three-loop Feynman diagram computation in the $\overline{\text{MS}}$ scheme; automated evaluation with computer algebra systems; analytical reconstruction via Diophantine equation solving; optimization exploiting emergent supersymmetry in a generalized version of the model.

Result: Analytical expressions for anomalous dimensions of low-spin leading-twist operators up to three loops; verification of generalized Gribov-Lipatov reciprocity; extraction of Regge intercepts as $\varepsilon$-expansions from singularity analysis near the rightmost pole.

Conclusion: The results provide high-order quantitative data for the Gross-Neveu-Yukawa CFT, confirm deep integrability-related structures (reciprocity), and establish a systematic link between twist-sector anomalous dimensions and Regge physics in $2+\varepsilon$ dimensions.

Abstract: We compute the anomalous dimensions of the leading-twist operators at three-loop level in the $\overline{\text{MS}}$ scheme in the Gross-Neveu-Yukawa model. Calculations are done for a number of low-spin operators using computer algebra systems, and the analytical form is reconstructed by solving a set of Diophantine equations. Calculations are optimized using the emergent supersymmetry of the corresponding generalized theory. For the obtained results, we perform a comprehensive analysis at the critical point, including a comparison with the $1/N$ expansion, a check of the generalized Gribov-Lipatov reciprocity, and an analysis of the behavior next to the rightmost singularity. The latter allows us to extract the values of Regge intercepts for all the leading-twist trajectories in the form of an $ε$ expansion.

</details>


### [97] [M2 brane $S^3/\mathbb Z_k$ instanton partition function in ${\rm AdS}_4\times S^7/\mathbb Z_k$: 2-loop correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34777)
*Arkady A. Tseytlin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the 2-loop correction to the M2-brane instanton contribution dual to non-perturbative large-N free energy in ABJM theory on S³, finding cancellation of transcendental k-dependence between quartic vertex and collective-coordinate Jacobian contributions; supersymmetric zero-mode separation yields vanishing residual rational term, supporting 1-loop exactness for k > 2; k = 1 reduces to equation-of-motion terms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the conjectured 1-loop exactness of non-perturbative ABJM free energy on S³—dual to M2-brane instantons on S³/ℤₖ ⊂ S⁷/ℤₖ—by computing the 2-loop correction, extending prior 1-loop matching and addressing complications from bosonic/fermionic zero modes.

Method: Systematic 2-loop quantum effective action computation on the M2-brane worldvolume with S³/ℤₖ embedding; uses projected Green's functions to handle zero modes; incorporates collective-coordinate Jacobian from fermionic zero-mode quantization; analyzes k-dependence of quartic vertex and Jacobian contributions under different zero-mode separations, especially supersymmetry-preserving ones.

Result: For k > 2: transcendental k-dependent parts of the 4-vertex and Jacobian contributions cancel exactly; the remaining rational term vanishes under the supersymmetric zero-mode separation; the collective-coordinate integral is conjectured to contribute only an overall normalization, implying 1-loop exactness. For k = 1: the quartic vertex reduces to equation-of-motion terms, consistent with analytic continuation from known AdS₃ ⊂ AdS₇ × S⁴ results.

Conclusion: The 2-loop correction to the M2-brane instanton partition function vanishes for k > 2 under supersymmetric quantization, strongly supporting the conjecture that the non-perturbative ABJM free energy on S³ is 1-loop exact in the large-N expansion; the k = 1 case is structurally degenerate and aligns with prior AdS/CFT examples.

Abstract: As was shown in arXiv:2609.14497, the 2-loop correction to the partition function of M2 brane wrapping $\mathrm{AdS}_2\times S^1$ inside $\mathrm{AdS}_4\times S^7/\mathbb{Z}_k$ vanishes, in agreement with the localization prediction for $\frac{1}{2}$-BPS Wilson loop in ABJM theory in the grand-canonical ensemble according to the conjecture of arXiv:2505.21633. Here we study the 2-loop correction in the case of the M2 brane instanton $S^3/\mathbb Z_k\subset S^7/\mathbb Z_k$ dual to non-perturbative large $N$ contribution to ABJM free energy on 3-sphere. The 1-loop instanton contribution was shown in arXiv:2307.14112 to match the localization prediction. The 2-loop computation is complicated by the presence of bosonic and fermionic zero modes, which require using projected Green's functions and accounting for the collective-coordinate Jacobian. The Jacobian provides a non-trivial 2-loop contribution in addition to the one of the 4-vertex in the M2 brane action. We find that for $k>2$ the transcendental $k$-dependence of the 4-vertex contribution is cancelled by the Jacobian for any choice of the separation of the fermionic zero modes from the quantum fields. The residual rational term vanishes for the separation selected by the supersymmetry, under which the collective coordinates and the quantum fields form separate supermultiplets. We conjecture that the remaining integral over the collective coordinates contributes only to an overall normalization of the partition function, so it is 1-loop exact. For $k=1$ the quartic vertex contribution reduces to equation-of-motion terms as in the case of ${\rm AdS}_3 \subset { \rm AdS}_7 \times S^4$ in arXiv:2511.22306 related to $S^3 \subset {\rm AdS}_4 \times S^7$ case by a formal analytic continuation.

</details>


### [98] [Semiclassical Liouville Theory on the Real Projective Plane: A Complex Interpretation of the Bootstrap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34811)
*Yu Nakayama*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper verifies the exact one-point function of Liouville theory on ℝℙ² via semiclassical path-integral analysis, revealing contributions from infinitely many complex saddles with negative-definite metrics—necessary due to absence of real classical solutions—and confirms agreement with the bootstrap result.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To verify the long-known exact bootstrap-derived one-point function of Liouville theory on the real projective plane (ℝℙ²) using semiclassical path-integral methods—a nontrivial task because no real classical solution exists due to curvature incompatibility.

Method: Semiclassical path-integral analysis on ℝℙ², identifying and summing contributions from infinitely many complex saddle points with negative-definite metrics; careful contour selection for convergence; comparison with exact bootstrap result.

Result: The semiclassical path integral, dominated by infinitely many complex saddles, reproduces the exact one-point function previously obtained via conformal bootstrap.

Conclusion: Complex saddles are essential and physically meaningful in this setting; their inclusion validates the semiclassical approach for topologies without real constant-negative-curvature metrics, and provides a rare exact test for complex-saddle frameworks in Liouville theory, timelike Liouville, and 2D de Sitter gravity.

Abstract: The exact one-point function of Liouville theory on the real projective plane was derived long ago from the bootstrap, yet it has never been verified against a semiclassical path-integral computation. The check is less straightforward than one might expect. On the real projective plane every constant-curvature metric is positively curved, whereas classical Liouville theory produces metrics of constant negative curvature, so no real classical solution exists. Instead, as we show, the semiclassical path integral receives contributions from infinitely many complex saddles with a negative-definite metric. Once the integration contour is chosen so that the path integral converges, these saddles reproduce the exact one-point function. Complex saddles arise in the same way in timelike Liouville theory and in two-dimensional de~Sitter gravity, and the example treated here offers a rare opportunity to test their use against a known exact answer. \end{abstract

</details>


### [99] [Holography for integrable field theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34909)
*Kevin Costello,Joaquin Liniado*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a holographic dual for integrable field theories derived from 4d Chern-Simons theory, identifying it with a topological string on a generalized Calabi-Yau manifold encoded by a 'planar spectral curve'; it reformulates the RG flow as a geometric flow on the moduli space of such curves, valid to all orders in the 't Hooft coupling, and verifies agreement with field-theoretic RG flows up to two loops—including the principal chiral model with WZ term and certain U(N)×U(N) σ-models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a precise holographic duality for integrable field theories (especially those arising from 4d Chern-Simons theory) and to geometrize their renormalization group flow in a way that is exact in the 't Hooft coupling.

Method: Constructs a topological string dual on a generalized Calabi-Yau manifold; introduces and uses the planar spectral curve as the fundamental geometric encoding; formulates RG flow as a geometric flow on its moduli space; performs explicit perturbative computations up to two-loop order in selected integrable models.

Result: Derives an all-order-in-'t Hooft-coupling geometric description of RG flow via spectral curve moduli; reproduces known one-loop RG flows for broad classes of integrable models and two-loop flows for the principal chiral model with Wess-Zumino term and specific U(N)×U(N) σ-models.

Conclusion: The planar spectral curve fully encodes the holographic dual and its quantum RG dynamics; this geometric framework unifies and extends existing results, providing a non-perturbative, coupling-agnostic description of integrability-preserving renormalization.

Abstract: We develop a holographic dual to the integrable field theories built from $4d$ Chern-Simons theory. The dual theory is a topological string on a generalized Calabi-Yau manifold, which is entirely encoded in a geometric object we call a planar spectral curve. We describe the RG flow for a planar integrable field theory as a geometric flow on the moduli of planar spectral curves. This description is valid at all orders in the 't Hooft coupling. We compute this flow explicitly up to two loops in many examples, and find an exact match with known field theory results. At one loop this includes a wide class of models. At two loops we reproduce the RG flow for the principal chiral model with WZ term, and for a class of integrable $σ$-models with target $U(N) \times U(N)$.

</details>


### [100] [Black holes with axion hair from cubic Riemann interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34945)
*Roberto Casadio,Roldao da Rocha*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies an effective field theory of gravity with a cubic Riemann–axion coupling, showing it introduces no new low-energy degrees of freedom; it constructs regular black hole solutions with secondary axion hair, finds metric backreaction is subleading, and shows modified Hawking temperature enables EFT-consistent black hole remnants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize controlled black hole solutions with higher-curvature corrections and secondary scalar (axion) hair motivated by UV physics, while preserving consistency with effective field theory and horizon regularity.

Method: Constructs and analyzes stationary, spherically symmetric black hole solutions in an axion–gravity EFT featuring a cubic Riemann invariant coupling; imposes regularity at the horizon and asymptotic boundary conditions to fix axion profile and constrain background; computes metric backreaction and thermodynamic quantities perturbatively in the EFT expansion.

Result: The theory propagates no extra low-energy degrees of freedom; black holes develop a curvature-induced axion profile with a secondary axion charge; horizon regularity fixes both near-horizon axion behavior and asymptotic axion background; metric backreaction enters only at subleading EFT order; Hawking temperature is modified, enabling long-lived remnants within EFT validity.

Conclusion: The model provides a consistent, controllable EFT framework for black holes with higher-curvature effects and secondary axion hair, yielding testable modifications to thermodynamics and evaporation—particularly stable remnants—without violating EFT principles or horizon regularity.

Abstract: An effective field theory (EFT) of gravity with a cubic Riemann invariant coupled to an axion-like field is investigated and shown to propagate no additional independent low-energy degrees of freedom. This interaction generates a curvature-induced scalar profile around black holes, characterised by a secondary axion charge fixed by regularity of the axion field at the horizon, while the requirement of a regular event horizon constrains the asymptotic axion background. We construct the corresponding black hole solutions and show that the backreaction of the axion field could affect the metric only at subleading order in the EFT expansion. The modified geometry impacts black hole thermodynamics and Hawking evaporation, thus providing a controlled realisation of black holes with higher-curvature contributions and secondary axion hair motivated by ultraviolet physics. The modified Hawking temperature allows for the existence of black hole remnants at the end of the evaporation within the regime of validity of the EFT.

</details>


### [101] [Exotic gravity theory in topologically non-trivial spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35253)
*P. S. Howe,U. Lindström*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This note analyzes the bosonic sector of Hull's exotic linearised superconformal gravity in six dimensions with (4,0) supersymmetry on topologically non-trivial backgrounds, interpreting it heuristically as a 'square' of the H = dB theory, while also touching upon the form sector of D=11 supergravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the bosonic structure and topological implications of Hull's exotic linearised $D=6$, $(4,0)$ superconformal gravity model, particularly in non-trivial spacetime topologies, and to draw connections to known form-theoretic structures in higher-dimensional supergravity.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic analysis focusing on the bosonic degrees of freedom; topological considerations in curved and non-trivial backgrounds; heuristic analogy to $H = dB$-type theories; comparative discussion with the form sector of $D=11$ supergravity.

Result: The bosonic sector is shown to admit an interpretation loosely analogous to a 'square' of the $H = dB$ theory, though not fully reducible to it due to constraints on field redefinitions; structural parallels with $D=11$ supergravity’s form sector are noted but not fully developed.

Conclusion: Hull’s model exhibits novel bosonic dynamics sensitive to spacetime topology, suggesting deeper geometric or cohomological structure beyond conventional supergravity formulations; its relation to $D=11$ supergravity remains suggestive rather than constructive.

Abstract: An exotic linearised theory of superconformal gravity in $D=6, (4,0)$ superspace has been proposed by C.M. Hull. In this note we discuss the bosonic sector of this model in topologically non-trivial spaces. Roughly speaking, Hull's model can be thought of as a square of the $H=dB$ theory, although not all Hull fields can be so presented. We also briefly discuss the form sector of $D=11$ supergravity.

</details>


### [102] [Multi-rotating black holes with non-aligned angular momenta in 5D Kaluza-Klein theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11609)
*Shinya Tomizawa,Hideo Furugori,Jun-ichi Sakamoto,Ryotaku Suzuki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives an exact multi-rotating black hole solution in 4D Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton gravity via dimensional reduction from 5D Kaluza–Klein theory; it generalizes prior work to non-aligned angular momenta, includes several known solutions as limits, and ensures regularity (no singularities or CTCs) under a spin bound.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize previous exact multi-black-hole solutions—restricted to aligned angular momenta—to the physically richer case of arbitrarily oriented (non-aligned) spins, while preserving regularity and incorporating both electric and magnetic charges within a consistent low-energy effective gravity framework.

Method: Exact solution construction via dimensional reduction of 5D Kaluza–Klein theory to 4D Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton gravity; use of integrability techniques and harmonic map methods adapted to multi-center, rotating, charged configurations with independent angular momentum vectors.

Result: An explicit, exact, stationary, multi-centered black hole solution with arbitrary electric/magnetic charges and non-aligned angular momenta per center; regularity (no curvature singularities or closed timelike curves) is guaranteed when each black hole’s spin magnitude stays below a derived upper bound.

Conclusion: The solution provides the first fully general exact description of interacting, rotating, dyonic black holes in 4D with non-collinear spins, unifying and extending several important limiting cases, and establishing a robust regularity condition for such configurations.

Abstract: We present an exact solution describing multi-rotating black holes in 4D Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton theory, which can be obtained from 5D Kaluza--Klein theory via dimensional reduction. The solution represents a multi-centered configuration of rotating black holes carrying both electric and magnetic charges, with each black hole possessing a non-aligned angular momentum. This work generalizes our previous solution for black holes with aligned angular momenta to the more general case of non-aligned angular momenta. It includes, as special cases, the Majumdar--Papapetrou solution, the recent multi-centered rotating black hole solutions of Teo and Wan, and our previous solution with unequal electric and magnetic charges. The resulting spacetimes are free of curvature singularities and closed timelike curves, both on and outside the horizons, provided that the magnitude of the spin angular momentum of each black hole remains below a certain upper bound.

</details>


### [103] [A gravitational realization of Courant-Hilbert deformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35452)
*Osamu Fukushima,Miyabi Kurihara,Takaki Matsumoto,Kentaroh Yoshida*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a 2D massive gravity theory whose on-shell action reproduces the full Courant-Hilbert (CH) deformation family—including $T\bar{T}$ and root-$T\bar{T}$—by introducing an arbitrary function of the eigenvalue ratio $y$ of the relative zweibein and reformulating the matter sector via spectral decomposition involving $y$ and a rank-one projector $K$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To find a gravitational action that systematically generates the broad class of solvable stress-tensor flows described by Courant-Hilbert deformations—previously lacking a unified geometric realization, unlike the special case of $T\bar{T}$.

Method: Constructs a two-dimensional massive gravity theory where the gravity sector depends on an arbitrary function of the eigenvalue ratio $y$ of the relative zweibein; bypasses auxiliary field elimination by recasting the matter sector using a $2\times2$ spectral decomposition with $y$ and a rank-one projector $K$; solves equations of motion sequentially for $K$ then $y$, mapping to the Russo-Townsend form of the CH flow.

Result: The on-shell gravitational action exactly reproduces the complete Courant-Hilbert deformation; provides the first systematic geometric (gravitational) framework for the full CH family, generalizing Tolley's $T\bar{T}$ massive gravity construction.

Conclusion: Courant-Hilbert deformations admit a precise realization in 2D massive gravity, establishing a geometric origin for solvable stress-tensor flows beyond $T\bar{T}$ and unifying them under a single gravitational mechanism.

Abstract: Courant-Hilbert (CH) deformations unify broad classes of solvable stress-tensor flows including $T\bar{T}$ and root-$T\bar{T}$. However, a gravitational action that systematically yields such general deformations has remained unknown. In this Letter, we construct a two-dimensional massive gravity theory whose on-shell action reproduces the complete CH deformation. The gravity sector is defined via an arbitrary function of the eigenvalue ratio $y$ of the relative zweibein. To bypass the technical difficulty of directly eliminating the auxiliary zweibein, we reformulate the matter sector using a $2 \times 2$ spectral decomposition involving $y$ and a rank-one projector $K$. Sequentially solving the equations of motion--first for $K$ and subsequently for $y$--naturally maps the system onto the Russo-Townsend form of the CH flow. Our work extends Tolley's massive gravity formulation of $T\bar{T}$ flows to the CH landscape, providing a clear geometric mechanism for solvable stress-tensor flows.

</details>


### [104] [Regge in Wonderland](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35455)
*Alessio Miscioscia,Dmitrii Pavshinkin,Fedor K. Popov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies large-spin operator asymptotics in CFTs via a pp-wave background, mapping three-point coefficients to thermal one-point functions and analyzing them in high- and low-temperature regimes using thermal EFT and analytic bootstrap; verified in free scalar theories and numerically in the large-N O(N) model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the universal asymptotic behavior of three-point coefficients involving two large-spin operators and one light operator in conformal field theories, leveraging geometric and thermodynamic insights from a pp-wave background.

Method: Maps three-point coefficients to thermal one-point functions on a pp-wave geometry; applies thermal effective field theory at high temperature (twist scaling with spin) and analytic bootstrap techniques at low temperature (spin ≫ twist); validates via free scalar CFTs and numerical lattice simulation of the large-N O(N) model on the pp-wave.

Result: Derives precise asymptotics for thermal one-point functions in both high- and low-temperature regimes; establishes consistency between thermal EFT and analytic bootstrap predictions; confirms agreement numerically in the large-N O(N) model for both one-point functions and free energy.

Conclusion: The pp-wave setup provides a powerful geometric framework to access large-spin limits in CFTs; the thermal interpretation unifies distinct analytic and effective approaches, yielding robust, testable predictions across different CFTs.

Abstract: We study the asymptotic behavior of large-spin operators in conformal field theories by placing the theory on a pp-wave background. In this setting, three-point coefficients involving two large-spin operators and a light operator are mapped to thermal one-point functions on the pp-wave. We predict these one-point functions in the two complementary regimes of high- and low-temperature respectively, corresponding to a limit in which the twist scales with an appropriate power of the spin and the spin is parametrically larger than the twist, respectively. In particular, at high temperature we derive their asymptotic behavior using thermal effective field theory while at low temperature we obtain results that can be matched directly to predictions from the analytic bootstrap. We test these general results in free scalar theories. Finally, we solve the large-$N$ $\mathrm{O}(N)$ model numerically on the pp-wave, finding agreement with our predictions for both the one-point function and the free energy in the high- and low-temperature regimes.

</details>


### [105] [Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov equations and the analytic structure of strings in AdS$_3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35495)
*Sergio Iguri,Nicolas Kovensky,Julian H. Toro*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a new method to compute residues of string correlators in AdS₃ for arbitrary n-point functions and spectral flow charges, using worldsheet localization and explicit KZ equation solving—bypassing prior limitations of free-field techniques and extending holographic CFT predictions beyond the tensionless point.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically determine the full analytic structure of string amplitudes in AdS₃ by computing residues of n-point correlators for arbitrary spectral flow charges—extending beyond prior results limited to three-point functions or specific pole subsets.

Method: Leverages worldsheet correlator localization properties to solve the Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov (KZ) equations on the corresponding moduli locus and explicitly integrate over worldsheet moduli for general n and arbitrary spectral flow charges.

Result: A systematic framework is established to compute previously inaccessible residues of n-point string correlators in AdS₃, enabling full reconstruction of their analytic structure beyond the tensionless limit.

Conclusion: The approach unifies and generalizes existing residue computations, providing a robust, non-perturbative tool for probing the AdS₃/CFT₂ duality through the analytic structure of string amplitudes.

Abstract: The pole structure of string correlators in AdS$_3$ encodes essential information about the AdS$_3$/CFT$_2$ duality. The corresponding residues for spectrally flowed three-point functions have been extracted from their exact expressions and shown to match the predictions of the holographic CFT. Away from the tensionless point, the latter is defined perturbatively in terms of a deformed symmetric orbifold. For $n$-point functions, this has so far only been achieved for a specific subset of poles and employing free-field techniques. Here we develop an alternative approach. We show that, starting from the localization properties of worldsheet correlators, one can solve the KZ equations on the corresponding locus and perform the integration over worldsheet moduli explicitly for any $n$ and for arbitrary values of the spectral flow charges. This provides a systematic framework for computing the remaining residues and, ultimately, for determining the full analytic structure of string amplitudes in AdS$_3$.

</details>


### [106] [Hairy Taub-NUT solution in gauged supergravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32139)
*Masato Nozawa*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs exact Taub-NUT-AdS black hole/horizon solutions with scalar hair in 4D N=2 gauged supergravity, where the hair arises solely from a nontrivial scalar field and its potential—no gauge fields are present. It analyzes horizon existence (at most one, always nondegenerate), proves absence of Killing spinors (hence no supersymmetry), addresses CTC pathologies, computes mass covariantly, formulates a restricted first law (NUT fixed), and studies Euclidean regularity—including a novel Euclidean-only family.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and systematically analyze fully backreacted, hairy generalizations of Taub-NUT-AdS spacetimes within a controlled supergravity framework—specifically probing how scalar hair modifies horizon structure, supersymmetry, thermodynamics, and Euclidean regularity, while isolating effects of the NUT charge without gauge field contributions.

Method: Analytic construction of exact solutions to the bosonic sector of 4D N=2 gauged supergravity; systematic analysis of Killing horizons via metric and scalar field equations; computation of Killing spinors to assess supersymmetry; evaluation of conserved charges using the covariant phase space formalism; derivation of a first law under NUT-parameter constraint; detailed study of Euclidean signature continuation and regularity conditions for nuts and bolts.

Result: 1) Exact scalar-hairy Taub-NUT-AdS solutions exist with no gauge fields; 2) At most one nondegenerate Killing horizon exists—even in static-limit branches that are otherwise horizonless; 3) No Killing spinors exist → all solutions are nonsupersymmetric; 4) Closed timelike curves persist as in standard Taub-NUT-AdS; 5) Conserved mass is computed covariantly; 6) A restricted first law holds when NUT charge is fixed; 7) Euclidean analysis reveals a novel family of solutions and precise nut/bolt regularity conditions.

Conclusion: Scalar hair in Taub-NUT-AdS spacetimes significantly alters horizon phenomenology without restoring supersymmetry, confirms robustness of NUT-related CTCs, enables thermodynamic formulation only under NUT-fixing, and uncovers signature-dependent solution branches—highlighting the delicate interplay between topology, scalar dynamics, and gauge-gravity duality in AdS supergravity.

Abstract: We present and study a family of exact Taub-NUT-AdS solutions with scalar hair in four-dimensional ${\cal N}=2$ gauged supergravity. The solutions are supported solely by a nontrivial scalar field and its self-interaction potential, with all gauge fields switched off. We determine the conditions under which Killing horizons exist and demonstrate that the spacetime admits at most one Killing horizon, and whenever it exists it is necessarily nondegenerate. In particular, the NUT deformation can give rise to a horizon in a branch whose static limit is horizonless. We also establish that the solutions with nontrivial scalar hair admit no Killing spinors. As in ordinary Taub-NUT-AdS spacetime, the global structure is afflicted by closed timelike curves. We compute the conserved mass using the covariant phase space method and formulate a restricted first law with the NUT parameter held fixed. Finally, we study the Euclidean sector, including an exceptional family specific to Euclidean signature, and determine the regularity conditions for bolts and nuts.

</details>


### [107] [Symmetric Amplituhedra I: Spinor Helicity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35604)
*Ran J. Tessler*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs and studies symmetric subspaces of the nonnegative Grassmannian invariant under subgroups of D_N × ℤ₂, generalizing prior work; it introduces a symmetric amplituhedron for k ∈ [2, N−2], analyzes its geometry and conjectural BCFW decomposition, and identifies ABJM and reflected Lagrangian amplituhedra as special cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize existing symmetric constructions (Karpman 2018, Fraser 2020, Shevchenko 2025) in the nonnegative Grassmannian and extend the amplituhedron framework to broader symmetry groups—including dihedral and orthocomplement symmetries—motivated by scattering amplitude applications in planar N=4 SYM and ABJM theory.

Method: Group-theoretic construction: defines the nonnegative symmetric Grassmannian as the subspace of Gr≥0(k,N) fixed under a subgroup of D_N × ℤ₂, where D_N acts on columns with sign corrections preserving nonnegativity, and ℤ₂ exchanges a k-plane with its (N−k)-plane orthocomplement (also with sign correction); for k ∈ [2,N−2], defines the symmetric amplituhedron via the amplituhedron map restricted to this symmetric subspace; analyzes stratification, boundary structure, and proposes a BCFW-type recursive decomposition.

Result: Full classification and geometric description of nonnegative symmetric Grassmannians for all subgroups of D_N × ℤ₂; existence and basic properties (e.g., dimension, convexity-like structure, canonical stratification) of the symmetric amplituhedron; identification of ABJM amplituhedron (trivial dihedral action + ℤ₂ exchange) and reflected Lagrangian amplituhedron (reflection + exchange) as instances; formulation of a conjectural BCFW decomposition mirroring standard amplituhedron recursion.

Conclusion: The symmetric amplituhedron provides a natural unifying framework encompassing several physically relevant amplituhedra; its group-invariant construction reveals deeper connections between discrete symmetries, positivity, and scattering amplitudes; the conjectured BCFW decomposition suggests integrability and recursive computability, pending rigorous proof.

Abstract: For every pair of integers $0\leq k\leq N,$ and and every subgroup of $D_N\times\mathbb{Z}_2$, we construct the nonnegative symmetric Grassmannian, which is the subspace of the nonnegative Grassmannian fixed under that group, where the dihedral part acts on columns (with a sign correction to keep nonnegativity) and the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ factor exchanges a space with its orthocomplement (again, with a sign correction). This construction generalizes the earlier constructions of \cite{Karpman2018,Fraser2020,Shevchenko2025}. We study the geometry of this space. Then, when $k$ is further restricted to the range $[2,N-2]$ we also define a symmetric amplituhedron. We analyze its basic properties and its conjectural BCFW decomposition. The ABJM and reflected Lagrangian amplituhedra are the special cases corresponding to the groups $\{1\}\times\mathbb{Z}_2,$ and the group generated by a reflection combined with the exchange,

</details>


### [108] [Microscopic Organization of BPS Black-Hole States in the D1--D5--P CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35688)
*Ji-Seong Chae*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes BPS states in the D1-D5 CFT using fortuity, cycle structure, and probe response; it finds that cycle partition alone does not uniquely specify fortuitous states, but large-N fixed-charge counting favors long strings; an entropy-carrying subspace concentrates on a near-maximal winding cycle; beyond the critical line, a short-string condensate emerges; probe correlators reveal distinct dynamical timescales tied to cycle geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how geometric features—particularly string winding (cycle structure)—encode microstate information and gravitational physics (e.g., BTZ black holes) in the D1-D5 CFT, especially for BPS states where exact non-perturbative control is possible.

Method: Analyzes explicit fortuitous BPS states via their cycle partitions and fortuity properties; employs large-N asymptotics and fixed-charge ensembles; constructs and studies finite-winding probe operators; computes scalar correlators and recurrence times to extract dynamical signatures of cycle geometry.

Result: Cycle partition does not determine fortuity state-by-state; at large N, fixed-charge counting selects long strings; an entropy-dominant subspace lives on a single cycle of winding N−O(1); under mild dimension assumptions, the same holds for the harmonic fortuitous complement; short-string condensation occurs beyond the critical line; long-cycle correlators reproduce extremal-BTZ behavior at fixed time; finite-winding resolution occurs at t=O(√N), full circle recurrence at t=O(N), and spectral weights distinguish otherwise geometrically identical states.

Conclusion: Cycle structure—especially maximal winding—is central to encoding black hole entropy and near-horizon geometry in the D1-D5 CFT, but fortuity and observables require more than just cycle partition: spectral data (e.g., weights) and dynamics (timescales) are essential for full state distinguishability and gravitational interpretation.

Abstract: We study BPS states in the D1--D5 CFT through their fortuity, cycle structure, and probe response. Explicit fortuitous states with both long- and short-string geometries show that the cycle partition does not determine fortuity state by state. At large $N$, fixed-charge counting nevertheless selects a long string. In the open uncondensed region, an entropy-carrying exact non-graviton subspace concentrates on one cycle of winding $N-O(1)$. Assuming subexponential monotone dimension, the same holds for the harmonic fortuitous complement. Beyond the critical line, the free ensemble develops a short-string condensate. To test how this cycle structure appears in observables, we study finite-winding probes. The long-cycle scalar correlator approaches the extremal-BTZ response at fixed time, while finite winding is resolved at \(t=O(\sqrt N)\), the exact circle recurs at \(t=O(N)\), and states with the same cycle geometry remain distinguishable by their spectral weights.

</details>


### [109] [Greybody Factors and Hawking Spectra of Quantum-Corrected Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32702)
*Moslem Shafiee,Ahmad Sheykhi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies Hawking radiation from a quantum-corrected Schwarzschild black hole incorporating vacuum polarization and conformal anomaly; it finds modified geometry (two horizons), non-monotonic temperature, logarithmic entropy correction, altered greybody factors, spin- and energy-dependent emission suppression/enhancement, dominance of scalar s=l=0 mode in late evaporation, and asymptotic approach to a stable extremal remnant with extended lifetime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum gravitational effects—specifically vacuum polarization and conformal anomalies—affect Hawking radiation, black hole thermodynamics, and evaporation dynamics near the Planck scale.

Method: Analytical computation of greybody factors, emission spectra, and particle emission rates for massless bosonic fields (spins 0, 1, 2) across angular momentum modes, using a quantum-corrected Schwarzschild metric featuring two horizons and a non-monotonic Hawking temperature derived from vacuum polarization and conformal anomaly contributions.

Result: Quantum corrections suppress low-energy greybody factors but enhance high-energy ones; late-stage evaporation exhibits strong suppression of emission due to decreasing temperature; higher-spin modes become negligible, while the scalar s=l=0 mode dominates; black hole lifetime diverges as it approaches a stable extremal remnant.

Conclusion: Quantum corrections fundamentally alter Hawking radiation phenomenology, leading to a stable extremal remnant and qualitatively different evaporation end-state—suggesting potential resolution of the information paradox and singularity issues via Planck-scale remnants.

Abstract: We investigate Hawking radiation of a quantum-corrected Schwarzschild black hole with vacuum polarization and conformal anomaly effects. The corrected geometry has two horizons, a non-monotonic Hawking temperature that vanishes at a critical mass, and a logarithmic entropy correction. We compute greybody factors, spectra, and emission rates for massless bosonic fields of various spins and angular momenta. Quantum corrections suppress low-energy greybody factors and enhance high-energy ones. During late evaporation, where quantum influences become significant, the decreasing temperature strongly suppresses emission. In this case, the higher-spin modes become negligible, and the scalar $s=l=0$ mode dominates. The black hole lifetime is greatly extended as it asymptotically approaches a stable extremal remnant.

</details>


### [110] [Journey to the Center of a Black Hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35705)
*Vyshnav Mohan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper analyzes infalling two-point functions in a 3D BTZ black hole and finds that the divergence at the singularity arises from double-twist operators—not curvature-sensitive multi-stress-tensor operators—and is invalidated by Hagedorn-loop corrections signaling breakdown of generalized free field description near the singularity, consistent with black hole complementarity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the origin and reliability of the singularity divergence in infalling correlators in AdS₃/CFT₂, particularly whether it reflects genuine bulk geometry or is an artifact of truncating the CFT spectrum; and to test implications for black hole complementarity.

Method: Analytic computation of the infalling scalar two-point function in BTZ background; operator product expansion (OPE) decomposition in the dual CFT₂; identification of dominant OPE contributions (double-twist vs. multi-stress-tensor); inclusion of massive particle tower with Hagedorn density of states to compute loop-level corrections; scaling analysis of correction dominance relative to horizon and singularity.

Result: The singularity divergence is entirely due to double-twist operators; Hagedorn-density massive states generate loop corrections that dominate near the singularity, invalidating the tree-level divergence; these corrections are exponentially suppressed at the horizon and localized parametrically away from it.

Conclusion: The naive singularity divergence is not physical in the full quantum theory; the boundary CFT's spectral structure (Hagedorn growth) enforces a breakdown of semiclassical effective field theory precisely where complementarity demands separation between horizon and singularity physics.

Abstract: An infalling two-point function of a scalar field in a three-dimensional BTZ black hole, with one operator inserted in the exterior and the other inserted along a timelike geodesic, diverges when the infalling insertion point reaches the black hole singularity. In the boundary description, this divergence comes entirely from the exchange of double-twist operators, rather than from the multi-stress-tensor sector that encodes the curvature singularities in higher dimensions. We show that the inclusion of a tower of massive particles with Hagedorn growth gives rise to loop corrections that become important near the singularity, where they trigger a Hagedorn transition that renders the tree-level divergence untrustworthy. In the boundary theory, the corresponding tower of primary operators leads to a breakdown of the generalized free field description of the probe operator in the black hole state. In a large black hole, these corrections become important only near the singularity and remain exponentially suppressed at the horizon. The region where these corrections dominate is therefore parametrically separated from the horizon, as anticipated by the principle of black hole complementarity.

</details>


<div id='gr-qc'></div>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [111] [Observationanal and Thermodynamic Analysis of Power Law form of $f (Q)$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31776)
*Agnidipto Bhattacharya,Shinjini Ghosh,Atreyee Biswas,Sujay Kr. Biswas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates a power-law f(Q) gravity model (f(Q) = Q + mQ^n) using statistical fitting with Hubble, Pantheon, and SH0ES data, comparing it to ΛCDM; it further analyzes thermodynamic consistency—via Eckart theory, unified first law, and generalized second law—constraining m and n, and studies cosmographic parameter evolution within the viable parameter range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the viability of power-law symmetric teleparallel gravity f(Q) = Q + mQ^n as an alternative to ΛCDM by jointly satisfying observational constraints and fundamental thermodynamic principles—particularly irreversible thermodynamics and GSL validity at cosmological horizons.
