Method: Statistical analysis using 31-point H(z) data, Pantheon SNe Ia, and 1721-point SH0ES data for parameter estimation; independent thermodynamic analysis employing Eckart’s non-equilibrium formalism, unified first law of thermodynamics to derive horizon entropy, and GSL validation to constrain m and n; subsequent cosmographic analysis (e.g., q, j, s) over the thermodynamically and statistically allowed parameter space.

Result: Best-fit parameters m and n are obtained from joint statistical analysis; the model shows competitive goodness-of-fit relative to ΛCDM; thermodynamic analysis yields physically viable ranges for m and n ensuring positive entropy production and GSL compliance; cosmographic parameters exhibit observationally consistent evolutionary behavior (e.g., transition redshift, jerk parameter) within the constrained (m,n) domain.

Conclusion: The power-law f(Q) gravity model f(Q) = Q + mQ^n is observationally and thermodynamically viable for specific bounded values of m and n, offering a self-consistent alternative to ΛCDM that satisfies both cosmological data and irreversible thermodynamic requirements at the apparent horizon.

Abstract: In this study, the power law form of f(Q) gravity $(f(Q)=Q+mQ^n)$ has been studied in view of statistical analysis with current observed data and irreversible thermodynamics. The best fit values of model specific parameters are found with the use of of 31point Hubble data, Patntheon type Supernva and 1721 point SH0ES data and a comparative study has been performed between $f(Q)$ and standard $Λ$CDM model. The thermodynamic consequence using standard Eckart theory of non-equilibrium thermodynamics has been examined independently for general form of $f(Q)$ gravity followed by power law form of $f(Q)$ where the horizon entropy is calculated using unified law of thermodynamics. Suitable range of power law model specific parameters has been explored through investigating validity of generalized second law of thermodynamics and thermodynamic equilibrium. Finally evolution nature of cosmographic parameters are studied within the specific range of $m,n$ which is compatible with the results obtained from both statistical and thermodynamical analysis.

</details>


### [112] [Chaos bound for spinning particles in a hairy black hole with two photon spheres](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31791)
*Chuanhong Gao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies orbital chaos bounds for spinning test particles in a hairy black hole spacetime with two photon spheres, finding that the inner branch of unstable circular orbits always satisfies the chaos bound while the outer branch can violate it—unlike prior near-horizon-focused studies. Total angular momentum enlarges bound violations on the outer branch; the background deformation parameter modulates the bound via Lyapunov exponent and surface gravity; particle spin has only a weak, shifting effect via spin-curvature coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how the classical orbital chaos bound depends on orbital branch (inner vs. outer unstable circular orbits) in a hairy black hole spacetime with double photon sphere structure, extending beyond prior near-horizon violation analyses.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of geodesic deviation and Lyapunov exponents for spinning test particles in a hairy black hole metric with two photon spheres; classification of unstable circular orbit branches; evaluation of chaos bound (λ ≤ 2πT_H) across parameter space including particle angular momentum, spin, and background deformation.

Result: The inner branch of unstable circular orbits always satisfies the chaos bound; violations occur exclusively on the outer branch. Total angular momentum enlarges the bound-violating region on the outer branch. The deformation parameter controls validity by jointly tuning orbital Lyapunov exponent and black hole surface gravity. Particle spin induces only small shifts in orbit locations and critical boundaries via spin-curvature coupling.

Conclusion: The orbital chaos bound exhibits nontrivial branch dependence in multi-photon-sphere spacetimes, with outer-branch violations driven by angular momentum and geometry deformation—highlighting limitations of near-horizon approximations and underscoring the role of global orbital structure in chaos diagnostics.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the unstable circular orbits of spinning test particles and the associated classical orbital chaos bound in a hairy black hole spacetime admitting a double photon sphere structure. This background supports two distinct branches of unstable circular orbits, referred to as the inner and outer branches. We find that, within the parameter ranges considered, the inner branch always satisfies the chaos bound, whereas violations can occur on the outer branch. This behavior contrasts with many previous studies focusing primarily on near-horizon violations of the chaos bound and reveals a clear dependence of the bound on the orbital branch. Our analysis further shows that the total angular momentum of the particle has a pronounced effect on the local orbital instability and can enlarge the bound-violating region on the outer branch. Meanwhile, the deformation parameter of the background geometry regulates the validity of the bound by modifying both the orbital Lyapunov exponent and the black hole surface gravity. By contrast, the effect of the particle spin is comparatively weak and mainly shifts the existing unstable circular orbits and the corresponding critical boundaries through the spin-curvature coupling.

</details>


### [113] [Cosmological Dynamics and Observational Constraints of a Logarithmic Herglotz $f(R,T)$ Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31800)
*Vishal M C,Sankarsan Tarai*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies a logarithmic Herglotz-type f(R,T) gravity model (f(R,T) = R + β ln(−T/T₀)) to explain late-time cosmic acceleration, formulates it as a dynamical system, constrains it with CC, BAO (DESI DR2), and SN Ia (Union3) data via Bayesian MCMC, and finds it consistent with observations—yielding w_eff,0 ≈ −0.702 and a viable transition from deceleration to acceleration—while deviating from ΛCDM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an alternative to ΛCDM that explains late-time cosmic acceleration without a cosmological constant, using a theoretically motivated extension of f(R,T) gravity incorporating Herglotz formalism and logarithmic coupling to the trace T of the energy-momentum tensor.

Method: Formulated modified Friedmann equations for f(R,T) = R + β ln(−T/T₀) in flat FLRW geometry with dust; recast them as a coupled autonomous dynamical system involving a Herglotz field and normalized matter density; adopted a phenomenological closure for the effective Herglotz sector; solved numerically and constrained parameters (β, H₀, Ωₘ₀) using Cosmic Chronometers, DESI DR2 BAO, and Union3 SNe Ia within a Bayesian MCMC framework.

Result: Combined dataset analysis yields statistically consistent background evolution; reconstructed expansion history gives present-day effective equation of state w_eff,0 ≈ −0.702; shows correct deceleration-to-acceleration transition; Om(z) and statefinder diagnostics confirm deviation from ΛCDM while remaining observationally viable.

Conclusion: The logarithmic Herglotz-type f(R,T) model offers a phenomenologically viable and self-consistent alternative to ΛCDM for describing homogeneous, isotropic late-time cosmic expansion, with testable deviations encoded in w_eff(z), Om(z), and statefinder parameters.

Abstract: We investigate the cosmological dynamics and observational viability of a logarithmic Herglotz-type $f(R,T)$ gravity model described by $f(R,T)=R+β\ln(-T/T_0)$. Assuming a spatially flat Friedmann--Lemaître--Robertson--Walker background and pressureless matter, the modified field equations are formulated as a coupled dynamical system involving the Herglotz field and the normalized matter density. A phenomenological closure relation is adopted for the effective Herglotz sector, allowing the background evolution to be determined numerically. The model is constrained using Cosmic Chronometer measurements, the DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillation compilation, and the Union3 supernova sample, considering both individual and combined datasets within a Bayesian Markov-chain Monte Carlo framework. The combined analysis provides a statistically consistent description of the background expansion. The reconstructed expansion history yields a present-day effective equation of state $w_{\rm eff,0}\simeq-0.702$ and exhibits the expected transition from accelerated expansion at late times toward decelerated expansion at higher redshifts. We further investigate the $Om(z)$ diagnostic and statefinder parameter. These results indicate that the logarithmic Herglotz model provides a consistent phenomenological description of the homogeneous and isotropic late-time expansion history, while allowing departures from the standard $Λ$CDM background.

</details>


### [114] [Identifiability Limits of Gravitational Wave Phase Deviations: Multiclass Classification with a Multihead Neural Network](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31993)
*Lavinia Heisenberg,Shayan Hemmatyar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper evaluates a neural network with dual classification and regression heads to detect and quantify gravitational-wave phase deviations from general relativity using waveform mismatch response functions; it achieves moderate classification accuracy (0.427–0.329) on simulated and real LIGO noise, attributes misclassifications to intrinsic similarities among post-Einsteinian phase families, and shows that performance is fundamentally limited by physics—not architecture—when source parameters are known.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop and assess a data-driven method for detecting and characterizing deviations from general relativity in gravitational-wave signals, specifically phase modifications described by parametrized post-Einsteinian (ppE) models, leveraging machine learning to overcome limitations of traditional matched-filtering approaches.

Method: A neural network with joint classification (6 ppE families + GR) and regression (log10|β|) heads is trained on response functions — sensitivity maps of waveform mismatch to phase deformations. Training/testing uses Gaussian noise (Advanced LIGO design spectrum, 262 sources; O3 Hanford spectrum, 132 sources) and real Hanford strain injections. Performance is benchmarked against an approximate classifier built from analytic waveform residuals; thirteen network architectures (including recurrent) are compared.

Result: Classification accuracies: 0.427 (design spectrum) and 0.329 (O3 Hanford spectrum); regression predicts log10|β| with reasonable fidelity. Recurrent network yields highest mean accuracy for loud deviations, yet all variants underperform the approximate residual-based classifier. Misclassifications align between both classifiers and correlate with residual similarity among neighboring b-exponents (e.g., b = −3 vs. −1). Networks trained on real noise generalize well to recorded strain.

Conclusion: The identification of ppE phase deviations is fundamentally constrained by degeneracies among similar waveform distortions—not by neural network capacity—when intrinsic parameters (masses, spins) are known. The response-function representation enables robust, noise-agnostic detection, and the dual-head architecture provides interpretable, physics-informed inference.

Abstract: We study how well gravitational-wave phase deviations can be identified using a neural network with classification and regression heads. The classification head distinguishes general relativity (GR) from six parametrized post-Einsteinian phase families with exponents $b\in\{-7,-5,-3,-1,+1,+2\}$; the regression head predicts the logarithm of the coupling magnitude, $\log_{10}|β|$. The network input is a response function quantifying the sensitivity of waveform mismatch to waveform deformations. We use stationary Gaussian noise with the Advanced LIGO design spectrum for 262 sources and a measured Hanford spectrum from the third observing run (O3) for 132 sources, and also test injections into recorded Hanford strain. On the two Gaussian datasets, the network identifies the exponent of deviations clearly above the noise with accuracies of $0.427$ and $0.329$. To interpret these results, we compare the network with an approximate classifier built from predicted waveform residuals for the six phase families and same sources. The two classifiers often confuse the same families. Examining residuals left by different phase corrections, we link these errors to similarities between the underlying deviations. On selected recorded Hanford strain, networks trained on simulated or recorded noise achieve overall classification accuracies close to those on simulated Hanford noise. Of thirteen neural-network variants tested on the Advanced LIGO design dataset, the recurrent network has the highest mean classification accuracy for loud deviations, although all variants remain below the approximate classifier. The network nearly matches that classifier's accuracy, indicating that identification is limited by similarities between neighboring phase families rather than by the network, for known intrinsic source parameters.

</details>


### [115] [Relational Frame Equivalence and Exact Cosmology in Brans--Dicke Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32047)
*Yaser Tavakoli*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs an exactly solvable relational cosmological model in Brans–Dicke theory using a canonical clock-sector framework, yielding closed-form expressions for key observables (e.g., scale factor, Hubble rate, e-folds) and demonstrating frame equivalence between Jordan and Einstein representations via clock-adapted phase-space reduction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain an explicit cosmological realization of frame-adapted relational dynamics in scalar–tensor gravity and derive closed-form cosmological observables within Brans–Dicke FLRW cosmology.

Method: Applies the canonical clock-sector framework to spatially flat FLRW Brans–Dicke cosmology; reduces full ADM theory to homogeneous sector; identifies clock momentum $P_Φ = p_φ - a p_a / (2φ)$; works with a quadratic Jordan-frame potential (yielding constant Einstein-frame potential); solves the relational system exactly using a single parameter $u$; compares exact solution with numerical integrations in Jordan/Einstein frames and naive reduction.

Result: Exact integrability of the relational FLRW system; closed-form analytic expressions for scale factor $a(u)$, cosmic-time Hubble rate $H(u)$, slow-roll parameter $ε(u)$, e-folds $N(u)$, and scalar equation-of-state $w_φ(u)$; identification of an exact inflationary regime starting at $u = \operatorname{arctanh}(1/\sqrt{3})$ and asymptoting to de Sitter; numerical confirmation of trajectory equivalence across clock-adapted Jordan/Einstein reductions.

Conclusion: The model serves as an exactly solvable laboratory for testing relational frame equivalence in scalar–tensor cosmology; provides explicit, parameterized cosmological observables that are amenable to generalization to broader potentials and scalar–tensor theories.

Abstract: We apply the canonical clock-sector framework developed in Ref.~\cite{Tavakoli:2026coc} to spatially flat FLRW cosmology in Brans--Dicke theory. The aim is to obtain an explicit cosmological realization of the frame-adapted relational dynamics and to determine the corresponding cosmological observables in closed form. Starting from the previously developed full ADM theory and restricting it to the homogeneous sector, we obtain the canonical clock momentum as $P_Φ=p_φ-ap_a/2φ$. This identification allows the Jordan and Einstein-frame reduced systems to be represented on a common canonical phase space. We then consider a quadratic Jordan-frame potential for which the Einstein-frame potential is constant. The resulting relational FLRW system is exactly integrable. The complete trajectory can be expressed in terms of a single parameter $u$, yielding closed-form expressions for the scale factor, the cosmic-time Hubble rate, the Hubble slow-roll parameter, the number of e-folds, and the scalar equation-of-state parameter. In particular, the exact solution provides an explicit inflationary regime that begins at $u=\operatorname{arctanh}(1/\sqrt{3})$ and transitions toward an asymptotic de Sitter regime. We finally compare the exact solution with direct numerical integration in the adapted Jordan and Einstein descriptions, and with the naively reduced Jordan system based on the unshifted scalar momentum. The numerical results reproduce the exact trajectory and illustrate how the different reduced canonical representations are related by the clock-dependent transformation implied by the canonical clock-sector construction. The analysis provides an exactly solvable laboratory for relational frame equivalence in scalar--tensor cosmology and gives explicit cosmological observables that can be used in extensions to more general potentials and scalar--tensor models.

</details>


### [116] [Inhomogeneous dust collapse in five-dimensional AdS-Chern-Simons gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32200)
*Luis Avilés,José Díaz,Cristian Quinzacara,Patricio Salgado*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies gravitational collapse of a 5D spherically symmetric dust cloud in AdS Chern–Simons gravity, deriving an exact interior solution in torsion-free LTB coordinates; it identifies shell-focusing curvature singularities, establishes regularity and energy condition preservation, and determines apparent horizon formation via a quasilocal mass threshold when matched to a static black-hole exterior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how higher-dimensional Chern–Simons gravity modifies classical gravitational collapse dynamics—particularly singularity formation, horizon emergence, and matching to vacuum solutions—beyond general relativity, leveraging the special geometric structure of AdS Chern–Simons theory in five dimensions.

Method: Uses comoving Lemaître–Tolman–Bondi (LTB) coordinates in the torsion-free sector of five-dimensional AdS Chern–Simons gravity; solves reduced field equations exactly for an inhomogeneous dust interior; analyzes regularity (central conditions, shell crossing), curvature (Kretschmann scalar), energy conditions, and junction conditions to a static black-hole exterior; computes null expansions to locate apparent horizons.

Result: Obtains an exact collapsing interior solution where areal radius evolves periodically but physical collapse terminates at shell focusing or loss of regularity; shows shell focusing is a genuine curvature singularity satisfying weak/strong energy conditions; derives a critical quasilocal mass threshold for finite-radius apparent horizon formation; confirms smooth matching to the black-hole branch of the static AdS Chern–Simons exterior; recovers 5D FLRW in the homogeneous limit.

Conclusion: Five-dimensional AdS Chern–Simons gravity supports physically consistent dust collapse with curvature singularities and horizon formation governed by quasilocal mass, preserving standard energy conditions and regularity constraints; the theory admits a well-defined black-hole exterior matching, indicating robustness of black-hole formation mechanisms even in this exotic higher-dimensional gauge-gravity framework.

Abstract: We study the gravitational collapse of a spherically symmetric, inhomogeneous dust cloud in five-dimensional AdS Chern--Simons gravity. Working in the torsion-free sector and using comoving Lemaître--Tolman--Bondi coordinates, we derive the reduced field equations and obtain an exact collapsing interior solution. The areal radius evolves periodically in time, but the physical collapsing branch extends only up to shell focusing or an earlier loss of regularity, while the initial density determines a radial function whose square is proportional to the conserved quasilocal mass. This separates the roles of the initial velocity and density profiles in controlling shell focusing and trapping.
  We derive conditions for central regularity and for avoiding shell crossing, analyze the Kretschmann scalar, and show that shell focusing corresponds to a genuine curvature singularity, the weak and strong energy conditions are preserved throughout the regular collapsing phase. The homogeneous limit reduces consistently to a five-dimensional FLRW interior.
  The collapsing cloud is matched smoothly to the black-hole branch of the static AdS Chern--Simons exterior. The null expansions show that a finite-radius apparent horizon forms only when the quasilocal mass exceeds a critical threshold. Initially untrapped shells above this threshold become trapped before shell focusing, whereas below the total-mass threshold no finite-radius future-trapped symmetry sphere forms in the regular interior.

</details>


### [117] [Stability Analysis of Canonical Acoustic Black Hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32277)
*HengZhong Fang,LiQiang Miao,ChangSen Liu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the stability of a canonical acoustic black hole under scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac field perturbations using third-order WKB to compute quasi-normal modes (QNMs); all cases yield positive effective potentials and negative QNM imaginary parts, confirming stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the stability of canonical acoustic black holes under non-minimally coupled scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac field perturbations—key for assessing their physical viability as analog systems.

Method: Third-order WKB approximation to compute quasi-normal modes (QNMs); analysis of effective potential behavior for each field type under varying quantum numbers and coupling parameters.

Result: All three perturbations yield positive effective potentials and negative imaginary parts of QNMs—indicating stable configurations; decay rate increases with scalar coupling ζ; angular dependence differs per field: l,n,ζ for scalar; l,n for EM; n,|k| for Dirac.

Conclusion: The canonical acoustic black hole is stable against non-minimally coupled scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac field perturbations, as confirmed by positive effective potentials and damped (negative-Im) QNMs.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate the stability of canonical acoustic black hole under the perturbation of non-minimally coupling scalar field, electromagnetic field and Dirac field. The quasi-normal modes(QNMs) are computed using the three-order WKB approximation method. The results show that, for all these three kinds of perturbation, the values of the effective potential are positive, while the imaginary parts of QNMs are negative, the canonical acoustic black hole is a table. In details respectively, for scalar field perturbation, the effective potential and the QNMs depend on the angular quantum number $l$, the overtone number $n$, and the coupling parameter ζ, the perturbation decays more rapidly for bigger ζ. For electromagnetic field perturbation, the effective potential and QNMs depend on $l$ and $n$. For Dirac field perturbation, the effective potential and QNMs depend on $n$ and the total angular quantum number | k |.

</details>


### [118] [Memory effect from neutron star merger counterparts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32412)
*T. Brabant,M. Pillas,H. Inchauspé,Z. Lin,F. Foucart,M. Bulla*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper quantifies nonlinear and linear gravitational-wave memory contributions from binary neutron star (BNS) mergers—including GWs, GRBs, afterglows, neutrinos, ejecta, and kilonovae—and assesses their detectability with Einstein Telescope (ET) and LISA; it finds ET could have detected the combined memory from GW170817 at SNR=10.7 (dominated by nonlinear memory), while linear memory from counterparts remains largely undetectable except in extreme cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable the first observational detection of the GW memory effect—particularly distinguishing between nonlinear (self-interaction of GWs) and linear (anisotropic matter/radiation emission) components—in multi-messenger BNS mergers using next-generation detectors (ET and LISA).

Method: The authors compute both nonlinear and linear memory waveforms from BNS mergers, incorporating contributions from GWs, gamma-ray bursts (GRBs), afterglows, neutrinos, dynamical and disk-wind ejecta, and kilonova emission; they then simulate signal-to-noise ratios (SNRs) for detection with ET and LISA, applying the framework to GW170817 and a synthetic GRB population.

Result: For GW170817, the total memory SNR in ET is 10.7, dominated by nonlinear memory; linear memory contributions (e.g., from GRB 170817A or kilonova) are subdominant and individually undetectable; across a synthetic GRB population, only extreme-energy, optimally oriented GRBs yield detectable linear memory in ET.

Conclusion: Next-generation ground-based detectors like ET will likely detect nonlinear GW memory from BNS mergers, but linear memory from electromagnetic and neutrino counterparts remains observationally inaccessible except under highly favorable, rare astrophysical conditions.

Abstract: The gravitational-wave (GW) memory effect is a prediction of general relativity, characterized by a permanent change in spacetime geometry. Although it has not yet been observed, next-generation detectors such as the Einstein Telescope (ET) and the Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA) are expected to provide the sensitivity required for its first detection. GW memory comprises two distinct contributions: the nonlinear memory, generated by the self-interaction of GWs, and the linear memory, originating from the anisotropic emission of matter and radiation associated with astrophysical transients. In this work, we quantify both nonlinear and linear memory contributions produced during binary neutron star mergers (BNSs), including those associated with GWs, gamma-ray bursts (GRBs), afterglows, neutrinos, dynamical and disk-wind ejecta, and kilonova emission. We evaluate the detectability of the resulting memory signals with ET and LISA through two applications. First, we apply our analysis to the multi-messenger event GW170817 - GRB 170817A - AT2017gfo. We find that the combined memory signal from this event would have been detectable by ET with a signal-to-noise ratio of 10.7 dominated by the nonlinear memory. We also assess the detectability of the GRB memory across a synthetic population of GRBs, and find that only extreme-energy and jointly favorable configurations could produce a detectable signal in ET. Together, these results demonstrate that while next-generation detectors will probe nonlinear memory, observing linear memory from BNS counterparts will remain elusive except in the most favorable scenarios.

</details>


### [119] [Energy extraction from Kerr--Bertotti--Robinson black holes: polar region collapse and the capture of negative energy protons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32494)
*Ke Wang,Xiao-Xiong Zeng*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes particle acceleration and energy extraction near a Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson black hole — an exact Einstein-Maxwell solution with rotation and asymptotically uniform magnetic field — revealing region-dependent charge separation, negative/positive energy capture depending on particle species, and magnetic-field-dependent net energy gain or extraction without requiring anisotropic plasma.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how charged particle dynamics and energy exchange differ in the Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (KBR) spacetime compared to the Wald solution for Kerr black holes, particularly regarding magnetic field geometry, particle acceleration, and conditions for energy extraction.

Method: Pointwise calculation of charged particle acceleration using electromagnetic invariants; identification of particle capture domains via the gauge-invariant flux criterion; integration over the (r, θ) plane to compute net energy and angular momentum fluxes; comparison of magnetic field line structure and E·B=0 surface geometry between KBR and Wald solutions.

Result: Protons and electrons experience opposite radial accelerations in polar vs. equatorial regions; polar acceleration region shrinks with increasing B; captured protons carry negative energy/angular momentum, electrons positive; weak B leads to black hole mass increase, strong B enables energy extraction; extraction occurs even under spherically symmetric plasma density — unlike Wald’s Kerr case requiring anisotropy.

Conclusion: The Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson black hole supports efficient energy extraction in strong magnetic fields without artificial anisotropic plasma assumptions, enabled by its distinct electromagnetic geometry and intrinsic charge-separation dynamics.

Abstract: The Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson black hole is an exact Einstein-Maxwell solution describing a rotating black hole in an asymptotically uniform magnetic field. It differs from the Kerr black hole in the Wald solution: the magnetic field lines are no longer cylindrical, with the constant-\(A_φ\) lines saturating at large distances, and the interface \(\vec{E}\cdot\vec{B}=0\) is no longer a cone but contracts with radius and eventually disappears. Determining the acceleration of charged particles pointwise, we find that protons are driven outward and electrons inward in the polar region, and vice versa in the equatorial region; the polar region shrinks as the magnetic field grows. Captured protons carry negative energy and angular momentum, whereas captured electrons carry positive energy and angular momentum. Fixing the capture domain by the gauge-invariant flux criterion and integrating over the \((r,θ)\) plane, we find that in weak magnetic fields the black hole gains mass, while in strong magnetic fields energy extraction occurs. Energy extraction can be achieved under the usual spherically symmetric density, without introducing the anisotropic density distribution required in the Wald solution for a Kerr black hole.

</details>


### [120] [Exact Solutions for Chiral Gravitational Waves from Spin-2 Mixing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32571)
*Mohammad Ali Gorji,Yuhang Zhu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper solves exactly the coupled system of inflationary tensor perturbations and a spectator spin-2 field with parity violation, yielding closed-form power spectra via hypergeometric functions; it shows exponential chiral enhancement of one graviton helicity, with circular polarisation bounded by tanh(πθ), independent of mass or mixing strength.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To nonperturbatively study how a spectator spin-2 field linearly mixed with metric perturbations—and violating parity—can source primordial gravitational waves during inflation, beyond standard perturbative treatments.

Method: Exact analytical solution of the coupled tensor mode equations in de Sitter space without expanding in the mixing parameter; construction of helicity-mode functions using operator-valued Gauss hypergeometric functions acting on Whittaker modes; derivation of late-time power spectra in terms of generalised hypergeometric _3F_2.

Result: Closed-form expressions for the late-time power spectra of both graviton helicities and the spectator field, plus their cross-spectrum; exponential enhancement of one helicity; circular polarisation bounded by tanh(πθ); recovery of Schwinger-Keldysh perturbative results in weak-mixing limit.

Conclusion: Strong linear mixing between the graviton and a massive spectator spin-2 field—especially with parity violation—can generate highly chiral, observationally significant primordial gravitational waves; the exact nonperturbative framework enables robust predictions beyond standard slow-roll or weak-coupling approximations.

Abstract: A spectator spin-2 field can mix linearly with the metric tensor perturbations. We study the coupled tensor system in an inflationary background, including a parity violating effect parametrised by $θ$. Without expanding in the linear mixing between the two fields, we solve the system exactly for arbitrary mass while treating the mixing strength nonperturbatively. The mode functions of both helicities are constructed by acting with an operator-valued Gauss hypergeometric function ${}_2F_1$ on Whittaker modes, and the late-time power spectrum of each graviton helicity is expressed in closed form in terms of the generalised hypergeometric function ${}_3F_2$. We find that one helicity is exponentially enhanced, showing that strong mixing with a spectator spin-2 field can generate a large and highly chiral primordial gravitational wave spectrum. The degree of circular polarisation is bounded by $\tanh(πθ)$, independently of the spin-2 mass and the mixing strength. The same solution also gives the late-time power spectrum of the spectator field and its cross spectrum with the graviton. In the weak mixing limit, our exact result reproduces the perturbative Schwinger-Keldysh result. These solutions provide a nonperturbative framework for studying primordial gravitational waves sourced by additional tensor modes.

</details>


### [121] [Thermal equilibrium in general relativity and the special nature of the cosmological constant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32608)
*M. L. Rodrigues,J. B. Formiga*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores thermal equilibrium in general relativity, highlighting the unique thermodynamic properties of the cosmological-constant fluid — namely, its universal thermal equilibrium and intrinsic energy E_Λ = ρ_Λ V — and applies teleparallel gravity to compute quasi-local energies for de Sitter-influenced black holes, addressing zero-point energy ambiguities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conceptual issues surrounding gravitational energy, thermal equilibrium in curved spacetime, and the physical interpretation of the cosmological constant — especially in light of quantum gravity constraints like minimal length scales and the zero-point energy problem.

Method: Uses the teleparallel equivalent of general relativity (TEGR) to define and compute quasi-local gravitational energy; analyzes thermodynamic equilibrium conditions for perfect fluids in GR; derives intrinsic energy expression for Λ-fluid; applies formalism to Reissner-Nordström-de Sitter and regular black hole spacetimes.

Result: Demonstrates that the Λ-fluid is uniquely always in thermal equilibrium; establishes E_Λ = ρ_Λ V as a universal, volume-dependent intrinsic energy; shows TEGR yields consistent quasi-local energies for Λ-influenced black holes; clarifies how TEGR avoids certain zero-point energy ambiguities inherent in GR-based energy definitions.

Conclusion: The cosmological constant fluid possesses exceptional thermodynamic and energetic properties — universality of thermal equilibrium and intrinsic volumetric energy — which are naturally explained in TEGR; this framework offers a physically coherent route to gravitational energy localization and may inform quantum gravity models incorporating minimal length scales.

Abstract: The problem of thermal equilibrium in general relativity is discussed and the unique role played by the fluid associated with the cosmological constant is emphasized. In addition to being the only fluid that is always in thermal equilibrium, it is shown here that its energy can also be seen as a universal quantity given by $E_Λ=ρ_ΛV$, where $V$ is a spatial volume. Furthermore, if the cosmological constant turns out to be a universal constant, then the conclusion that any spatial volume $V$ has an intrinsic energy follows naturally from the approach considered here. A possible application to quantum theories of gravity that postulate the existence of a minimal length scale is discussed. In studying the energies, the so-called teleparallel equivalent of general relativity is used, and the difficulty with the zero-point energy is discussed in light of the gravitational energy problem. The quasi-local energies of the Reissner-Nordström-de Sitter black hole and of a regular black hole are calculated. The results obtained here with the teleparallel theory are consistent with our expectations.

</details>


### [122] [Two-horizon sector thermodynamics and NUT bi-hair in Kerr-Newman-NUT spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33032)
*Di Wu,Robert B. Mann,Shuang-Qing Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes thermodynamic consistency in Kerr-Newman-NUT black holes using a two-horizon (Σ/Δ) sector decomposition, showing that NUT charge N behaves as a charge-like variable while its secondary hair J_N = MN acts as rotation-like—distinct and irreducible within minimal homogeneous state spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguity in black hole state space construction for NUT-charged spacetimes, where first-law consistency alone is insufficient to uniquely fix thermodynamic variables due to multiple inequivalent macroscopic formulations.

Method: Develops a two-horizon diagnostic by decomposing inner- and outer-horizon entropies and inverse temperatures into sum (Σ) and difference (Δ) sectors; constructs and tests minimal homogeneous state spaces for the charged rotating Kerr-Newman-NUT family; examines closure of first law and Bekenstein–Smarr relations under variable reductions.

Result: Clear sector separation emerges: Σ sector closes with Q and N only; Δ sector requires J and J_N = MN; eliminating either N or J_N breaks first-law coefficient matching and scaling-weight closure; J_N is identified as an off-shell, thermodynamically defined secondary hair—not a metric parameter or conserved charge.

Conclusion: NUT charge N and its secondary hair J_N = MN play distinct, non-interchangeable roles: N is charge-like, J_N is rotation-like; the charged rotating family supports a 'bi-hair' structure for NUT, invalidating simplifications that conflate them with Q or J.

Abstract: First law consistency alone does not determine the black hole state space of NUT-charged spacetimes, because several inequivalent macroscopic formulations can yield consistent thermodynamics. We develop a two-horizon sector diagnostic for the electrically charged Kerr-Newman-NUT family by organizing the inner- and outer-horizon entropies and inverse temperatures into sum ($Σ$) and difference ($Δ$) sectors. The charged rotating geometry gives a more stringent test of the uncharged Taub-NUT sector result, since the mass $M$, ordinary electric charge $Q$, ordinary angular momentum $J$, NUT charge $N$, and the NUT secondary hair $J_N=MN$ all enter the same two-horizon thermodynamics. For the physically anchored minimal homogeneous state spaces defined and tested here, we find a clear sector separation: the $Σ$ sector closes with the charge-like variables $Q$ and $N$, whereas the $Δ$ sector additionally requires the rotation-like variables $J$ and $J_N$. Natural reductions that eliminate either $J_N$ or $N$ fail to reproduce the first law coefficient fixed by the sector temperature, and their associated Bekenstein-Smarr relations do not close with the assigned scaling weights. Within this class, electric charge and ordinary rotation therefore do not absorb the NUT secondary response, and the charged rotating family supports the NUT bi-hair organization: $N$ has a charge-like sector role, while $J_N=MN$ is a rotation-like thermodynamic secondary hair. The latter is defined off shell in the homogeneous equation of state, not as a new metric parameter or asymptotic conserved charge.

</details>


### [123] [A construction of charged naked singularities for the Einstein--Maxwell-charged scalar field equations in 3+1 dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33168)
*Weihao Zheng*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Constructs global naked singularity solutions for the 3+1D spherically symmetric Einstein–Maxwell–charged scalar field system, from mildly regular (C^{1,α}) asymptotically flat initial data; proves the singularity is globally visible (not hidden by a horizon), genuinely charged, and develops a well-characterized exterior region.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously construct and characterize physically plausible, globally naked singularities in a coupled gravitational–electromagnetic–scalar field system—addressing the cosmic censorship conjecture in a concrete, charged setting.

Method: Nonlinear stability-type analysis of characteristic initial value problem; construction of solutions from C^{1,α} asymptotically flat one-ended data; development of a general geometric framework for building naked singularity exteriors as the causal future of the past light cone from the singularity; careful control of coupled Einstein–Maxwell–scalar field dynamics near the singularity, including singular Maxwell field behavior.

Result: Existence of a class of global, spherically symmetric, asymptotically flat spacetimes solving the Einstein–Maxwell–charged scalar field equations, featuring a curvature singularity that is causally visible (no event horizon), carries nonzero charge with quantitative lower bound on |F| up to the singularity, and admits an explicit asymptotic profile for the Maxwell field; plus a general framework for constructing such naked singularity exteriors.

Conclusion: Naked singularities can arise robustly—even under mild regularity assumptions (C^{1,α})—in gravitationally coupled charged matter systems; cosmic censorship violation is realizable in this model, and the exterior geometry near such singularities is systematically tractable via the introduced light-cone-based framework.

Abstract: We construct a class of global naked singularity solutions for the $(3+1)$-dimensional spherically symmetric Einstein--Maxwell-charged scalar field equations. The solutions arise from $C^{1,α}$ asymptotically flat one-ended characteristic initial data with $0<α\ll1$ and develop a curvature singularity that is not enclosed by an event horizon. The resulting spacetimes are genuinely charged, with a nonzero Maxwell field that satisfies a quantitative positive lower bound up to the singularity and admits an explicit asymptotic profile. As part of the construction, we develop a general framework for constructing naked singularity exteriors, namely, the causal future region of the past light cone emanating from the singularity. The framework accommodates a broad range of singular characteristic data and, in particular, allows for singular behavior of the Maxwell field near the singularity.

</details>


### [124] [Quasinormal modes of Hayward black holes from Schwarzschild to extremality: a comparative spectral analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33267)
*Davide Batic,Denys Dutykh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates quasinormal modes (QNMs) of the Hayward black hole for scalar, electromagnetic, and axial gravitational perturbations across the Schwarzschild-to-extremal range, using high-precision Chebyshev collocation; it reproduces known low-lying modes, identifies new overtone candidates, highlights model dependence in the spin-2 equation, distinguishes physical QNMs from unclassified purely imaginary eigenvalues, and cautions against overinterpreting spacing patterns (e.g., near $M\omega = 1/4$ or extremal product $\sim 1/3$) as universal features.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously compute and classify quasinormal modes of the regular Hayward black hole—particularly probing the transition from Schwarzschild to extremal limits—and to clarify ambiguities in spectral interpretation arising from numerical discretization, model-dependent potentials, and misattribution of spurious imaginary-axis eigenvalues as physical QNMs.

Method: Multiprecision Chebyshev collocation applied to scalar, electromagnetic, and axial Regge–Wheeler-type wave equations; comparison of two spin-2 effective potentials (including a newly derived one differing from prior literature); careful spectral classification distinguishing true QNMs (poles of the continued Green function) from numerically robust but unphysical purely imaginary eigenvalues; analysis of gap dispersion and convergence with respect to number of included gaps.

Result: Accurate reproduction of known low-lying QNMs; identification of new overtone candidates; quantified frequency differences (up to ~1% in real part, larger in imaginary part) between competing spin-2 potentials; discovery of highly reproducible purely imaginary eigenvalue ladders—classified as unconfirmed spectral candidates, not QNMs; observed $M\omega \approx 0.25$ spacing and extremal $M\omega \cdot r_{\text{throat}} \approx 1/3$ pattern among those candidates—but no evidence these are universal or physically significant.

Conclusion: Quasinormal spectra of the Hayward black hole are sensitive to the choice of gravitational perturbation potential; purely imaginary eigenvalues from discretization do not automatically correspond to physical QNMs and require analytic continuation validation; reported spacing patterns are numerical artifacts of the candidate set and do not imply universal asymptotic behavior or throat-controlled damping.

Abstract: We study scalar and electromagnetic quasinormal modes of the Hayward black hole and the axial spectrum of a specified Regge--Wheeler-type effective model, from the Schwarzschild limit to exact extremality. Multiprecision Chebyshev collocation reproduces most available low-lying oscillatory benchmarks and supplies further overtone candidates. The spin-2 equation is model dependent. It differs from the effective-source potential used in recent Hayward studies, and we quantify representative frequency differences. The discretisation also produces purely imaginary eigenvalue sequences, whose physical classification requires special care because highly reproducible imaginary-axis ladders need not be poles of the continued Green function. Accordingly, the imaginary-axis entries reported here are retained as unclassified spectral candidates, not established QNMs. We quantify their finite-table gap dispersion and sensitivity to the number of included gaps. Their spacings near one quarter in $Mω$, and the extremal product near $1/3$ with the throat radius, remain properties of the tabulated candidates, and they do not establish universal QNM spacing or throat-controlled damping.

</details>


### [125] [Dimensional (In)dependence of Lorentzian Quantum Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33452)
*Masazumi Honda,Hiroki Matsui,Kota Numajiri,Kazumasa Okabayashi,Takahiro Terada*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper computes quantum transition amplitudes for the universe's creation from nothing in D-dimensional de Sitter minisuperspace using Lorentzian path integrals and Picard-Lefschetz theory; it resolves Stokes ambiguities via Borel-Padé resurgence and finds the tunneling proposal universally realized across dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a well-defined, physically meaningful quantum transition amplitude for the universe's creation from nothing in arbitrary spacetime dimensions within Einstein gravity, overcoming ambiguities inherent in Euclidean or naive Lorentzian path integrals.

Method: Lorentzian path integral quantization within the de Sitter minisuperspace approximation; saddle-point analysis using Picard-Lefschetz theory to identify dominant complex contours; numerical resurgence analysis via Borel-Padé resummation to resolve residual Stokes ambiguities.

Result: Well-defined Dirichlet-boundary-condition transition amplitudes are obtained; tunneling proposal dominates for universe creation from nothing in D = 3, 4, 5 and in the large-D limit; universality of tunneling behavior across dimensions is demonstrated.

Conclusion: The tunneling proposal for quantum cosmological creation from nothing is a robust, dimension-independent feature of Einstein gravity under the de Sitter minisuperspace approximation and Lorentzian path integral formulation with proper contour selection and ambiguity resolution.

Abstract: We study transition amplitudes of the quantum universe in $D$-dimensional spacetime within the de Sitter minisuperspace approximation in Einstein gravity. Using the Lorentzian path integral combined with Picard-Lefschetz theory, we identify the saddle points that contribute to the integral and derive a well-defined transition amplitude under Dirichlet boundary conditions. We further show numerically that a resurgence analysis based on Borel-Padé resummation is effective in resolving the residual Stokes ambiguities that arise in certain cases. Analyzing three-, four-, and five-dimensional cases, as well as the large-$D$ limit, we find that the tunneling proposal is commonly realized for the creation of the universe from nothing. This result implies a universal feature of quantum cosmology based on Einstein gravity across dimensions.

</details>


### [126] [Polar-metric/axial-gauge perturbations of the Maldacena-Milekhin-Popov wormhole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33511)
*Abhishake Sadhukhan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes linear perturbations of the Maldacena-Milekhin-Popov (MMP) traversable wormhole on a magnetically charged background, revealing coupling between polar metric and axial gauge field perturbations; it derives two coupled Zerilli-Moncrief–type master equations, shows consistency requires inclusion of the full 2D conformal anomaly (including trace part) and an induced Hall current from fermionic backreaction, identifies AdS₂×S² throat geometry at α=0 with Poschl-Teller spectra, computes O(α) corrections that mix modes and lower frequencies, and proves absence of growing modes for l ≥ 2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess classical stability of the MMP traversable wormhole under linear perturbations, particularly addressing inconsistencies in prior treatments of fermionic backreaction in the throat — specifically, the omission of the trace part of the 2D conformal anomaly and the associated Hall current — and to determine whether unstable (growing) modes exist that could invalidate traversability.

Method: Reduction of linearized Einstein-Maxwell-fermion system to two coupled master equations of Zerilli-Moncrief type using gauge-invariant variables; exact computation of fermionic stress-tensor response via 2D conformal anomaly (including trace); derivation of induced Hall current from effective action requiring an Euler-term coupling (c/48π) analogous to Wen-Zee term; spectral analysis of throat equations in AdS₂×S² limit (α=0) as Poschl-Teller problems; closed-form perturbative solution at O(α) enforcing matching across mouth-throat overlap and Bianchi identity preservation.

Result: Throat dynamics are consistent only when both full conformal anomaly (trace included) and Hall current are incorporated; O(α) corrections mix the two decoupled Poschl-Teller modes, lower normal mode frequencies, induce real level splitting, and yield a symmetric potential that is everywhere positive — excluding exponential growth; no growing modes exist for l ≥ 2 at first order in fermionic backreaction α.

Conclusion: The MMP wormhole’s polar–axial sector is linearly stable against perturbations for l ≥ 2 at O(α), provided the fermionic backreaction includes both the complete 2D conformal anomaly and the topologically required Hall current — establishing a refined consistency condition beyond the original MMP construction.

Abstract: We study linear perturbations of the Maldacena-Milekhin-Popov (MMP) traversable wormhole. On a magnetically charged background, polar metric perturbations couple to axial perturbations of the gauge field. We treat this sector and reduce it to two coupled master equations of Zerilli-Moncrief type. The wormhole has two regions. In the mouths the geometry is a nearly extremal magnetic Reissner-Nordstrom black hole with no quantum source. In the throat the Casimir energy of the lowest Landau level of charged fermions holds the wormhole open. We show that the throat equations are consistent only if the fermions respond to the perturbation, computed exactly from the two-dimensional conformal anomaly with the full stress tensor including the trace part that MMP discard. This response must be accompanied by an induced Hall current of the fermions. With both effects included, the linearised Bianchi identities hold at first order in the fermionic backreaction $α$. Deriving the Hall current from the fermion effective action instead requires an Euler term with coefficient $c/48π$, which becomes a coupling between magnetic flux and curvature similar to a Wen-Zee term. At $α=0$ the throat is exact AdS$_2\times S^2$ and the modes decouple into two Poschl-Teller problems with masses $l(l-1)$ and $(l+1)(l+2)$, with levels spaced by $1/\ell$ in frequency. We give the $O(α)$ corrections in closed form. They mix the two modes and depend on frequency, and match the mouths in the overlap region. The symmetric part of the potential is positive throughout the wormhole, excluding purely exponential growth. The corrections lower the normal frequencies and split the levels shared by the two modes, with real shifts at first order in $α$. This sector has no growing mode for $l\ge2$ at this order.

</details>


### [127] [Stochastic gravitational-wave background from self-interacting superradiant clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33596)
*Yin-Da Guo,Richard Brito,Chen Yuan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the stochastic gravitational-wave background (SGWB) from black hole–boson cloud systems with ultralight self-interacting scalar fields, showing that self-interactions suppress the SGWB—relaxing current LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA constraints—and quantifying detectability prospects for next-generation detectors (Einstein Telescope and Cosmic Explorer).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve predictions for the stochastic GW background from BH–boson clouds by incorporating scalar self-interactions—a physically relevant effect previously neglected—and assess implications for current and future GW detector sensitivity to ultralight scalar fields.

Method: Analytical and numerical modeling of superradiant boson cloud evolution including quartic self-interactions; computation of the ensemble-averaged SGWB spectrum from cosmological populations of BH–cloud systems; comparison with noise curves and sensitivity projections of LIGO, Einstein Telescope (ET), and Cosmic Explorer (CE).

Result: Self-interactions suppress the SGWB amplitude, rendering LIGO at design sensitivity insensitive to scalars with decay constant fs ≲ 3×10¹⁷ GeV regardless of mass; ET and CE can detect moderately self-interacting clouds: ET probes ms ∈ [10⁻¹³.⁰, 10⁻¹¹.⁸] eV for fs = 10¹⁷ GeV (minimum detectable fs ∼ 6×10¹⁶ GeV); CE probes ms ∈ [10⁻¹³.², 10⁻¹¹.⁷] eV (minimum fs ∼ 3×10¹⁶ GeV).

Conclusion: Scalar self-interactions significantly weaken existing GW bounds on ultralight bosons and reshape detection forecasts; next-generation detectors remain sensitive to such fields even with moderate self-coupling, enabling robust probes of axion-like particles in unexplored parameter space.

Abstract: Gravitational-wave (GW) observations offer a powerful probe of new fundamental fields. One well-motivated source is black hole (BH)--boson cloud systems, in which an ultralight scalar field forms a cloud around a rotating BH via superradiance and emits long-lived nearly monochromatic GWs. In this work, we compute the stochastic GW background (SGWB) from such systems, extending previous work by including scalar self-interactions, and discuss its detectability with next-generation ground-based GW detectors. We find that self-interactions can suppress the SGWB and thereby relax existing LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA constraints inferred from null searches. Namely, the LIGO detectors at design sensitivity are insensitive to a SGWB produced by scalar fields with a decay constant $f_\mathrm{s} \lesssim 3\times 10^{17}$ GeV, independently of the scalar field mass. Looking ahead, we show that a moderately self-interacting cloud can still produce a detectable SGWB with next-generation detectors. Under conservative assumptions, for a decay constant $f_\mathrm{s} = 10^{17}$ GeV, the Einstein Telescope (ET) will be sensitive to scalar masses in the range $\sim[10^{-13.0},10^{-11.8}]$ eV, while Cosmic Explorer (CE) will be sensitive to scalar masses in the range $\sim[10^{-13.2},10^{-11.7}]$ eV. We also find that the minimum decay constants that still yield a detectable SGWB for ET and CE are $f_\mathrm{s}\sim 6\times10^{16}\,$GeV and $\sim 3\times10^{16}\,$GeV, respectively.

</details>


### [128] [Exact solutions of Maxwell vacuum equations in null homogeneous Petrov spaces with non-solvable motions groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33613)
*V. V. Obukhov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper classifies exact vacuum solutions of Maxwell's equations in null homogeneous Petrov spacetimes admitting simply transitive unsolvable $G_3$ isometry groups acting on null hypersurfaces, using canonical frame-based metric and field component representations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify all exact electromagnetic vacuum solutions compatible with the geometric symmetry constraints imposed by null homogeneous Petrov spacetimes possessing unsolvable $G_3$ isometry groups acting simply transitively on null hypersurfaces.

Method: Exploits the canonical frame associated with the $G_3$ group action to express both the contravariant metric tensor components and the invariant electromagnetic field components as functions of the frame vector fields; applies Lie group classification techniques to solve Maxwell's vacuum equations under these symmetry reductions.

Result: A complete, explicit classification of all such exact invariant vacuum electromagnetic field solutions in the specified spacetime class, tabulated in a form optimized for physical interpretation and further applications.

Conclusion: The symmetry-adapted canonical frame method enables full classification; the resulting solutions exhaust all possibilities consistent with the given null homogeneous Petrov geometry and unsolvable $G_3$ invariance.

Abstract: A complete classification of exact solutions of Maxwell vacuum equations for invariant electromagnetic fields in null homogeneous Petrov spaces with unsolvable groups of motions $G_3$, acting simply transitively on null hypersurfaces of null homogeneous Petrov spaces, is conducted. The classification of exact solutions of Maxwell vacuum equations is based on using the representation of components of the contravariant metric tensor and components of the invariant electromagnetic field in terms of vector fields of a canonical frame. The classification results are presented in a form convenient for further use.

</details>


### [129] [Perturbations of Kerr-Newman black holes: separation and mode stability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33661)
*Peter Hintz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves mode stability for linearized perturbations of subextremal Kerr-Newman black holes by decoupling spin-1 and spin-2 field equations via a matrix integrating factor and applying a Whiting-type transform to radial ODEs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing problem of coupled electromagnetic (spin-1) and gravitational (spin-2) perturbations in the Kerr-Newman spacetime, originally described by Chandrasekhar as 'apparently indissoluble'.

Method: Uses a matrix integrating factor to decouple first-order systems representing radiative degrees of freedom; performs angular separation to obtain radial ODEs; applies a Whiting-type transformation to prove absence of real-frequency quasinormal modes.

Result: Establishes mode stability on the real axis for linearized Einstein-Maxwell perturbations of subextremal Kerr-Newman black holes — i.e., no exponentially growing or stationary non-decaying modes with real frequency.

Conclusion: The coupling between spin-1 and spin-2 perturbations is surmountable via appropriate transformation techniques, enabling rigorous mode stability analysis and supporting the linear stability of subextremal Kerr-Newman black holes under Einstein-Maxwell dynamics.

Abstract: We study linearized perturbations of the subextremal Kerr-Newman black hole solution of the Einstein-Maxwell equations. We show how to overcome what Chandrasekhar called the "apparent indissolubility of the coupling between the spin-1 and spin-2 fields" by using a matrix integrating factor for suitably transformed first-order systems that describe the radiative degrees of freedom. For the radial ODEs obtained after angular separation, we prove mode stability on the real axis, i.e., the absence of quasinormal modes with real frequencies, using a Whiting-type transform.

</details>


### [130] [Relic Neutrinos Probing Small Scale Primordial Non-Gaussianity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34324)
*Suraj Gavhale,Shin'ichi Nojiri,Sergei D. Odintsov,Oem Trivedi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives a cubic photon number source from acoustic damping and constructs a unified response kernel incorporating neutrino decoupling, thermalization, and acoustic coherence to link primordial folded non-Gaussianity to relic neutrino capture rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe primordial modes erased from the CMB by using relic neutrinos as carriers of electromagnetic entropy information post-neutrino decoupling.

Method: Derivation of the cubic photon number source from acoustic damping; construction of a single response kernel integrating neutrino decoupling, thermalization, and acoustic coherence; analysis of folded bispectrum sensitivity and transmission conditions for excited initial states.

Result: A unified response kernel that filters the physical folded profile for excited initial states and yields a sharpness-dependent transmission condition, enabling direct mapping from small-scale primordial non-Gaussianity to observable relic neutrino capture rates.

Conclusion: Relic neutrinos serve as a novel probe of primordial folded non-Gaussianity, with capture rates quantitatively tied to the shape and sharpness of initial-state correlations via the derived kernel.

Abstract: Relic neutrinos retain a record of electromagnetic entropy production after neutrino decoupling, probing primordial modes erased from the primary CMB. We derive the cubic photon number source from acoustic damping and its leading folded sensitive bispectrum window, with neutrino decoupling, thermalization, and acoustic coherence combined in a single response kernel. For excited initial states, this kernel filters the physical folded profile and yields a sharpness dependent transmission condition, directly connecting small scale primordial non-Gaussianity to relic neutrino capture rates.

</details>


### [131] [Avoided crossings and resonances in black hole ringdown with coupled fields: A case study of the Einstein-Maxwell-axion system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34401)
*Takuya Takahashi,Hayato Motohashi,Kazufumi Takahashi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies avoided crossings in quasinormal-mode (QNM) spectra of charged black holes in the Einstein-Maxwell-axion system, revealing resonant enhancements, branch character exchange, and effective slowing of ringdown decay due to interference between gravity- and EM-led modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how avoided crossings—resonant features in QNM spectra—affect black hole ringdown waveforms in multi-field perturbation systems, where coupling breaks mode degeneracy and enables novel interference effects.

Method: Analytical and numerical study of QNMs in the Einstein-Maxwell-axion black hole background; classification of QNM branches by asymptotic decoupling limits; time-domain waveform evolution; QNM reconstruction and decomposition; analysis of excitation factors, decay rates, and mode interference near an avoided crossing between gravity- and EM-led branches.

Result: Near the avoided crossing: (i) QNM reconstruction accurately matches time-domain ringdown; (ii) certain initial perturbations yield effectively slower decay; (iii) resonantly enhanced contributions from the two nearby QNMs cancel in the physical waveform due to destructive interference; (iv) the gravitational and electromagnetic 'characters' of the two branches exchange across the resonance.

Conclusion: Avoided crossings in multi-field black hole perturbations leave distinct imprints on ringdown—namely, interference-driven waveform suppression, emergent slow decay, and branch character swapping—highlighting the necessity of coupled-mode treatment for accurate QNM modeling and gravitational-wave interpretation.

Abstract: Avoided crossings in black hole quasinormal-mode (QNM) spectra exhibit characteristic resonant properties, including enhanced excitation factors. We extend the study of this phenomenon to perturbation systems with multiple coupled fields and investigate its consequences for ringdown waveforms. As a concrete example, we consider a charged black hole in the Einstein-Maxwell-axion system, whose QNM branches can be classified according to the gravitational, electromagnetic, and axion modes to which they continuously connect in the decoupling limit. We focus on an avoided crossing between the gravity- and electromagnetic-led branches thus defined. We verify that the QNM reconstruction accurately reproduces the ringdown portion of the directly evolved time-domain waveform. Near the avoided crossing, an effectively slower decay emerges for particular initial perturbations, while the resonantly enhanced contributions from the two QNMs largely cancel in the physical waveform. The QNM decomposition further reveals an exchange of the physical characters of the two branches across the resonance. These results characterize the imprint of resonances on black hole ringdown in systems with multiple coupled fields.

</details>


### [132] [Charged Simpson-Visser-AdS spacetime: QPOs and observational constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34443)
*Saeed Ullah Khan,Javlon Rayimbaev,Muhammad Zahid,Tolaniddin Nurmukhamedov,Abat Muratov,Weiwei Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies particle motion and quasi-periodic oscillations (QPOs) in a charged Simpson-Visser-AdS black hole/wormhole spacetime, revealing how electric charge Q, regularization parameter b, and AdS radius l affect horizon structure, ISCO, orbital frequencies, and twin-peaked QPOs—showing that b enhances QPO frequencies and current QPO data cannot constrain b.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how quantum-gravity-inspired regularization (via the Simpson-Visser metric) and asymptotic AdS curvature jointly influence geodesic dynamics and observational signatures—particularly QPOs—in charged black hole and wormhole spacetimes.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of the charged Simpson-Visser-AdS metric: classification of horizon structure via metric function roots; derivation of effective potential for timelike geodesics; computation of ISCO, energy, and angular momentum; calculation of Keplerian, radial, and vertical epicyclic frequencies for QPO modeling; comparison with observed twin-peaked HF-QPOs from black hole binaries.

Result: Three horizon topologies emerge (non-extremal BH, extremal BH, horizonless wormhole); b and l strongly shift ISCO inward and increase all epicyclic frequencies; b systematically elevates fundamental QPO frequencies above GR predictions; QPO data rule out Schwarzschild at ~4σ but only constrain a degenerate Q–l combination—b remains unconstrained.

Conclusion: The regularization parameter b introduces a distinct, observationally relevant deviation from classical black hole geometries in QPO spectra, yet current HF-QPO observations lack sensitivity to isolate b; future high-precision timing missions or multi-frequency QPO modeling may break the Q–l degeneracy and probe b.

Abstract: In this article, we examine particle dynamics and Quasi-periodic oscillations within a charged Simpson-Visser-Anti-de Sitter (AdS) spacetime. We first analyze the behavior of the metric and black hole horizons under the competing impact of electric charge $Q$, regularization parameter $b$, and AdS radius $l$. The roots of the metric function reveal transitions between three distinct topological classes: non-extremal configurations featuring distinct inner and outer horizons, extremal limits with a single degenerate horizon, and strictly positive, horizonless wormhole geometries. By evaluating the effective potential, we determine how this modified geometry shifts the Innermost Stable Circular Orbit (ISCO) and alters the angular momentum and energy of test particles. Moreover, we derive the Keplerian, radial, and vertical frequencies to model quasi-periodic oscillations (QPOs). We show that the interplay between the regularization parameter $b$ and the AdS radius $l$ significantly modifies twin-peak QPOs. In particular, $b$ drives fundamental QPO frequencies higher than those predicted by standard general relativistic backgrounds. Twin-peak high-frequency QPO data from two black-hole binaries disfavor the Schwarzschild limit at $\sim\!4σ$, but constrain only a degenerate combination of charge and AdS curvature, leaving the regularization parameter entirely unconstrained by the data.

</details>


### [133] [Dynamical dark energy from quantum gravity condensates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34917)
*Daniele Granata,Daniele Oriti*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends group field theory (GFT) condensate cosmology to model dynamical dark energy from quantum gravity, incorporating multiple condensate modes, phase dynamics, and an extra scalar matter field; it shows asymptotic de Sitter behavior with transient phantom-like evolution and phantom-divide crossing enabled by matter–condensate coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize quantum-gravity-induced cosmic acceleration beyond minimal GFT condensate models, addressing robustness, physical completeness (e.g., inclusion of matter fields), and observational relevance of the resulting dark-energy dynamics.

Method: Uses mean-field approximation with coherent peaked states in Hermitian GFT; derives effective cosmological equations including relational clock, additional scalar matter field, condensate phase dynamics, and contributions from multiple condensate modes.

Result: The system asymptotically approaches de Sitter space dominated by a single condensate mode; transient dynamics exhibit time-varying equation of state w(a), including phantom behavior (w < −1) and phantom-divide crossing (w = −1), conditioned on scalar matter field parameters.

Conclusion: Dynamical dark energy with rich phenomenology—including phantom evolution and divide crossing—naturally arises in GFT condensate cosmology when extending to realistic settings with matter and multi-mode phase dynamics, strengthening its viability as a quantum-gravity explanation for late-time acceleration.

Abstract: We investigate dynamical dark energy emerging from quantum gravity within the framework of group field theory (GFT) condensate cosmology. We generalise previous models of quantum gravity induced cosmic acceleration by considering a broader parameter space, including an additional scalar matter field besides the relational clock, and accounting simultaneously for the phase dynamics and the contribution of multiple condensate modes. Working in a mean-field approximation with coherent peaked states and restricting to Hermitian GFT interactions, we derive the corresponding effective cosmological dynamics and analyse its late-time behaviour. We show that the system approaches an asymptotic de Sitter regime, dynamically dominated by a single condensate mode, while the approach to this regime can exhibit a non-trivial dynamical dark-energy evolution. In particular, both the condensate phase and subdominant modes can generate deviations from the asymptotic equation of state $w=-1$, including phantom behaviour and, under suitable conditions, a crossing of the phantom divide. The additional scalar matter field enters directly into these conditions, providing a link between the matter content and the quantum gravity induced dark-energy dynamics. These results extend the robustness and generality of the GFT mechanism for dynamical dark energy and provide a basis for confronting the underlying quantum gravity dynamics with cosmological observations.

</details>


### [134] [Horizon, Null-Orbit, and Photon-Sphere Phase Structures in Logarithmic Nonlinear Electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34957)
*Peshwaz Abdulkareem Abdoul*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes static, charged, asymptotically flat black holes in logarithmic nonlinear electrodynamics (LNED), identifying distinct phase structures in the (Q, ε) parameter space—horizon geometry, background circular null orbits, and effective optical photon orbits—unified by a zero-radius boundary Q_nc but differing in critical curves (extremal, merger, photon-merger). It reveals 'black points' at Q → Q_nc for ε ≥ ε_b: horizonless, zero-temperature (at bifurcation) or divergent-temperature objects with quantum-corrected entropy S_η → η; background potential becomes an infinite repulsive barrier, while photon potential remains regular with a finite-radius photon sphere. For ε < ε_b, both geometries admit horizonless configurations above Q_ext; for ε ≥ ε_b, only the optical geometry does so in Q_nc < Q < Q_m^ph. Geodesic behavior distinguishes radial (singularity/escape) from non-radial (blocked, metastable) motion, with critical impact parameter encoding dual gravitational–optical phase signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how nonlinear electrodynamics—specifically logarithmic NED (LNED)—alters black hole phase structure, critical phenomena, and singularity resolution, particularly through interplay between spacetime geometry, background null geodesics, and effective optical geometry, and to explore emergent horizonless objects ('black points') and their thermodynamic and geodesic properties.

Method: Analytical and qualitative analysis of static, spherically symmetric LNED black hole solutions in the (Q, ε) parameter space; identification and comparison of three critical boundaries—zero-radius horizon (Q_nc), extremal curve (Q_ext), and merger curves (Q_m, Q_m^ph); computation of thermodynamic quantities (temperature, entropy), effective potentials (V_eff^bg, V_eff^ph), and geodesic behavior (radial vs. non-radial, impact parameter dependence); bifurcation analysis near the critical point (Q_b, ε_b).

Result: Three distinct but related phase structures share Q_nc(ε) = γ M^{2/3} ε^{1/6} as a common zero-radius boundary, yet possess different critical curves terminating at a bifurcation point (Q_b, ε_b); for ε ≥ ε_b, approaching Q_nc yields 'black points' with vanishing or divergent temperature (except when ηδ = 1), quantum-corrected entropy S_η → η, infinite background potential barrier at r = 0, and a finite-radius photon sphere due to regularized photon potential; horizonless configurations exist only in the optical geometry for ε ≥ ε_b and Q_nc < Q < Q_m^ph; non-radial geodesics are repelled from r = 0 and support metastable bound states, whereas radial ones either terminate at r = 0 or escape; the critical impact parameter reflects both gravitational and optical phase transitions.

Conclusion: LNED induces rich phase structure in charged black holes, with bifurcation-driven emergence of horizonless 'black points' exhibiting novel thermodynamics and optical behavior; the decoupling of background and optical critical curves signals fundamental separation between causal structure and light propagation; geodesic confinement and photon-sphere persistence suggest observational signatures distinct from classical black holes or naked singularities.

Abstract: We investigate static, charged, asymptotically flat black holes in LNED in the $(Q,ε)$ parameter space. We distinguish three phase structures: the horizon geometry, the background circular null orbits, and the circular photon orbits of the effective optical geometry. All share a zero-radius boundary $Q_{\rm nc}(ε;M)=γM^{2/3}ε^{1/6}$, but have distinct critical structures: the extremal curve $Q_{\rm ext}(ε;M)$ and the merger curve $Q_{\rm m}(ε;M)$ both terminate at the bifurcation point $(Q_{\rm b},ε_{\rm b})$, with $Q_{\rm b}=9M/[2Γ^2(1/4)]$ and $ε_{\rm b}=128π^2Q_{\rm b}^2$, whereas the effective photon-orbit merger curve $Q_{\rm m}^{\rm ph}(ε;M)$ does not pass through this point.
  For $ε\geε_{\rm b}$, approaching $Q_{\rm nc}$ shrinks the horizon to zero and produces black points with quantum-corrected entropy $S_η(r_{\rm h}\to0)=η$. Their temperature vanishes at the bifurcation point and diverges for $ε>ε_{\rm b}$, except at $ηδ=1$. The background potential $V_{\rm eff}^{\rm bg}(r)$ develops an infinite barrier at $r=0$ without local extrema, acting as a perfect repeller. The photon potential $V_{\rm eff}^{\rm ph}(r)$ is regularized with a maximum at finite radius, acting as a partial reflector. Thus the black points retain a photon sphere at finite radius.
  Away from $Q_{\rm nc}$, for $ε<ε_{\rm b}$ both geometries support horizonless structures above $Q_{\rm ext}$; for $ε\geε_{\rm b}$ only the optical geometry does so in $Q_{\rm nc}<Q<Q_{\rm m}^{\rm ph}$. Non-radial geodesics are blocked from the singularity and can form metastable bound states, while radial geodesics either reach $r=0$ or escape to infinity. The critical impact parameter encodes both gravitational and optical-geometry effects, probing the distinct phase structures.

</details>


### [135] [A Cuscuton Representation of the Loop Quantum Cosmology Bounce](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35222)
*Niayesh Afshordi,Kristina Giesel*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a local, generally covariant effective field theory—based on the cuscuton model—that exactly reproduces the standard flat-FLRW Loop Quantum Cosmology (LQC) background dynamics, including the bounce, without extra dynamical degrees of freedom. It identifies the LQC polymerization angle 2λb with an angular coordinate θ in the cuscuton scalar constraint, yielding H ∝ sin θ and ρ = ρ_c sin²(θ/2), matching the modified Friedmann equation. A branchwise Legendre transform links it to a homogeneous mimetic system, and the action admits an f(K) form in terms of extrinsic curvature K, providing a covariant effective representation—not a derivation—of LQC holonomy corrections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a local, generally covariant, closed-form effective theory that exactly reproduces the standard flat-FLRW LQC background dynamics (especially the bounce) while avoiding unphysical degrees of freedom or ad hoc non-locality, and to clarify its relation to mimetic gravity and f(K) theories.
