Method: Constructs a cuscuton scalar field theory where the scalar is parameterized by an angular coordinate θ identified with the LQC polymerization angle 2λb; imposes the cuscuton constraint to derive H ∝ sin θ and combines with the Einstein constraint to recover the LQC Friedmann equation; performs a branchwise Legendre transformation to establish canonical equivalence with a clock-gauge-fixed homogeneous mimetic system (at zero mimetic dust energy density); reformulates the action as a branched f(K) function of the mean extrinsic curvature K.

Result: Exact reproduction of the standard LQC modified Friedmann equation H² = ρ(1 − ρ/ρ_c)/(3Mₚ²) from a local, generally covariant, constraint-based (non-dynamical) cuscuton scalar; canonical equivalence with a specific mimetic setup; explicit f(K) representation; no new local propagating degrees of freedom introduced.

Conclusion: The LQC flat-FLRW background—including the quantum bounce—is effectively captured by a local cuscuton theory, offering a covariant, low-energy effective description of LQC holonomy corrections. This is not a derivation from full loop quantum gravity but a consistent, minimal, and physically viable effective framework compatible with the null energy condition.

Abstract: Loop Quantum Cosmology (LQC) replaces the big bang singularity of the homogeneous universe by a bounce, usually described by the modified Friedmann equation $H^2=ρ(1-ρ/ρ_c)/(3M_p^2)$. We show that this background dynamics follows from a local cuscuton effective theory, whose scalar equation is a constraint rather than a wave equation. One way to establish this is to write the cuscuton in terms of an angular coordinate $θ$, identified with the LQC polymerization angle $2λb$. Its constraint gives $H\propto\sinθ$, while the Einstein constraint gives $ρ=ρ_c\sin^2(θ/2)$, exactly reproducing the LQC bounce. To our knowledge, this is the first closed-form, local, generally covariant realization of the exact standard flat-FLRW LQC background dynamics for minimally coupled matter satisfying null energy condition, without introducing additional local dynamical degrees of freedom. A branchwise Legendre transformation establishes a canonical equivalence between the clock-gauge-fixed homogeneous mimetic system in the case of vanishing mimetic dust energy density and the cuscuton systems. It selects the constant-tension cuscuton coordinate $Θ=\sinθ-θ$, while $θ$ retains its interpretation as the LQC polymerization angle. This equivalence does not establish agreement of the inhomogeneous theories or their perturbations. The angular action also admits an explicit branched $f(K)$ representation, where $K$ is the mean extrinsic curvature of spatial hypersurfaces. The construction is therefore an effective covariant representation of the LQC holonomy correction, not a derivation from full loop quantum gravity.

</details>


### [136] [Kasner critical scaling in Einstein--Maxwell--scalar black holes with nonlinear collapse and scalarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35500)
*Chihang He,Chao Liu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper refutes the universality of the 1/2 critical scaling exponent for the Kasner parameter in asymptotically flat scalarized black holes, proving instead a tunable family of exponents 1/(2m) via higher-order scalar couplings; it provides a rigorous construction, derives the full scaling law including prefactor, and establishes a hierarchy (1/2, 1/4, 1/6, ...).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the conjectured universality of the 1/2 critical scaling exponent for the Kasner parameter β_K in scalarized black holes within Einstein–Maxwell–scalar theory.

Method: Develops a rigorous mathematical framework for static, spherically symmetric solutions; constructs non-quadratic scalar couplings parametrized by integer m ≥ 1 at fixed quadratic coupling μ; analyzes nonlinear interior collapse to a spacelike Kasner singularity; matches interior Kasner behavior (β_K ∝ ε⁻¹) with exterior near-critical charge scaling (q/q_⋆ − 1 ∝ ε²ᵐ) to derive β_K ∝ |q/q_⋆ − 1|⁻¹/(²ᵐ); computes explicit prefactor.

Result: Demonstrates non-universality of the 1/2 exponent; constructs explicit couplings yielding critical exponents 1/(2m) for all integers m ≥ 1; derives exact scaling law β_K = C_m |q/q_⋆ − 1|⁻¹/(²ᵐ) with analytically determined prefactor C_m; recovers known 1/2 case and extends to 1/4, 1/6, etc.

Conclusion: The critical exponent for the Kasner parameter in asymptotically flat scalarized black holes is not universal but controllably tunable via higher-order scalar self-interactions; universality fails beyond the quadratic coupling limit, and a discrete hierarchy of scaling exponents emerges.

Abstract: A critical scaling exponent of $1/2$ was numerically observed for the Kasner parameter of asymptotically flat scalarized black holes and conjectured to be universal [Phys.\ Rev.\ Lett.\ \textbf{136}, 251402 (2026)]. In the same static, spherically symmetric Einstein--Maxwell--scalar setting, we show that this exponent is not universal. We develop a rigorous mathematical framework and construct couplings realizing exponents $1/(2m)$ for any integer $m\ge1$ by tuning higher-order terms at fixed quadratic coupling parameter $μ$. Nonlinear interior collapse produces a spacelike Kasner singularity with $β_{\rm K}\propto\varepsilon^{-1}$, where $\varepsilon$ is the value of scalar field at the event horizon. An exterior branch satisfying $q/q_\star-1\propto\varepsilon^{2m}$, therefore, yields $β_{\rm K}\propto|q/q_\star-1|^{-1/(2m)}$, where $q$ is the charge-to-mass ratio and $q_\star$ its critical value. We also determine the prefactor explicitly. This recovers the $1/2$ law and establishes a hierarchy including $1/4$ and $1/6$.

</details>


### [137] [From Density to Mass: A New Parametrized Framework for Dark Matter Environment Around Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35771)
*Rajes Ghosh,Nicholas Speeney,Avijit Chowdhury,Chiranjeeb Singha,Miguel A. S. Pinto,Emanuele Berti*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a mass-based framework to model black holes embedded in dark matter halos, enabling systematic study of environmental effects on gravitational-wave observables—quasinormal modes (inner-halo sensitive), tidal Love numbers (outer-halo sensitive), and EMRI fluxes (orbital + propagation effects).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Astrophysical black holes reside in complex environments (e.g., DM halos, baryonic matter), which modify spacetime geometry and leave imprints on GW signals; existing density-based halo models lack direct connection to observable gravitational fields and physical consistency constraints.

Method: Develops a general spacetime framework parameterized by the enclosed mass function M(r); enforces regularity, causality, and asymptotic flatness; computes QNMs, static TLNs, and EMRI gravitational-wave fluxes within this geometry.

Result: QNMs are most sensitive to the inner halo (r ≲ 10M), static TLNs to outer regions (r ≳ 100M), and EMRI fluxes encode combined effects of orbital dynamics and GW propagation through the environment; common halo profiles (e.g., NFW, cored) emerge as limiting cases.

Conclusion: The enclosed-mass formulation provides a physically grounded, flexible, and observationally relevant framework for linking astrophysical environment models to precision GW measurements—enabling future tests of BH environments with LISA and next-generation detectors.

Abstract: Astrophysical black holes (BHs) are not isolated vacuum objects but are expected to reside within complex environments, such as dark matter (DM) halos and baryonic distributions. Such environments can modify the spacetime geometry surrounding the BH and leave observable imprints on gravitational-wave (GW) signals, including shifts in the quasinormal mode (QNM) spectrum, tidal Love numbers (TLNs), and the inspiral dynamics of extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs). We develop a general framework for describing DM-dressed BH spacetimes by parameterizing the geometry directly in terms of the enclosed mass function, rather than the traditional density-based profile. This mass-based formulation is naturally connected to the total gravitational field, accommodates several common halo profiles as limiting cases, and incorporates basic physical consistency requirements such as regularity, causality, and appropriate asymptotic behavior. Within this framework, we investigate the environmental imprints on QNMs, TLNs, and GW fluxes from EMRIs. We find a complementary sensitivity to the halo structure: QNMs predominantly probe the inner regions of the environment, whereas static TLNs are more sensitive to its outer structure. EMRI fluxes, in contrast, probe the matter distribution through its influence on the orbital motion and GW propagation. Our results establish the enclosed-mass formulation as a flexible and physically controlled framework for connecting astrophysical environment models with precision GW observables.

</details>


### [138] [Analytic detector responses and entanglement in constant-curvature spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29992)
*Dyuman Bhattacharya,Jiayue Yang,Ming Zhang,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives analytic response functions for Unruh–DeWitt detectors coupled to massless, conformally coupled scalar fields across multiple spacetimes (Minkowski, AdS, dS, BTZ), isolating geometric and spectral effects from interaction protocol. It computes excitation probabilities and coherences for Gaussian- and sharply-switched detectors, identifies KMS-constrained optimal gaps for entanglement in de Sitter, characterizes logarithmic singularity behavior in BTZ, and validates results numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle the contributions of quantum field spectrum, global spacetime geometry, and detector interaction protocol (e.g., switching function) to detector responses—particularly for entanglement harvesting and coherence generation—in curved and flat spacetimes.

Method: Analytic derivation of Wightman-function-based response functions for Unruh–DeWitt detectors; explicit computation of leading-order excitation probabilities and two-detector coherences using Gaussian and sharp switching; application of KMS condition in de Sitter; Legendre series integration and image-sum techniques for BTZ; numerical verification of convergence and physical predictions.

Result: 1) Time-ordered coherence requires spatially separated worldlines; local response and single-excitation coherence allow coincidence. 2) In de Sitter, KMS detailed balance fixes the gap maximizing nonlocal-to-local coherence ratio—enabling a single-gap entanglement test and bounding entangling gaps. 3) Concurrence peaks at a smaller positive gap than the coherence-ratio optimum. 4) In 3D BTZ, infalling detector response exhibits logarithmic divergence near singularity with coefficient determined by covering-AdS response; smooth switching removes onset artifacts but preserves leading-log coefficient. 5) All analytic results confirmed numerically.

Conclusion: Detector response functions are highly sensitive to global geometry via the field’s Wightman function, and analytic control over switching protocols (especially Gaussian) enables clean separation of geometric, spectral, and protocol effects—providing rigorous tools for entanglement harvesting diagnostics, singularity probing, and testing quantum field theory in curved spacetime.

Abstract: We derive analytic response functions for Unruh--DeWitt detectors coupled to real, massless, conformally coupled scalar fields, to distinguish the effects of the field spectrum and global geometry from those of the interaction protocol. For Gaussian-switched static pairs in Minkowski, anti-de Sitter, and de Sitter spacetimes, we obtain the leading-order excitation probabilities and two-detector coherences in spacetime dimension $\mathcal D=d+1\ge3$. Time-ordered coherence requires distinct spatial worldlines; the local response and single-excitation coherence also admit coincidence. For equal-redshift pairs, Gaussian switching separates the gap dependence of the time-ordered coherence. In de Sitter space, KMS detailed balance fixes the gap that maximizes the ratio of nonlocal coherence to local excitation, giving a single-gap test for the existence of leading-order entanglement and restricting any entangling gaps to one bounded interval. When entanglement is present, the concurrence itself peaks at a smaller positive gap. In the three-dimensional BTZ black hole, an integrated Legendre series gives the sharply switched response of a radially infalling detector. A uniform image-sum estimate establishes logarithmic growth near the singularity and determines its coefficient from the covering-AdS response. A sufficiently smooth monotone onset removes the finite-time onset glitches but leaves a positive logarithmic coefficient; the accumulated leading-order response remains finite. Independent numerical calculations test the analytic expressions, their convergence properties, and these physical distinctions.

</details>
